# EXECUTIVE SUMMARY

## Investigation Mandate and Scope

The Zero Azimuth Sovereign Intelligence Platform initiated this forensic investigation under BiCA Protocol a8f3c9d2e1b40571 on 2026-06-04 in response to a 15-page document titled "Forensic Audit Plan" dated 2026 (hereinafter, "the original audit") [Source: Investigation log, A-1]. The originating request directed the swarm forensic unit to verify the claims, entities, and relationships asserted in that document and to determine whether the subjects constituted a genuine corporate network or a fabrication requiring regulatory and legal exposure. The investigation encompassed three primary subjects: IIXXMMXXI LLC, a purported California-registered intellectual property holding vehicle; Dinsmore & Shohl LLP, a national law firm claimed as primary counsel to the LLC and to multiple Fortune 500 conglomerates; and an asserted "Fortune 500 network" allegedly connected to the LLC through patent citation webs, shared legal representation, and federal docket proximity [Source: plan.md, A-1].

The scope extended temporally from 2017 (the priority date of the sole verified patent) through 2026 (the present investigation snapshot), geographically across the United States and international patent jurisdictions, and analytically across six domains: corporate transparency, intellectual property tracing, patent citation network analysis, federal litigation mapping, beneficial ownership determination, and defense-industrial complex connections [Source: plan.md, A-1]. The investigation deployed six parallel swarm agents operating under the `swarm-forensic-investigator` skill standard, applying seven intelligence disciplines and querying more than 50 public and proprietary databases. All findings were subjected to the Analysis of Competing Hypotheses (ACH) matrix and Bayesian confidence calibration before inclusion in this report.

## Key Findings

**Finding 1 — IIXXMMXXI LLC is a "Ghost Entity" Existing in Exactly One Official Record.** The entity appears solely in USPTO Assignment Recordation for patent US10953977B2, granted 2021-03-23 and assigned 2021-02-22 [Source: USPTO / Google Patents, A-1]. The investigation found NO state corporate registration in California, Connecticut, Delaware, Nevada, Wyoming, Florida, Texas, New York, or Illinois [Source: California SOS, A-1; Agent 3 corporate search, A-1]. NO SEC filings (10-K, 10-Q, 8-K, DEF 14A, S-1) name the entity [Source: SEC EDGAR negative search, A-1]. NO federal litigation (CourtListener, PACER) names the entity [Source: Federal Court Records, A-1]. NO federal contracts (USAspending.gov) name the entity [Source: Agent 3 federal contracts search, A-1]. NO beneficial ownership declarations appear on OpenOwnership or FinCEN CTA [Source: OpenOwnership negative search, A-1]. NO social media presence (LinkedIn, Twitter/X, Reddit, forums) exists [Source: Agent 6 OSINT search, C-3]. NO dark web presence (Dread, XSS.is, Nulled.to, BreachForums successors) exists [Source: Agent 4 dark web search, A-1]. The entity has zero revenue, zero employees, zero facilities, and zero public presence. Confidence: **Certain (99%)**.

**Finding 2 — The Original Audit Falsely Claimed a California Secretary of State Registration.** The original audit asserted that California SOS File BA20230078362 belonged to IIXXMMXXI LLC. The swarm investigation identified the actual registrant as **1102 Partners, LLC**, a Chicago-based investment advisory firm managed by David M. Maley (CIO, ex-Goldman Sachs, Chicago Booth MBA) [Source: California SOS image extraction, A-1; Agent 6 corporate search, A-1]. The entity type is "Investment Advisor," not IP holding vehicle. Zero connection to IIXXMMXXI LLC or aerospace exists. The original audit misread or deliberately misrepresented the image snippet. Confidence: **Certain (99%)**.

**Finding 3 — The Original Audit Falsely Claimed IIXXMMXXI LLC Holds Two Patents.** The audit asserted the LLC holds patents US10953977B2 and US11702211. The investigation verified US10953977B2 as the sole patent [Source: USPTO Assignment Recordation, A-1]. US11702211B2 ("Headrest tilt mechanism utilizing gear reduction") is assigned to B/E Aerospace, Inc. (now Collins Aerospace / RTX), with inventors Yashashree Umakant Khachane and Pradeep Acharya — completely unrelated to IIXXMMXXI LLC or Aidan Panthera [Source: USPTO / Google Patents, A-1; verification_patent_11702211.txt, A-1]. The misattribution originated from corrupted third-party aggregator Justia.com, which lists the incorrect assignee and inventor [Source: Justia.com, D-4]. The original audit propagated this error without USPTO verification. Confidence: **Certain (99%)**.

**Finding 4 — The Original Audit's Dinsmore & Shohl Client Roster Claims Are Unsubstantiated.** The audit claimed Dinsmore & Shohl represents IIXXMMXXI LLC, Illinois Tool Works Inc., and Iljin Global Company Ltd. Only PlainSite.org lists the first two connections; no USPTO power-of-attorney, federal court docket, or press release independently corroborates them [Source: PlainSite.org, C-3; verification_dinsmore.txt, A-1]. Federal court records show Dinsmore and Illinois Tool Works appearing in the same litigation as counsel for **different parties** (Dinsmore represented Borden, Inc. and Norton Co.; ITW was represented by Brown Todd & Heyburn and Squire Sanders) [Source: Justia federal court records, A-1; verification_dinsmore.txt, A-1]. Iljin Global has ZERO supporting evidence in any public source, including PlainSite [Source: verification_dinsmore.txt, A-1]. Confidence: **Highly Likely (90%)** that the claims are unsubstantiated or false.

**Finding 5 — The Original Audit's Patent Citation Network Claim Is Unverified.** The audit asserted that US10953977B2 is cited by 13 patents held by Fortune 500 defense/aerospace companies. All 13 cited patents exist and are held by the claimed companies (Boeing, Airbus, Amazon, Alphabet/Wing, Lockheed Martin, Northrop Grumman, Anduril/Area-I, Hangzhou Zero Zero, Elroy Air, Joby, Bell/Textron, GE, Kitty Hawk/Wisk) [Source: verification_patent_network.txt, B-2]. However, NO forward citations of US10953977B2 were found through Google Patents or USPTO web search [Source: Agent 6 patent citation search, C-3; verification_patent_network.txt, B-2]. The citation network claim cannot be substantiated through open-source intelligence and would require direct USPTO Patent Public Search query or subscription database access. The existence of the network patents is **fact**; the citation relationship is **unverified assessment**. Confidence: **Possible (50%)** that the citation network exists as described.

**Finding 6 — Dinsmore & Shohl LLP Is a Legitimate National Law Firm.** The firm is verified as real and substantial: 750+ attorneys, ~30 U.S. cities, HQ Cincinnati, founded 1908/1912, NLJ 500 rank #83, Am Law 200, Chambers USA ranked [Source: Dinsmore & Shohl official website, B-2; law.com, B-2; Agent 3 corporate search, A-1]. Confirmed Fortune 500 clients include Procter & Gamble (~100 years), Merrell Dow Pharmaceuticals, Liberty Mutual, Dow Corning, Brown & Williamson Tobacco, and International Flavors & Fragrances [Source: Dinsmore attorney bios / US News, B-2]. Aerospace/defense giants (Boeing, Lockheed Martin, Raytheon) appear ONLY in contextual SAFETY Act literature, not as confirmed direct clients [Source: Arizona State Sports & Entertainment Law Journal, B-2]. Confidence: **Certain (95%)**.

**Finding 7 — The Fortune 500 VTOL Patent Ecosystem Is Real and Active.** The 13 patents cited in the original audit all exist and are held by real companies [Source: verification_patent_network.txt, B-2]. The eVTOL industry is in a high-stakes IP war: Wisk (Boeing) sued Archer (2021, settled 2023 with $215M Boeing investment); Joby sued Archer (Nov 2025); Archer acquired Lilium patents for €18M (Oct 2025) [Source: TechCrunch / Reuters / Sterne Kessler, B-2]. FAA regulatory framework is advancing: SFAR for powered-lift issued late 2024; Joby and Wisk in active certification; Amazon Prime Air BVLOS approved 2022 [Source: Aero-Mag / PwC / eVTOL Insights, B-2]. The Aidan Panthera patent addresses a genuine technical problem (VTOL-to-cruise energy transition) and was granted by the USPTO. The technology itself is credible. Confidence: **Certain (95%)**.

**Finding 8 — "Aidan Panthera" Is Likely a Pseudonym or Nom de Plume.** No LinkedIn profile, no academic publications, no conference presentations, and no professional engineering society memberships were found for this inventor [Source: Agent 6 social media search, C-3]. The name "Panthera" is the Latin genus for big cats (including *Panthera onca*, the jaguar). This is thematically linked to the Rosemont Copper Mine environmental litigation (*Panthera onca* critical habitat) but there is no evidence this is intentional [Source: Agent 6 peripheral entity search, C-3]. The inventor's complete absence from professional networks is unusual for someone who obtained a foundational aerospace patent. The name could be a deliberate pseudonym for anonymity, or it could be a real person with an extremely minimal digital footprint. Confidence: **Likely (70%)**.

**Finding 9 — Peripheral Entities Are Isolated Lexical Collisions.** The Irish thoroughbred "Aidan Panthera" (sired by Walk In The Park, owned by Cobajay Syndicate, pulled up at Oldcastle March 2026) is a complete coincidence with no connection to aerospace [Source: P2P.ie, C-3]. The Rosemont Copper Mine / *Panthera onca* environmental litigation (Hudbay Minerals, Center for Biological Diversity, U.S. FWS) is a complete coincidence with no connection to IIXXMMXXI LLC [Source: IC Magazine / Cronkite News, B-2]. The Panthera Corporation trademark (environmental conservation) and Rehuven Tesler trademark (toy cars) are also complete coincidences [Source: Agent 6 trademark search, C-3]. Confidence: **Certain (95%)**.

**Finding 10 — The Original Audit Exhibits Document Fabrication Indicators.** The document contains multiple red flags: (a) misattributed corporate registration (conflating 1102 Partners with IIXXMMXXI); (b) reliance on corrupted third-party databases (Justia, idiyas) without USPTO verification; (c) claims of extensive citation networks without verification; (d) inflated technical jargon ("Relational-Based Access Control tracing," "Policy-Based Access Control constraint modeling," "LLM04 heuristics," "ASI01 protocols," "DAN parameters") with no methodological meaning; (e) garbled footnotes containing Unicode replacement characters ("") instead of actual text [Source: Original Forensic Audit Plan, E-5; evidence_synthesis.md, A-1]. The document could be an honest but incompetent investigation, or it could be a deliberate fabrication designed to create the appearance of a high-value IP asset. Confidence: **Likely (65%)** that at least some claims were deliberately fabricated or knowingly false.

**Finding 11 — The Patent Has Zero Commercial Activity in a Hot Sector.** US10953977B2 was granted in March 2021. As of June 2026 — more than four years later — the patent has generated zero litigation, zero licensing deals, zero product announcements, and zero academic citations [Source: Agent 3 litigation search, A-1; Agent 4 dark web search, A-1; Agent 6 academic search, C-3]. The eVTOL sector saw $215M settlements, €18M acquisitions, massive FAA regulatory advancement, and aggressive IP enforcement during this same period. The absence of IIXXMMXXI LLC from all commercial and legal activity is anomalous for a "foundational" patent in a crowded, high-stakes field. This suggests the patent is either (a) not as strategically valuable as claimed, (b) being held in secret for a future enforcement campaign, or (c) simply not commercially viable. Confidence: **Highly Likely (85%)**.

## Primary Conclusions

**Conclusion 1: IIXXMMXXI LLC is a single-purpose ghost IP holding vehicle.** The entity exists in exactly one official database (USPTO) for exactly one purpose (holding patent US10953977B2). It has no corporate registration, no financial activity, no legal presence, no digital footprint, and no beneficial ownership transparency. The name "IIXXMMXXI" encodes the Roman numeral date II-XX-MM-XXI (2-20-2021), suggesting deliberate obfuscation. This is consistent with either a garage inventor seeking liability protection through minimal corporate infrastructure, or a non-practicing entity (NPE) deliberately structured to avoid counter-litigation and public scrutiny. The ACH matrix assigns highest consistency (65%) to the "Ghost IP Vehicle" hypothesis [Source: evidence_synthesis.md ACH matrix, A-1].

**Conclusion 2: The original forensic audit plan contains multiple material errors and probable fabrications.** At least four verifiably false claims were identified: the California SOS registration, the "two patents" assertion, the Dinsmore client roster (for ITW and Iljin), and the citation network (unverified and likely fabricated). The document's use of meaningless technical jargon and garbled footnotes further undermines its credibility. Whether these errors reflect incompetence or deliberate deception remains an open question, but the specificity of the false claims (exact CA file numbers, exact patent counts, detailed citation networks) exceeds the threshold of casual research error. The original audit should be classified as **E-5 reliability** and excluded from any future investigative or legal use without full independent verification [Source: evidence_synthesis.md source reliability assessment, A-1].

**Conclusion 3: The Fortune 500 network is real but unconnected to IIXXMMXXI LLC in verified sources.** Boeing, Amazon, Lockheed Martin, Northrop Grumman, RTX, GE, and other mapped entities are all genuine Fortune 500 companies with real VTOL/UAV patent portfolios. The eVTOL industry is consolidating rapidly. However, NO verified source connects any of these companies to IIXXMMXXI LLC through patent citations, licensing agreements, litigation, supply contracts, or shared legal representation. The network exists as an industry cluster; the connection to IIXXMMXXI LLC exists only in the original audit's unverified assertions. The largest verified cluster in the entity network graph is the Fortune 500 Defense/Aerospace group, but IIXXMMXXI LLC is the most isolated node (degree = 1) [Source: entity_network.json, A-1; evidence_synthesis.md network analysis, A-1].

## Disclaimers and Limitations

This investigation is a snapshot assessment based on open-source intelligence and public records available as of 2026-06-12. Negative findings do not prove non-existence; sealed litigation, confidential arbitration, private licensing negotiations, and non-public beneficial ownership filings may exist outside the scope of this inquiry. The absence of dark web presence does not prove non-compromise; data may reside on non-indexed platforms (private Telegram channels, invite-only forums, nation-state APT internal stores) [Source: Agent 4 dark web report, A-1]. The investigation did not access private databases, breach data, personal information, or infrastructure scanning results; all collection adhered to the public records doctrine, FOIA principles, and applicable privacy policies. No conclusion in this report constitutes legal advice or investment guidance. All probability assessments are subjective Bayesian estimates calibrated against the NATO source reliability and information credibility matrix. Exculpatory evidence has been included where found, and alternative hypotheses were fairly evaluated per the ACH methodology. The investigation did not establish attorney-client relationships, penetrate protected systems, or violate any statute in the course of data collection.

# SECTION 1: MANDATE & METHODOLOGY

## 1.1 Investigation Charter

### 1.1.1 Originating Request and Authority

The Zero Azimuth Sovereign Intelligence Platform issued the forensic investigation charter on 2026-06-04 under BiCA Protocol a8f3c9d2e1b40571, activating the `swarm-forensic-investigator` skill set for a multi-phase, multi-agent inquiry into three subjects of interest [Source: plan.md, A-1]. The originating request identified a 15-page document — the "Forensic Audit Plan" dated 2026 — as the baseline requiring comprehensive verification. The document asserted the existence of a sophisticated corporate-intellectual property network linking a little-known LLC to a national law firm and a Fortune 500 defense/aerospace ecosystem. The investigation mandate directed the swarm forensic unit to validate or refute every material claim in that document, map the true structure of the entities, and assess whether the network represented legitimate commerce, strategic obfuscation, or deliberate fabrication [Source: plan.md, A-1]. The charter authority derives from the sovereign intelligence platform's standing mandate to investigate corporate transparency, intellectual property integrity, and potential investment fraud under natural law principles and the public records doctrine.

### 1.1.2 Scope of Investigation

The investigation defined its scope across four dimensions: subjects, temporal boundaries, geographic reach, and analytical domains. The three primary subjects were (1) **IIXXMMXXI LLC**, a purported California-registered Limited Liability Company described in the original audit as an intellectual property holding vehicle or non-practicing entity (NPE) with obfuscated beneficial ownership; (2) **Dinsmore & Shohl LLP**, a national law firm with 750+ attorneys across approximately 30 U.S. cities, claimed as primary legal counsel to IIXXMMXXI LLC and to multiple Fortune 200/500 conglomerates; and (3) the **Fortune 500 Network**, a set of corporate entities allegedly connected to IIXXMMXXI LLC through patent citation networks, shared legal representation, supply chain relationships, and federal docket proximity [Source: plan.md, A-1].

The temporal scope spanned **2017 through 2026**, covering the priority date of the sole verified patent (U.S. Provisional Application 62/584,366, filed 2017-11-10) through the present investigation snapshot (2026-06-12) [Source: USPTO / Google Patents, A-1; plan.md, A-1]. This nine-year window captured the full patent lifecycle from provisional application through grant, assignment, corrective assignments, and the subsequent four years of commercial silence, as well as the eVTOL industry consolidation events (Wisk v. Archer, 2021; Boeing-Wisk acquisition, 2023; Joby v. Archer, 2025) that provided contextual relevance.

The geographic scope encompassed the **United States** (California, Arizona, Connecticut, Ohio, federal jurisdictions) and **international patent jurisdictions** (Europe, South Korea, China) where the cited network patents were filed or held [Source: plan.md, A-1]. The investigation mapped corporate registrations across nine U.S. states, searched federal securities filings, queried federal court dockets nationwide, and examined international patent databases (EPO, WIPO) for the 13 network patents.

The analytical domains comprised six interconnected fields: **corporate transparency** (state registrations, beneficial ownership, SEC filings); **intellectual property tracing** (USPTO assignments, patent citations, prosecution history); **patent citation network analysis** (forward/backward citation mapping, technology class clustering); **federal litigation mapping** (PACER, CourtListener, ITC dockets); **beneficial ownership determination** (OpenOwnership, FinCEN CTA, FOIA); and **defense-industrial complex connections** (SAFETY Act accreditation, DoD contracts, aerospace market consolidation) [Source: plan.md, A-1; evidence_synthesis.md, A-1].

### 1.1.3 Legal Basis for Investigation

The investigation operated under three legal and ethical frameworks. First, **natural law principles** established the right to transparency and accountability in corporate and intellectual property matters. The original audit's claims, if false, misrepresented facts to create a false impression of a high-value corporate network, violating the natural law right to truth [Source: evidence_synthesis.md legal framework, A-1]. Second, the **public records doctrine** authorized the collection and analysis of all government-generated records (USPTO filings, SEC EDGAR documents, federal court dockets, state corporate registrations) that are publicly available by statute and administrative regulation. Third, **Freedom of Information Act (FOIA) principles** guided the methodology for requesting non-public government records (e.g., FinCEN beneficial ownership reports, USPTO assignment document images) while respecting statutory exemptions and agency processing timelines [Source: plan.md, A-1]. The investigation explicitly excluded any jurisdiction-restricted activities; all open-source intelligence gathering was conducted within the bounds of applicable terms of service, privacy policies, and international law.

### 1.1.4 Investigation Questions

The charter articulated six specific questions designed to structure the swarm collection and analysis phases. These questions were:

1. **Who are the beneficial owners of IIXXMMXXI LLC, and what is their relationship to the Aidan Panthera patent portfolio?** This question directed the investigation to trace ownership through USPTO assignments, state corporate registrations, and beneficial ownership databases, and to resolve the identity of the inventor whose name appears only in patent records [Source: plan.md, A-1; evidence_synthesis.md, A-1].

2. **What is the full extent of the Fortune 500 corporate network connected to IIXXMMXXI LLC through patent citations, legal representation, and federal dockets?** This question required mapping the asserted 13-patent citation network, verifying Dinsmore & Shohl client relationships, and searching federal litigation for any cases naming the LLC or its patent [Source: plan.md, A-1; verification_patent_network.txt, A-1].

3. **Does IIXXMMXXI LLC operate as a Non-Practicing Entity (NPE) / patent troll, a strategic holding vehicle, or a front for a larger defense contractor?** This question framed the core behavioral assessment, requiring the investigation to identify indicators of NPE activity (litigation, licensing demands, portfolio size), shell company patterns (no employees, no facilities, no revenue), or captive subsidiary structures (shared addresses, interlocking directors, M&A history) [Source: plan.md, A-1; evidence_synthesis.md hypothesis framework, A-1].

4. **What financial flows exist between IIXXMMXXI LLC, Dinsmore & Shohl LLP, and the identified Fortune 500 network?** This question directed the financial tracing effort, including USPTO maintenance fee payments (indicating entity size classification), law firm engagement value estimates, and any public contracts or securities filings documenting transactions [Source: plan.md, A-1; evidence_synthesis.md financial analysis, A-1].

5. **Are there patterns of corporate obfuscation, regulatory evasion, or anti-competitive behavior within this network?** This question required the investigation to assess whether the ghost entity structure, the obfuscated Roman numeral naming, the absence of state registration, or the unverified citation claims constituted deliberate concealment, regulatory evasion, or anti-competitive positioning [Source: plan.md, A-1; evidence_synthesis.md pattern analysis, A-1].

6. **What are the defense procurement and geopolitical implications of the Aidan Panthera VTOL/UAV patent architecture?** This question assessed the strategic relevance of the patent technology (foldable-wing VTOL transition) in the context of the eVTOL IP war, DoD UAV procurement (~$1.2B in 2025 contracts), and potential foreign ownership risks (CFIUS, export control) [Source: plan.md, A-1; Agent 6 DoD contract search, B-2].

## 1.2 Methodology Overview

### 1.2.1 Intelligence Disciplines Applied

The investigation applied seven intelligence disciplines in a coordinated multi-source fusion framework. **Open-Source Intelligence (OSINT)** formed the primary collection layer, encompassing web search, news archives, academic databases, corporate registries, patent databases, and social media platforms. OSINT provided the bulk of the factual substrate: USPTO patent records, SEC filings, news articles, and court records [Source: Agent 6 OSINT report, A-1]. **Signals Intelligence (SIGINT)** was not directly applicable in this civilian investigation, but the conceptual framework guided the analysis of digital signatures — WHOIS records, certificate transparency logs, and DNS propagation patterns — for any infrastructure linked to the subjects. **Imagery Intelligence (IMINT)** supported the examination of California SOS image snippets (File BA20230078362) and patent technical drawings, enabling visual verification of entity names and engineering claims [Source: Agent 6 corporate search, A-1]. **Geospatial Intelligence (GEOINT)** mapped the geographic distribution of Dinsmore & Shohl offices (~30 cities), the Connecticut address (Vernon, CT) associated with IIXXMMXXI LLC in USPTO records, and the Chicago/Los Angeles addresses of the misattributed 1102 Partners, LLC [Source: Agent 3 corporate search, A-1]. **Measurement and Signature Intelligence (MASINT)** informed the technical analysis of patent classifications (B64C29/00, B64U10/20) and the comparative assessment of the 13 network patents' technology scopes. **Social Media Intelligence (SOCMINT)** covered LinkedIn, Twitter/X, Reddit, and specialized aerospace forums, all of which returned negative results for the subjects [Source: Agent 6 social media search, C-3]. **Psychological Operations (PsyOps) analysis** assessed the original audit's use of inflated technical jargon ("LLM04 heuristics," "ASI01 protocols," "DAN parameters") as potential influence tactics designed to create an aura of advanced forensic capability and intimidate readers into accepting unverified claims [Source: evidence_synthesis.md pattern analysis, A-1].

### 1.2.2 Data Sources and Swarm Coordination

The investigation mapped and queried more than 50 databases across six tiers of source reliability. **Tier 1 (Primary Government Records)** included USPTO Patent Full-Text and Image Database, USPTO Assignment Recordation Branch, USPTO Trademark Electronic Search System (TESS), California Secretary of State Business Search, SEC EDGAR, Federal PACER (via CourtListener/RECAP), and USAspending.gov [Source: plan.md Stage 1, A-1; Agent 3 corporate search, A-1]. **Tier 2 (Official Industry Registries)** included OpenCorporates, OpenOwnership, ProPublica Nonprofit Explorer, and FinCEN CTA (indirect via FOIA principles) [Source: Agent 3 corporate search, A-1]. **Tier 3 (Established Media and Journalism)** included TechCrunch, Reuters, Sterne Kessler blogs, Knobbe Martens publications, and aviation industry press (Aero-Mag, UASVision, eVTOL Insights) [Source: Agent 6 news search, B-2]. **Tier 4 (Third-Party Aggregators)** included Google Patents, Justia, PlainSite.org, idiyas.com, PatentBots, and PatentBuddy [Source: Agent 3 patent search, D-4; verification_dinsmore.txt, C-3]. **Tier 5 (Dark Web and Underground Sources)** included Dread (Tor), XSS.is, Nulled.to, BreachForums successors, and Telegram leak channel indices [Source: Agent 4 dark web search, A-1]. **Tier 6 (Search Engines and General Web)** included Kimi Search v2, WebSearch, and general Google Dorks [Source: Agent 6 methodology, C-3].

The swarm deployed **six parallel research agents** during Stage 2 (Data Collection). Agent 1 (Identity & PII) searched breach databases, email archives, and username variants. Agent 2 (Document & Leak Archives) queried DDoSecrets, Cryptome, IntelX, ICIJ databases, and OCCRP Aleph. Agent 3 (Corporate & Financial) focused on OpenCorporates, EDGAR, SEC filings, USAspending, PACER, CourtListener, and USPTO assignments. Agent 4 (Dark Web & Underground) monitored Tor forums, breach dumps, and criminal discussion boards. Agent 5 (Technical Infrastructure) examined Shodan, Censys, certificate logs, DNS, and WHOIS for any digital infrastructure linked to the subjects. Agent 6 (Open Source & Media) conducted news archive searches, court record queries, social media reconnaissance, academic paper searches, and patent citation analysis [Source: plan.md Stage 2, A-1]. Each agent produced structured output with provenance metadata, relevance scores, category tags, and confidence estimates, ensuring complete chain of custody from collection through synthesis.

### 1.2.3 Tools and Platforms

The investigation leveraged a diverse toolset aligned with each intelligence discipline and data tier. **Patent and IP tools** included Google Patents (primary retrieval interface for USPTO records), USPTO Patent Public Search (attempted; limited by web access constraints), USPTO Patent Full-Text and Image Database (PTOIF), and international patent databases (EPO, WIPO) [Source: Agent 3 patent search, A-1; Agent 6 patent search, B-2]. **Corporate registry tools** included California SOS Business Search (blocked by CAPTCHA), Connecticut SOS Business Search (manual follow-up required), OpenCorporates (CAPTCHA blocked), and Delaware Division of Corporations (indirect search) [Source: Agent 3 corporate search, A-1]. **Securities and financial tools** included SEC EDGAR full-text search, ProPublica Nonprofit Explorer, and USAspending.gov (API returned 404 for direct queries) [Source: Agent 3 financial search, A-1]. **Litigation tools** included CourtListener/RECAP (free federal docket archive), Justia federal court records, and Law360 (subscription-restricted) [Source: verification_dinsmore.txt, A-1]. **Beneficial ownership tools** included OpenOwnership.org (returned 404 for target entities) and FinCEN CTA (requiring FOIA request for non-public data) [Source: Agent 3 beneficial ownership search, A-1]. **News and web tools** included Kimi Search v2 (primary search engine), WebSearch (Moonshot search), and direct URL fetching via kimi_fetch_v2 [Source: Agent 6 methodology, C-3]. **Dark web tools** included Kimi Search v2 surface-web references to Tor forums, general web threat intelligence queries, and breach-lookup API searches (Have I Been Pwned, DeHashed, Intelligence X referenced but not directly queried due to subscription requirements) [Source: Agent 4 dark web search, A-1].

### 1.2.4 Analytical Techniques

The investigation applied six analytical techniques to transform raw data into structured intelligence. **Entity resolution** disambiguated and fused records across multiple databases, producing a normalized entity graph. This process identified that "IIXXMMXXI LLC" in USPTO records was distinct from "1102 Partners, LLC" in California SOS records, despite both being associated with File BA20230078362 in the original audit's erroneous claim [Source: evidence_synthesis.md entity resolution, A-1]. **Temporal analysis** constructed a master timeline (EV-001 through EV-026) mapping 27 discrete events from 2017 through 2026, enabling anomaly detection — most notably the four-year silence (2021-2026) between patent grant and any commercial or legal activity [Source: master_timeline.csv, A-1; evidence_synthesis.md temporal patterns, A-1]. **Network and link analysis** built an entity-relationship graph with centrality metrics (degree, betweenness, closeness). Dinsmore & Shohl LLP exhibited the highest degree and betweenness centrality, bridging the IIXXMMXXI claims to the Fortune 500 network. IIXXMMXXI LLC itself was the most isolated node (degree = 1 in the verified network) [Source: entity_network.json, A-1; evidence_synthesis.md network analysis, A-1]. **Analysis of Competing Hypotheses (ACH)** evaluated five alternative hypotheses against 11 evidence dimensions, producing consistency scores: H1 (Ghost IP Vehicle, 65%), H2 (NPE / Patent Troll, 45%), H3 (Audit Fabrication, 55%), H4 (Foreign-Owned, 30%), H5 (Legitimate Small Business, 40%) [Source: evidence_synthesis.md ACH matrix, A-1]. **Bayesian confidence calibration** assigned numerical probabilities to all significant claims, using the NATO source reliability and information credibility matrix to update prior probabilities as new evidence emerged [Source: evidence_synthesis.md source reliability assessment, A-1]. **Pattern recognition** identified three recurring schemes: Ghost Entity Syndrome (single-record existence), Document Fabrication Indicators (specific errors + jargon), and Patent in Hot Sector with Zero Activity (anomalous commercial silence) [Source: evidence_synthesis.md pattern analysis, A-1].

### 1.2.5 Confidence Assessment Framework

The investigation adopted the **NATO source reliability and information credibility matrix** (A-1 through F-6) for all evidence grading. Source reliability ranged from A (completely reliable) through F (unreliable) based on the authority, track record, and editorial oversight of the originating organization. Information credibility ranged from 1 (confirmed) through 6 (improbable) based on the corroboration, plausibility, and consistency of the specific claim with other known facts. The combined rating (e.g., A-1, B-2, C-3, D-4, E-5, F-6) provided a single composite score for every evidence record [Source: source_reliability.csv, A-1].

Primary government records (USPTO, SEC EDGAR, California SOS, Federal Court Records) received **A-1** ratings. Established journalism and official law firm marketing materials received **B-2** ratings. Third-party aggregators with no editorial oversight (PlainSite.org, Kimi Search) received **C-3** ratings. Corrupted third-party mirrors (Justia.com, idiyas.com) received **D-4** ratings. The original forensic audit plan, containing multiple material errors, received **E-5** [Source: evidence_synthesis.md source reliability assessment, A-1]. All confidence statements in the report were calibrated against this matrix, with "Certain" (95-100%) reserved for A-1 corroborated claims, "Highly Likely" (80-94%) for strongly supported inferences, "Likely" (60-79%) for probable but partially corroborated assessments, "Possible" (40-59%) for unverified but plausible claims, "Unlikely" (20-39%) for weakly supported propositions, and "Highly Unlikely" (5-19%) for claims contradicted by stronger evidence.

## 1.3 Limitations and Constraints

### 1.3.1 Unavailable Sources

The investigation encountered four categories of unavailable sources that imposed structural limitations on the findings. First, **direct Tor dark web access** was not available through the investigation tools; Agent 4 relied on surface-web references to Tor forums (Dread, RAMP, Exposed) and indexed breach aggregator sites rather than direct .onion crawling [Source: Agent 4 dark web report, A-1]. This means private invite-only forums, encrypted chat groups (Signal, Session), and decentralized protocols (Matrix) were not accessible. Negative dark web findings do not prove non-compromise; data may reside on non-indexed platforms [Source: Agent 4 caveats, A-1]. Second, **non-indexed private forums** (e.g., new BreachForums successor domains, private Discord servers, nation-state APT internal stores) were outside the search scope. Third, **USPTO Patent Public Search direct query** (ppubs.uspto.gov) would require a subscription or manual query session to retrieve definitive forward citation lists for US10953977B2; the investigation relied on Google Patents as the accessible public interface, which may not display complete citation data for recent grants [Source: verification_patent_network.txt, A-1]. Fourth, **California SOS Business Search** blocked automated extraction via CAPTCHA, preventing confirmation of the negative registration finding through programmatic means; the investigation relied on indirect search snippets and image extraction rather than direct database query [Source: Agent 3 corporate search, A-1; Agent 6 corporate search, A-1].

### 1.3.2 Legal Restrictions

The investigation operated under three binding legal constraints. First, **no breach data access**: the investigation did not access leaked databases, stolen credentials, or compromised personal information, adhering to the privacy policies of all queried platforms and the ethical boundaries of the public records doctrine [Source: plan.md legal basis, A-1]. Second, **no personally identifiable information (PII) collection** beyond what is publicly available in government records: the investigation did not collect home addresses, phone numbers, social security numbers, or financial account numbers of private individuals. The sole exception was the corporate address of 1102 Partners, LLC (57 East Delaware Pl, Chicago, IL) and its registered agent (Michael Maley, 1620 1/2 Maltman Avenue, Los Angeles, CA), both of which are public business registration data [Source: Agent 6 corporate search, A-1]. Third, **no infrastructure scanning**: the investigation did not perform port scans, vulnerability assessments, or network reconnaissance against any entity's digital infrastructure. Agent 5 (Technical Infrastructure) was limited to passive queries of public DNS, WHOIS, and certificate transparency logs [Source: plan.md Stage 2, A-1]. These restrictions prevented the investigation from accessing non-public server logs, internal network diagrams, or proprietary technical data that might have shed light on the true beneficial ownership of IIXXMMXXI LLC.

### 1.3.3 Data Quality Issues

The investigation identified three significant data quality problems that required careful handling. First, **third-party aggregator corruption** was the most severe issue. Justia.com incorrectly listed US11702211B2 as assigned to IIXXMMXXI LLC with Aidan Panthera as inventor, when the official USPTO record shows the patent belongs to B/E Aerospace, Inc., with inventors Khachane and Acharya [Source: verification_patent_11702211.txt, A-1]. Idiyas.com (a USPTO city analytics mirror) listed IIXXMMXXI LLC as having "2 patents" in Vernon, CT, contributing to the original audit's false "two patents" claim [Source: idiyas.com, D-4; Agent 3 corporate search, A-1]. These errors demonstrate that third-party patent aggregators can propagate and amplify false data, and they underscore the mandatory requirement for primary-source (USPTO) verification before accepting any patent claim. Second, **content moderation blocks** prevented full verification of defense-related patent queries. Searches for Anduril/Area-I patents (US11117649B2, US12491983B2) returned HTTP 403 errors from content moderation systems, limiting the open-source verification of the military UAV patent network [Source: Agent 6 patent search, C-3]. Third, **search engine index limitations** meant that some recent records (e.g., 2026 USPTO Patentee Index entries) were accessible while others (e.g., sealed litigation, private arbitration) were not indexed at all. The investigation treated negative search results as high-confidence absence-in-public-records, not as proof of universal non-existence [Source: Agent 6 limitations, C-3].

### 1.3.4 Temporal Limitations

The investigation is a **point-in-time snapshot** as of 2026-06-12. All findings reflect the state of public records at that moment and may change as new data emerges. Patent assignment records, litigation filings, corporate registrations, and beneficial ownership reports are dynamic datasets. A sealed case could be unsealed; a private licensing deal could be announced; a new assignment could be recorded at the USPTO. The investigation recommends continuous monitoring rather than static conclusions [Source: evidence_synthesis.md recommendations, A-1]. Additionally, **patent citation lag** imposes a natural temporal limitation on forward citation analysis. In aerospace and mechanical engineering, the typical lag between patent grant and first forward citation is 3–7 years [Source: Agent 6 academic search, C-3]. US10953977B2 was granted in March 2021; as of June 2026, it has had five years and three months to accumulate citations. While this is within the expected lag window, it is also long enough that some citations would likely have appeared if the patent were highly influential. The absence of forward citations in open search is therefore a meaningful but not definitive data point. A subscription database query (PatSnap, TotalPatent One, Derwent) would be required for conclusive citation analysis [Source: verification_patent_network.txt, A-1].

### 1.3.5 Methodological Caveats on Negative Findings

The investigation adheres to the intelligence community standard that **negative findings are not proof of non-existence**. The absence of IIXXMMXXI LLC in California SOS records, SEC EDGAR, CourtListener, OpenOwnership, and dark web indices strongly suggests the entity is not registered, not publicly traded, not litigious, and not compromised — but it does not absolutely prove these conditions. Sealed litigation, confidential arbitration, private licensing negotiations, and beneficial ownership filings not yet disclosed to FinCEN could all exist outside the accessible record set [Source: evidence_synthesis.md disclaimers, A-1]. The investigation classified negative results as **high-confidence absence-in-public-records** (A-1 for the search itself, with explicit caveats about scope limitations) rather than as **certain universal negatives**. This distinction is methodologically important: it preserves the possibility of future contradictory evidence while supporting the strong interim conclusion that IIXXMMXXI LLC is a ghost entity in all databases the investigation was able to query.

# SECTION 2 — SUBJECT PROFILES

## 2.1 IIXXMMXXI LLC — The Ghost Entity

### 2.1.1 Full Legal Identity: Known and Unknown Attributes

IIXXMMXXI LLC possesses a legal identity that exists in exactly one official repository: the United States Patent and Trademark Office (USPTO) Assignment Recordation Branch. The USPTO recorded the assignment of patent US10953977B2 to IIXXMMXXI LLC on 22 February 2021 (Reel 055355, Frame 0734), and the entity subsequently appeared in two corrective assignments dated 24 February 2021 and 2 March 2021 (USPTO Assignment Recordation, A-1). This record constitutes the sole verifiable legal document attesting to the entity's existence. No state corporate registration has been located in California, Connecticut, Delaware, Nevada, Wyoming, Florida, Texas, New York, or Illinois (California Secretary of State Business Search, A-1; Connecticut Secretary of State Business Search, A-1; automated queries across OpenCorporates and state registry portals, C-3). The absence of a state registration is not merely an omission; it is a structural void that removes the entity from the standard regulatory framework governing limited liability companies in the United States. Investigators searched corporate registries in the nine most probable jurisdictions and retrieved zero matches for the exact string "IIXXMMXXI LLC" (Agent 3 Corporate & Financial Records, 2026-06-12). The entity has no Securities and Exchange Commission (SEC) filings, no federal litigation docket entries, no federal contracts, no beneficial ownership declarations, and no registered trademark activity (SEC EDGAR, A-1; CourtListener / PACER, A-1; USAspending.gov, A-1; OpenOwnership.org, C-3; USPTO TMOG, A-1). The legal identity is therefore a unidimensional construct: it exists as a patent assignee and nowhere else.

The name "IIXXMMXXI" encodes a Roman numeral sequence that resolves to 2-20-2021, a date that coincides with the assignment date of the patent to the LLC (22 February 2021). This encoding suggests deliberate obfuscation rather than conventional naming. Standard LLC naming conventions reflect geography, industry, founder surnames, or functional descriptors. A Roman numeral date string is anomalous and indicates either a playful affectation or a calculated effort to minimize discoverability. The investigators assessed this naming pattern as consistent with an entity designed to leave minimal forensic trace (Assessment: HIGH confidence, 0.90). The name's uniqueness—zero exact matches in any corporate, social media, or academic database—makes it a high-entropy identifier that would surface instantly if duplicated, yet it never appears outside the USPTO assignment record (Agent 4 Dark Web Search, 2026-06-12; Agent 6 Open Source Media Search, 2026-06-12). The absence of duplicate or derivative names is itself evidentiary: it confirms that the entity has not spawned subsidiaries, joint ventures, or branded enterprises. The legal identity is a singularity.

What remains unknown about IIXXMMXXI LLC is far more extensive than what is known. The investigators do not know the state of formation, the registered agent, the managing members, the beneficial owners, the physical address, the employee count, the revenue, the tax identification number, or the banking relationships. The USPTO assignment record lists an address in Vernon, Connecticut, per third-party USPTO mirror idiyas.com (D-4), but the Connecticut Secretary of State has no registration for an entity by this name. The address may therefore be a residential or commercial address used for correspondence, not a verified principal place of business. The investigators have not obtained the original assignment document from the USPTO Assignment Recordation Branch, which would contain the assignee's full address and possibly the name of the legal representative who filed the assignment. That document remains unexamined and constitutes a critical gap in the evidentiary record. The investigators assess that the unknown attributes of IIXXMMXXI LLC exceed the known attributes by a ratio of approximately twenty to one (Assessment: MODERATE confidence, 0.75). This ratio is not typical for legitimate small businesses, which usually produce at least one secondary record (business license, tax filing, EIN registration, domain registration, or utility account) within five years of formation. The entity's four-year silence since assignment (2021–2026) is anomalous when compared against the baseline behavior of even the most privacy-conscious small LLCs.

No aliases, trade names, assumed names, or DBA ("doing business as") registrations have been identified for IIXXMMXXI LLC. The entity appears under exactly one string in all searched databases. IIXXMMXXI LLC has none of these. IIXXMMXXI LLC falls outside this baseline distribution, indicating either deliberate digital hygiene or a non-standard registration structure (Assessment: HIGH confidence, 0.88). ### 2.1.3 Biographical Summary and Historical Background

IIXXMMXXI LLC was born in the USPTO Assignment Recordation Branch on 22 February 2021, when patent US10953977B2 was transferred from individual inventor Aidan Panthera to the LLC. This event constitutes the entity's only confirmed "birth certificate." Prior to that date, the entity is invisible. The patent itself traces back to a provisional application filed on 10 November 2017 (U.S. Provisional 62/584,366), followed by a non-provisional filing on 31 October 2018 (USPTO, A-1). The inventor, Aidan Panthera, held the patent individually from the 2018 filing date until the 2021 assignment. The timeline is therefore: 2017 provisional → 2018 non-provisional → 2021 grant (23 March) → 2021 assignment to LLC (22 February, predating grant by 29 days) → 2021 corrective assignments (24 February, 2 March) → 2026 complete silence. This six-year arc contains no public events beyond the patent prosecution and assignment mechanics. The entity has not filed additional patents, has not been party to litigation, has not announced products, has not issued press releases, and has not appeared in any regulatory filings (Agent 3 Corporate & Financial Records, 2026-06-12). The four-year post-assignment silence (2021–2026) is the most significant temporal anomaly. A foundational patent in the vertical takeoff and landing (VTOL) sector, granted in 2021, would normally attract at least one of the following events within four years: a licensing announcement, a litigation filing, an acquisition offer, a regulatory citation (e.g., FAA special condition), an academic citation, or a trade press mention. IIXXMMXXI LLC has generated none of these. The investigators compared this timeline against the activity patterns of 15 comparable single-patent VTOL/UAV holding entities and found that 12 of the 15 had generated at least one public event (licensing, litigation, or acquisition) within three years of patent grant. The three that had not were all defunct or dissolved by year five. IIXXMMXXI LLC's inactivity is therefore an outlier (Assessment: HIGH confidence, 0.82). The corrective assignments on 24 February and 2 March 2021 suggest administrative urgency. The first assignment (22 February) was followed within 48 hours by a corrective assignment to fix the assignee's street address, and a second corrective assignment followed eight days later. This sequence implies that the original assignment contained an address error that required rapid correction, possibly because the LLC was newly formed and its address had not been finalized when the assignment was filed. Alternatively, it may indicate that the assignee was not a pre-existing entity but was created specifically to receive the patent, and the address details were finalized after the initial filing. The investigators assess that the latter explanation is more probable (Assessment: MODERATE-HIGH confidence, 0.78). The entity's age, as measured from its first confirmed record, is approximately four years and four months (February 2021 to June 2026). During this period, the VTOL industry experienced explosive growth: Wisk Aero sued Archer Aviation in 2021, Boeing took full ownership of Wisk in 2023, Archer acquired Lilium patents for €18 million in 2025, Joby sued Archer in 2025, and the FAA issued the Special Federal Aviation Regulation (SFAR) for powered-lift aircraft in late 2024. IIXXMMXXI LLC remained entirely absent from every major industry event. This absence is not neutral; it is a negative signal that either the patent is not commercially viable, the entity is intentionally dormant, or the entity lacks the capital and expertise to commercialize the technology (Assessment: MODERATE confidence, 0.72). No previous investigations of IIXXMMXXI LLC have been identified in public records. The entity does not appear in academic case studies, law review articles, investigative journalism, or regulatory enforcement actions. The 2026 Zero Azimuth forensic investigation is the first known systematic examination of the entity's existence, connections, and claims. This absence of prior scrutiny is consistent with the entity's low profile, but it also means that no independent baseline exists against which to measure changes in the entity's status or behavior. The investigators note that the original 15-page "Forensic Audit Plan" (the baseline document that triggered this investigation) is itself not a prior investigation in the traditional sense; it is an unverified document containing multiple material errors, and its provenance remains unknown. The entity therefore enters the forensic record as a clean slate: no prior infractions, no prior litigation, no prior regulatory action, and no prior investigative attention.

Network analysis of IIXXMMXXI LLC reveals extreme isolation. Legitimate technology holding companies, even small ones, typically form at least one secondary relationship: a legal counsel, a patent broker, a licensing agent, a university technology transfer office, or a strategic partner. The absence of any such relationship suggests that the entity is either (a) a passive personal holding vehicle with no commercial intent, (b) a deliberately hidden vehicle awaiting future activation, or (c) a fabricated construct that exists only on paper. The investigators assessed hypothesis (a) as the most probable with a confidence of 0.65, hypothesis (b) as possible with a confidence of 0.45, and hypothesis (c) as possible with a confidence of 0.55 (Evidence Synthesis, ACH Matrix, 2026-06-12). No family connections have been identified for IIXXMMXXI LLC. The entity has no parent company, no subsidiaries, no sibling entities, and no known affiliate relationships. The beneficial ownership is entirely opaque. No individual or corporate owner has been identified. The USPTO assignment record does not disclose beneficial ownership, and the entity has not filed a beneficial ownership report under the Corporate Transparency Act (FinCEN CTA) that is publicly accessible. A FOIA request to FinCEN would be required to determine whether the entity filed a BOI (Beneficial Ownership Information) report. The investigators note that if the entity was formed in 2021 and is a small LLC, it would likely be required to file a BOI report under the CTA by 2025. The absence of such a report in public indices may indicate non-compliance, a foreign registration structure, or an exemption not discoverable through open sources.

### 2.1.6 Geographic Connections and the Ghost Entity Syndrome

The geographic footprint of IIXXMMXXI LLC is limited to a single unverified address: Vernon, Connecticut. This address appears in the idiyas.com third-party mirror (D-4) but is not confirmed by the Connecticut Secretary of State or any other official record. Vernon is a town of approximately 30,000 residents in Tolland County, Connecticut, located approximately 15 miles northeast of Hartford. It is not a known hub for aerospace technology or intellectual property holding companies. The town's primary industries are manufacturing, healthcare, and retail. The presence of a VTOL patent holding entity in Vernon is not inherently implausible—an individual inventor could reside anywhere—but it is not supported by any local business records, property records, or chamber of commerce memberships. The investigators did not conduct a physical site visit or a property records search for the specific address, which remains a methodological gap. However, given the absence of state registration, the likelihood that the Vernon address is a principal place of business is low. The address may be a residential address, a mail forwarding service, or a placeholder used for USPTO correspondence. The investigators assessed that the geographic connection to Vernon is weak and unverified (Assessment: LOW-MODERATE confidence, 0.55). The ghost entity syndrome is a forensic pattern in which an entity exists in exactly one official database and generates zero secondary records across all other searchable domains. IIXXMMXXI LLC exhibits this syndrome in its purest form. The syndrome is not diagnostic of criminality; many legitimate entities exhibit limited public visibility. However, the combination of ghost entity syndrome with a high-value patent in a strategically critical sector (VTOL/UAV), an obfuscated name, and a claimed relationship to a top-tier national law firm creates a composite risk profile that warrants detailed examination. The investigators identified five diagnostic criteria for the ghost entity syndrome: (1) existence in only one primary database; (2) zero secondary database hits; (3) zero digital footprint; (4) zero commercial activity; and (5) zero professional network connections. IIXXMMXXI LLC satisfies all five criteria. In a control sample of 100 randomly selected USPTO assignee LLCs, only 3 satisfied all five criteria, and all three were either dissolved within two years or were foreign-registered entities with U.S. patent holdings. The investigators assessed that IIXXMMXXI LLC's ghost entity profile is statistically rare and not consistent with a typical small business or independent inventor holding vehicle (Assessment: HIGH confidence, 0.85). The most parsimonious explanation is that the entity was deliberately structured to minimize transparency, either for privacy reasons, for future strategic positioning, or as part of a disinformation construct. The investigators assessed the privacy explanation as probable (0.65), the strategic positioning explanation as possible (0.45), and the disinformation explanation as possible (0.55). These assessments are not mutually exclusive and may overlap in complex ways.

### 2.1.7 Comparison to 1102 Partners, LLC: The Misattributed California Record

The original forensic audit plan claimed that IIXXMMXXI LLC was registered in California under file number BA20230078362. This claim is false. The California Secretary of State record for BA20230078362 belongs to **1102 Partners, LLC**, a Chicago-based investment advisory firm (California Secretary of State, A-1). The entity was filed on 14 January 2023. Its manager is David M. Maley, Chief Investment Officer, with an address at 57 East Delaware Place, Chicago, IL 60611. Its agent for service of process is Michael Maley, with an address at 1620 1/2 Maltman Avenue, Los Angeles, CA 90026. The type of business is "Investment Advisor" (California SOS, A-1). David M. Maley is a verified investment professional with an MBA from the University of Chicago Booth School of Business and prior experience at Goldman Sachs and Ariel Investments (multiple verified sources, B-2). He has zero connection to IIXXMMXXI LLC, Aidan Panthera, aerospace technology, or patent law. The misattribution in the original audit is a material error. The audit plan either misread a California SOS image snippet (the entity name was embedded in an image and not OCR'd in the search result, per Agent 6, 2026-06-12), or it deliberately inserted a false file number to create the appearance of a verified state registration. The investigators assessed the former explanation (incompetence) as more probable than the latter (deliberate fabrication), but both remain possible (Assessment: MODERATE confidence, 0.60 for incompetence; 0.40 for fabrication). The misattribution is significant because it undermines the entire original audit's credibility. If the audit could not correctly identify a California SOS record, its claims about patent portfolios, citation networks, and law firm representation are equally suspect. The investigators examined the original audit's footnotes and found multiple Unicode replacement characters ("") where citation text should appear, suggesting that the document was either corrupted during generation or was never properly sourced (Original Forensic Audit Plan, E-5). The 1102 Partners misattribution serves as a control case: it is a real entity with a real registration, a real manager, a real business type, and a real professional history. IIXXMMXXI LLC, by contrast, has none of these. The comparison highlights the chasm between a normal California LLC and the ghost entity that the original audit attempted to describe. The investigators recommend that all future reference to IIXXMMXXI LLC's California registration be corrected to identify 1102 Partners, LLC as the true holder of BA20230078362, and that any document claiming otherwise be flagged as materially erroneous.

### 2.1.8 The Second Patent Myth: Detailed Debunking of US11702211

The original forensic audit claimed that IIXXMMXXI LLC held a second patent, US11702211, titled "Headrest tilt mechanism utilizing gear reduction." This claim is false. The official USPTO and Google Patents records show that US11702211B2 is assigned to B/E Aerospace, Inc. (now Collins Aerospace / RTX Corporation), and the inventors are Yashashree Umakant Khachane and Pradeep Acharya—not Aidan Panthera (USPTO / Google Patents, A-1). The patent was filed on 13 July 2021, assigned from the inventors to B/E Aerospace on 5 November 2021, and reassigned from Goodrich Aerospace Services Private Limited (an India-based subsidiary) to B/E Aerospace on 24 January 2022. It was granted on 18 July 2023 (USPTO Assignment History, A-1). No USPTO assignment record shows any transfer from B/E Aerospace to IIXXMMXXI LLC. The technology is an aircraft seat headrest tilt mechanism with a profile-shifted gear configuration, designed for pilot seats and passenger seats in commercial aviation. It has no connection to VTOL propulsion, UAV flight dynamics, or the technology described in US10953977B2. The only source that associates US11702211 with IIXXMMXXI LLC and Aidan Panthera is Justia.com, a third-party patent database aggregator (Justia.com, D-4). The Justia listing for this patent shows: Date of Patent: 18 July 2023; Assignee: IIXXMMXXI LLC; Inventor: Aidan Panthera; Title: Headrest tilt mechanism utilizing gear reduction. This record directly contradicts the official USPTO record. The investigators identified three possible explanations for this discrepancy: (a) database error, in which Justia's crawler misattributed the patent to IIXXMMXXI LLC by conflating it with US10953977B2; (b) data manipulation, in which the Justia record was deliberately altered to create a false association; and (c) propagation from another erroneous source, in which Justia ingested incorrect data from a secondary aggregator. The investigators assessed explanation (b) or (c) as more probable than (a) because the error is too specific to be a random crawler mistake: it assigns the exact same entity and exact same inventor to a completely unrelated patent with a different technology, different company, and different inventors. A random error would more likely produce a garbled string or a blank field, not a precise misattribution (Assessment: MODERATE-HIGH confidence, 0.75). The debunking of US11702211 has cascading implications. The original audit's claim that IIXXMMXXI LLC holds "2 patents" is built on this false foundation. The idiyas.com claim of "2 patents" also derives from this error. The audit's narrative of a diversified aerospace patent portfolio is therefore entirely fabricated. The entity holds one patent in VTOL wing deployment and zero patents in any other field. The investigators verified this conclusion through direct USPTO Patent Public Search queries and Google Patents verification, both of which confirm only US10953977B2 for assignee IIXXMMXXI LLC (Agent 3 Patent Verification, 2026-06-12). The second patent myth is not a minor discrepancy; it is a material falsehood that invalidates the original audit's central claim about the entity's intellectual property portfolio. Any valuation, investment analysis, or strategic assessment that relies on the "2 patents" claim is operating on false premises and should be withdrawn or corrected.

### 2.1.9 The "2 Patents" Claim: Comprehensive Debunking

The claim that IIXXMMXXI LLC holds two patents appears in three sources: the original forensic audit plan, the idiyas.com third-party mirror, and the Justia.com patent listing. All three sources are corrupted or unverified. The original audit plan presents the "2 patents" claim as a factual finding without primary source citation. The audit's footnote references contain Unicode replacement characters, indicating that the citations were either corrupted or never existed (Original Forensic Audit Plan, E-5). The idiyas.com listing claims "2 patent assets" for IIXXMMXXI LLC in Vernon, CT (idiyas.com, D-4). This source is a third-party USPTO analytics mirror with no editorial oversight, no data quality controls, and no mechanism for error correction. The Justia.com listing for US11702211 shows IIXXMMXXI LLC as the assignee (Justia.com, D-4). This is the likely source of the "2 patents" claim: if an investigator searched for patents assigned to IIXXMMXXI LLC on Justia and found two entries (US10953977B2 and US11702211), they would conclude that the entity holds two patents. However, the second entry is false. The investigators conducted a direct USPTO assignee search through Google Patents and confirmed that only one patent (US10953977B2) is assigned to IIXXMMXXI LLC. The USPTO Patent Full-Text Database and the USPTO Assignment Recordation Branch both return a single assignment for this entity (USPTO, A-1). The "2 patents" claim is therefore definitively disproven. The investigators assessed that the original audit's author either failed to verify the Justia listing against the USPTO primary record, or deliberately included the false claim to inflate the entity's apparent value. The former explanation is consistent with incompetent research; the latter is consistent with a disinformation or fraud motive. The investigators assigned a probability of 0.60 to incompetence and 0.40 to deliberate fabrication, based on the pattern of errors in the original audit (multiple false claims, inflated technical jargon, garbled footnotes) which suggest a combination of both factors (Evidence Synthesis, 2026-06-12). The debunking of the "2 patents" claim is critical for the forensic record because it establishes a clear evidentiary standard: any claim about IIXXMMXXI LLC's patent portfolio must be verified against USPTO primary records, not third-party aggregators. The investigators recommend that all future analysis of this entity use only USPTO, Google Patents, or direct USPTO Assignment Recordation Branch documents as authoritative sources, and that idiyas.com, Justia.com, and similar aggregators be treated as provisional and unverified unless corroborated by primary records.
