### 3.2 Primary Evidence

#### 3.2.1 Patent US10953977B2 — Complete Technical and Procedural Record

Patent US10953977B2, titled "Aircraft with in-flight form varying apparatus," constitutes the sole verified intellectual property asset of IIXXMMXXI LLC and the foundational technical artifact of this investigation. The USPTO granted this patent on March 23, 2021, following a non-provisional application filed October 31, 2018, which claimed priority to U.S. Provisional Patent Application 62/584,366 filed November 10, 2017 (Source: USPTO / Google Patents, A-1, Confidence: 0.99, Fact).

The inventor of record is Aidan Panthera, an individual who appears in no other patent, academic publication, or professional database (Source: USPTO Assignment Search, Agent 6 Open Source Media report, A-1, Confidence: 0.85, Fact). The patent was originally assigned to the individual inventor and subsequently transferred to IIXXMMXXI LLC on February 22, 2021, via an Assignment of Assignors Interest recorded at USPTO Reel 055355 Frame 0734 (Source: USPTO Assignment Recordation, A-1, Confidence: 0.99, Fact). Two corrective assignments followed on February 24, 2021 (Reel 055386 Frame 0092), and March 2, 2021, to rectify the assignee street address (Source: USPTO Assignment Recordation, A-1, Confidence: 0.95, Fact). The compressed timeline of three assignment transactions within eight days suggests administrative urgency or correction of address errors.

The patent's technical scope addresses a VTOL aircraft architecture with deployable and stowable wing segments. The abstract describes an aircraft operable between deployed and stowed positions, wherein wing segments translate downward and rotate in opposite directions to stack atop one another parallel to the fuselage long axis. This configuration enables VTOL flight in constrained spaces, high wind conditions, and obstacle-rich environments, with applications to UAVs and manned personal aerial vehicles (Source: USPTO Patent Full-Text Database, A-1, Confidence: 0.99, Fact).

The technical specification includes: a fuselage with rectangular or square cross-section and pyramidal tapered ends; a pair of wing segments attached via a translation and rotation mechanism comprising upper and lower assemblies with C-shaped outer shafts and semi-cylindrical inner shafts; propulsion devices translatable and rotatable between deployed and stowed positions, including electric motors, propellers, jet engines, and rocket engines; an inverted T-tail empennage mounted to the bottom of the rear fuselage; and a landing pad or dock-based configuration eliminating traditional landing gear (Source: USPTO Patent Claims and Description, A-1, Confidence: 0.95, Fact). The deployed wing configuration provides continuous wing span with high aspect ratio, high lift-to-drag ratio, and low induced drag for efficient cruise flight, while the stowed configuration minimizes footprint for VTOL and storage.

The patent classification codes confirm its aerospace domain: B64C29/00 (VTOL aircraft), B64C3/56 (folding wings), B64U10/20 (UAV / VTOL equipment), B64U30/12 (variable wings), and Y02T50/10 (climate-change mitigation aeronautics) (Source: USPTO Classification Data, A-1, Confidence: 0.99, Fact). The climate-change mitigation classification reflects relevance to electric and hybrid-electric propulsion in the urban air mobility sector.

The adjusted expiration date is August 24, 2039, based on filing date and patent term adjustment (Source: Google Patents Legal Status, A-1, Confidence: 0.95, Fact). The patent remains active as of June 12, 2026, with no recorded lapse for non-payment of maintenance fees. USPTO maintenance fees for small entities are approximately $800, $1,800, and $3,700 at 3.5, 7.5, and 11.5 years; for large entities, approximately $1,600, $3,600, and $7,400 (Source: USPTO Fee Schedule, B-2, Confidence: 0.90, Fact). The entity size status of IIXXMMXXI LLC is not recorded in public USPTO data, but the minimal corporate footprint suggests small entity status is probable.

The claims summary encompasses independent claims covering: (1) the aircraft structure with deployable/stowable wing segments and translation/rotation mechanism; (2) the method of transitioning between VTOL and cruise configurations; and (3) the propulsion system integration with the wing deployment mechanism. Dependent claims specify geometric proportions, propulsion types, control surface configurations, and docking interface details. The patent contains 20 claims total: 3 independent and 17 dependent (Source: USPTO Claims Section, A-1, Confidence: 0.95, Fact).

The assignment history reveals Aidan Panthera filed the provisional application in November 2017 and the non-provisional in October 2018. The assignment to IIXXMMXXI LLC in February 2021 is standard practice for inventors transferring patent rights to an LLC for liability protection, tax optimization, or commercialization. The inventor's address is not fully disclosed in public search, but the assignee address was corrected twice in early March 2021, suggesting the original address was inaccurate (Source: USPTO Assignment Recordation, A-1, Confidence: 0.90, Assessment).

The patent has been cited in the original audit plan as part of an alleged "Fortune 500 citation network." However, no forward citations were identified through Google Patents, web search, or academic databases (Source: Google Patents, PMC, NASA NTRS, C-3, Confidence: 0.70, Negative finding). Citation lag for mechanical and aerospace patents is typically 3-7 years; the 2021 grant date makes the absence of forward citations within five years consistent with normal patent system behavior, though it does not substantiate the original audit's claim of an extensive citation network (Source: PatSnap Blog, C-3, Confidence: 0.65, Assessment). Definitive forward citation analysis requires a direct USPTO Patent Public Search query or subscription database such as PatSnap, TotalPatent One, or Derwent Innovation.

#### 3.2.2 Patent US11702211 — The Debunked Second Patent

The original forensic audit plan asserted that IIXXMMXXI LLC holds a second patent, US11702211, with Aidan Panthera as inventor. This claim has been definitively disproven through direct verification of the official USPTO record. Patent US11702211B2, titled "Headrest tilt mechanism utilizing gear reduction," is assigned to B/E Aerospace, Inc. (now part of Collins Aerospace / RTX Corporation), and its inventors are Yashashree Umakant Khachane and Pradeep Acharya — not Aidan Panthera and not IIXXMMXXI LLC (Source: USPTO / Google Patents, A-1, Confidence: 0.99, Fact).

The official USPTO assignment history shows: application filed July 13, 2021, by B/E Aerospace, Inc.; assigned November 5, 2021, from individual inventors Khachane and Acharya; further assigned January 24, 2022, from Goodrich Aerospace Services Private Limited (India-based subsidiary) to B/E Aerospace, Inc.; granted and published July 18, 2023 (Source: USPTO Assignment Recordation, A-1, Confidence: 0.99, Fact). No record exists of any assignment to IIXXMMXXI LLC or involvement by Aidan Panthera.

The technology is unequivocally an aerospace interiors patent, not a VTOL or UAV propulsion patent. The primary classification is B64D11/0642 (Aircraft seats — Adjustable headrests), within the broader B64 sector (Source: USPTO Classification Data, A-1, Confidence: 0.99, Fact). The patent describes a headrest tilt mechanism for aircraft seats, specifically pilot seats, utilizing a profile-shifted gear configuration (13-tooth driven gear, 12-tooth driver gear) to provide a large reduction ratio in a single step for fine tilt adjustment. The mechanism includes a manual knob actuator, an optional automated actuator, an approximately 35-degree angular range, a biasing mechanism with tension springs to return the headrest to full aft position, and reduced part count compared to conventional harmonic reduction drives (Source: USPTO Patent Full-Text, A-1, Confidence: 0.95, Fact). The patent text explicitly states: "Aircraft seats such as pilot seats are typically equipped with a headrest" and "the mechanism and the assembly may be utilized to equip a seat, such as a passenger seat, and in particular an aircraft pilot seat, with an adjustable headrest" (Source: USPTO Patent Text, A-1, Confidence: 0.99, Fact).

The assignee address comparison confirms complete separation. US11702211B2 is assigned to B/E Aerospace, Inc., headquartered in Winston-Salem, North Carolina (now Collins Aerospace / Raytheon Technologies), with inventor assignments flowing through Goodrich Aerospace Services Private Limited in India. US10953977B2 is assigned to IIXXMMXXI LLC in Vernon, Connecticut, with Aidan Panthera as sole inventor (Source: USPTO Assignment Records, A-1, Confidence: 0.99, Fact). No corporate connection, licensing relationship, or merger activity links IIXXMMXXI LLC to B/E Aerospace, Collins Aerospace, or RTX Corporation.

The inventor verification search for "Aidan Panthera" returned only US10953977B2 in USPTO databases. No other patents, academic publications, LinkedIn profiles, or professional records link this name to aircraft seat design or mechanical engineering. In contrast, Khachane and Acharya are established patentees in aerospace interiors, with multiple patents assigned to B/E Aerospace, Inc. (Source: USPTO Patent Search, Google Patents, A-1, Confidence: 0.95, Fact). An additional related patent, US12559239 ("Integrated seat assembly with headrest and neck support"), was found by Sambasiva Rao Kodati, also assigned to B/E Aerospace, Inc. (Source: USPTO Patent Search, A-1, Confidence: 0.90, Fact).

The only source associating US11702211 with IIXXMMXXI LLC and Aidan Panthera is the third-party aggregator Justia.com (patents.justia.com), which lists the patent with incorrect assignee and incorrect inventors (Source: Justia.com, D-4, Confidence: 0.10, Corrupted data). The discrepancy raises three possibilities: (a) a database error in Justia's crawler or ingestion pipeline misattributing the patent to IIXXMMXXI LLC by conflating it with US10953977B2; (b) deliberate data manipulation or fabrication to create a false association; or (c) propagation from another erroneous source that Justia ingested without verification (Source: Evidence Synthesis, Assessment). The fact that ONLY Justia shows this association while the authoritative USPTO source does not, combined with prior verification that IIXXMMXXI LLC holds only one patent, strongly suggests either a serious database error or deliberate data poisoning (Source: Verification Patent 11702211 Report, A-1, Confidence: 0.85, Assessment).

The implication is severe: any narrative, report, or valuation relying on IIXXMMXXI LLC holding two patents is built on false premises. The entity that supplied this "second patent" information either failed to verify against authoritative sources or deliberately manufactured the connection (Source: Evidence Synthesis, Assessment). IIXXMMXXI LLC remains a one-patent LLC with a single, narrow-focus VTOL patent. It does not demonstrate cross-industry innovation or a diversified patent portfolio (Source: Verification Patent 11702211 Report, A-1, Confidence: 0.95, Assessment).

#### 3.2.3 The 1102 Partners Misattribution — California SOS Record BA20230078362

The original forensic audit plan claimed that IIXXMMXXI LLC is registered in California under Secretary of State file number BA20230078362. This claim is false. The California Secretary of State record for BA20230078362 belongs to 1102 Partners, LLC, a Chicago-based investment advisory firm with zero connection to IIXXMMXXI LLC, aerospace, or patent holding (Source: California Secretary of State Business Search, A-1, Confidence: 0.99, Fact).

The California SOS record, obtained via web search image extraction from bizfileonline.sos.ca.gov, documents: File Number BA20230078362; Date Filed January 14, 2023; Manager/Member David M. Maley; Manager Address 57 East Delaware Place, Unit 1605, Chicago, Illinois 60611; Agent for Service of Process Michael Maley; Agent Address 1620 1/2 Maltman Avenue, Los Angeles, California 90026; Type of Business Investment Advisor; Electronic Signature David M. Maley, January 14, 2023 (Source: California SOS Image Extraction, A-1, Confidence: 0.99, Fact). The entity name rendered in the image is "1102 Partners, LLC" — not IIXXMMXXI LLC. The original audit's misattribution of this record to IIXXMMXXI LLC constitutes a material error.

David M. Maley is a verified investment professional. He serves as Chief Investment Officer (CIO) of 1102 Partners, LLC, and holds an MBA from the University of Chicago Booth School of Business. His prior employment includes Goldman Sachs and Ariel Investments. His profile is documented in investment industry databases and professional directories (Source: Agent 3 Corporate & Financial Report, B-2, Confidence: 0.90, Fact). There is no evidence that David M. Maley, Michael Maley, or 1102 Partners, LLC has any relationship to Aidan Panthera, IIXXMMXXI LLC, Dinsmore & Shohl, or the aerospace patent ecosystem.

The original audit plan's specific citation of file number BA20230078362 as belonging to IIXXMMXXI LLC is too precise to be a random error. The file number exists, the entity is real, but the attribution is completely wrong. This pattern — taking a real, verifiable record and attaching it to the wrong subject — is consistent with either incompetent research (failure to verify the entity name) or deliberate fabrication (using a real file number to create an aura of verifiability while misattributing the content) (Source: Evidence Synthesis, Assessment). The investigation cannot distinguish between these two explanations with available evidence, but both undermine the original audit's credibility (Source: Evidence Synthesis, Confidence: 0.65, Assessment).

#### 3.2.4 Dinsmore & Shohl LLP — Firm Profile and Representation Claims

Dinsmore & Shohl LLP is a verified, legitimate national law firm with a substantial history and documented client base. The firm was founded in 1908 by Frank Dinsmore, with Walter M. Shohl joining as a name partner in 1912. Headquarters: 255 East Fifth Street, Cincinnati, Ohio 45202 (Source: Dinsmore.com, B-2, Confidence: 0.99, Fact). The firm employs approximately 670 attorneys per the National Law Journal 500 (ranked #83 in the United States), while Revelio Labs reports 1,773 total employees as of December 2025 (Source: Law.com, Revelio Labs, B-2, Confidence: 0.90, Fact). The firm maintains approximately 30 offices across U.S. cities including California, Colorado, Illinois, Texas, and Washington, D.C. (Source: Dinsmore.com, B-2, Confidence: 0.95, Fact).

Dinsmore & Shohl is an Am Law 200 and National Law Journal 250 firm with Chambers USA rankings. In 2025, Juristat named Dinsmore a leading firm in its Top Patent Rankings (February 2026), and 260 attorneys were listed as 2026 Best Lawyers / Ones to Watch (August 2025) (Source: Juristat, Dinsmore Press Releases, B-2, Confidence: 0.90, Fact). The IP practice comprises more than 100 IP attorneys nationally, with Matthew A. Molloy serving as co-chair of Patent Procurement. Robert H. Eichenberger (Louisville) is identified as counsel to Fortune 500 corporations in space hardware, medical devices, and agricultural equipment (Source: Dinsmore.com Attorney Bios, B-2, Confidence: 0.85, Fact).

Confirmed Fortune 500 clients documented in public sources include: Procter & Gamble (longstanding relationship approaching 100 years, covering IP, real estate, corporate matters, and litigation including Rely tampon/toxic shock syndrome litigation); Merrell Dow Pharmaceuticals (national trial counsel and MDL counsel in Bendectin litigation); Liberty Mutual Insurance; Dow Corning Corporation (silicone gel breast implant litigation); Brown & Williamson Tobacco Corporation (smoking/health litigation); and International Flavors & Fragrances, Inc. (microwave popcorn butter flavoring litigation) (Source: US News Law Firm Profile, Dinsmore.com Attorney Bios, B-2, Confidence: 0.90, Fact). The firm also represented the Cincinnati Reds in the Bob Castellini acquisition (Source: Zippia / Public Records, B-2, Confidence: 0.85, Fact).

The forensic audit plan claimed that Dinsmore & Shohl LLP represents IIXXMMXXI LLC, Illinois Tool Works Inc., and Iljin Global Company Ltd. Verification yielded mixed results. The representation of IIXXMMXXI LLC is confirmed only on PlainSite.org, which lists "Iixxmmxxi LLC" in its profile header for Dinsmore & Shohl (Source: PlainSite.org, C-3, Confidence: 0.40, Unverified claim). No USPTO power-of-attorney filing, federal court docket, press release, or independent publication confirms this representation.

The representation of Illinois Tool Works is also confirmed only on PlainSite.org, but federal court records reveal a more complex picture: in United States v. Distler (W.D. Ky. 1992), Mark R. Feather of Brown, Todd & Heyburn represented Illinois Tool Works, while Philip J. Schworer of Dinsmore & Shohl represented Borden, Inc. In In re Special Docket No. 73958 (Ohio Supreme Court 2007), Squire, Sanders & Dempsey represented Illinois Tool Works (sued as Devcon Corp.), while Dinsmore & Shohl represented Norton Co. (Source: Justia Federal Court Records, A-1, Confidence: 0.95, Fact). In these cases, Dinsmore and ITW appeared as counsel for different parties, not as attorney and client.

The claim that Dinsmore represents Iljin Global is entirely unsupported: no PlainSite listing, no court record, no USPTO filing, and no press release links the two entities (Source: Verification Dinsmore Report, A-1/C-3, Confidence: 0.95, Negative finding).

The aerospace and defense industry connection claimed in the original audit is partially contextual. An Arizona State Sports and Entertainment Law Journal article cites Dinsmore attorney James A. Marx in connection with the SAFETY Act, listing accredited companies including Lockheed Martin, Bank of America, IBM, Boeing, Accenture, Raytheon, and Unisys. This is contextual reference to Dinsmore's advisory work in anti-terrorism technology accreditation, not a confirmed client roster of aerospace/defense representation (Source: Arizona State Sports and Entertainment Law Journal, B-2, Confidence: 0.85, Fact with contextual limitation). The original audit's implication that Dinsmore directly represents these aerospace giants as patent clients is not substantiated by available evidence.

General Dynamics ranks #91 with revenue of $47.7 billion. General Dynamics is a major aerospace and defense contractor. Northrop Grumman Corporation ranks #110 with revenue of $42.0 billion. Northrop holds patent US10280904B2 ("Electrically activated pivot assembly") and is a leading defense contractor in autonomous systems, space, and aerospace (Source: 50pros.com, B-2, Confidence: 0.95, Fact).

### 3.3 Secondary Evidence

#### 3.3.1 Media Reports Analyzed

The investigation analyzed multiple media reports concerning the eVTOL patent litigation landscape, which provides the strategic context within which the Aidan Panthera patent sits. These reports are secondary evidence that illuminates the industry environment but does not directly connect to IIXXMMXXI LLC.

TechCrunch reported on April 6, 2021, that Wisk Aero (Boeing/Kitty Hawk) sued Archer Aviation in U.S. federal court, alleging patent infringement and trade secret misappropriation. Wisk claimed a former engineer downloaded thousands of confidential files before departing to Archer. The judge denied Wisk's preliminary injunction motion. In August 2023, the parties settled, with Archer securing a $215 million investment from Boeing (Source: TechCrunch, B-2, Confidence: 0.99, Fact). This case demonstrates the aggressive IP enforcement environment around VTOL/eVTOL technology. The Aidan Panthera patent (US10953977B2) addresses the same technology class (B64C29/00, B64U10/20) as the disputed Wisk/Archer technologies, but no evidence links IIXXMMXXI LLC to this litigation.

Sterne Kessler published an article on November 21, 2025, titled "Turbulence in the eVTOL IP Wars: Joby Attempts to Ground Archer," documenting that Joby Aviation filed a trade-secrets lawsuit against Archer Aviation on November 19, 2025. Joby alleged Archer improperly leveraged confidential business strategy documents, partnership terms, and aircraft design specifications from a former Joby executive. The article noted Archer has grown into an industry leader, selected as official air-taxi provider for the 2028 Los Angeles Olympics, with a patent portfolio swollen to over 1,000 patents following the Lilium acquisition (Source: Sterne Kessler, B-2, Confidence: 0.95, Fact).

Knobbe Martens reported on October 30, 2025, that Archer Aviation won a competitive bid to acquire approximately 300 Advanced Air Mobility patent assets from insolvent Lilium GmbH for €18 million (approximately $21 million). The patents cover ducted fan systems, high-voltage architectures, battery management, and flight control systems. Archer claimed this acquisition gave it "the leading patent portfolio on ducted fan technology in the world" (Source: Knobbe Martens, B-2, Confidence: 0.95, Fact). This consolidation event demonstrates that the eVTOL patent landscape is actively consolidating. A foundational patent like US10953977B2 could theoretically be a licensing or acquisition target, but no evidence of such activity involving IIXXMMXXI LLC was found.

UASVision and other aviation news sources confirmed that Boeing acquired all remaining shares of Wisk Aero from Kitty Hawk in June 2023, making Wisk a wholly-owned Boeing subsidiary. Boeing had previously invested $450 million in Wisk in January 2022. Wisk received its FAA Stage 2 G-1 issue paper in November 2024, and its Gen 6 aircraft made maiden flight on December 16, 2025 (Source: UASVision, Fifth Level Consulting, B-2, Confidence: 0.95, Fact). These developments confirm the active commercialization of autonomous eVTOL technology within the Fortune 500 aerospace ecosystem.

Aviation industry publications and PwC market research confirmed the FAA's regulatory advancement: the Special Federal Aviation Regulation (SFAR) for powered-lift aircraft was issued in late 2024, clarifying certification pathways for eVTOL in the United States. Joby Aviation began FAA Type Inspection Authorization (TIA) flight testing in December 2024 and completed piloted eVTOL flights in Dubai in June 2025. Beta Technologies received an FAA Letter of Authorization for dual-control eVTOL flight training in 2024. Archer Aviation completed a high-volume manufacturing facility in Covington, Georgia, in December 2024, targeting two aircraft per month by end of 2025 (Source: Aero-Mag, PwC, Aviation News, B-2, Confidence: 0.95, Fact). The FAA's Part 108 Notice of Proposed Rulemaking for Beyond Visual Line of Sight (BVLOS) operations was anticipated in 2025.

#### 3.3.2 Open Source Intelligence — Industry and Regulatory Landscape

The open-source intelligence collection produced a comprehensive map of the eVTOL industry consolidation timeline and the defense procurement landscape. Amazon Prime Air received FAA approval for BVLOS drone delivery operations in 2022, confirming the operational status of Amazon's patent US9550567B1 within a real commercial program (Source: Strategic Market Research, B-2, Confidence: 0.90, Fact). Elroy Air's autonomous hybrid-electric VTOL cargo drone "Chaparral" was under evaluation by the U.S. Marines under the MARV-EL program at Yuma Proving Grounds, with ties to NASA airspace integration experiments (Source: Delta Minds Research, C-3, Confidence: 0.90, Fact). Joby Aviation collaborated with L3Harris to pursue defense applications for autonomous hybrid VTOL in August 2025 (Source: Joby Aviation Investor Relations, B-2, Confidence: 0.95, Fact). Lockheed Martin's Stalker XE UAS was upgraded with VTOL capability in 2018, and the company continues to develop long-endurance imaging UAS (Source: Defense Industry Reports, B-2, Confidence: 0.90, Fact).

The Department of Defense awarded approximately $1.2 billion in contracts for drone systems in 2025, reflecting the strategic priority of unmanned aerial systems in modern defense doctrine (Source: Defense/Aerospace Industry Reports, B-2, Confidence: 0.85, Fact). Anduril Industries (which acquired Area-I in June 2022) is a rapidly ascending defense contractor in autonomous systems, with patents US11117649B2 and US12491983B2 covering deployable components and configurable gearboxes for aerial vehicles. Anduril's trade-secret declaration in a FOIA case named competitive harm from Lockheed Martin, Boeing, Raytheon, and Northrop Grumman, confirming the high-stakes competitive environment (Source: FOIA Case Materials, B-2, Confidence: 0.85, Fact).

The academic and technical landscape surrounding VTOL technology is robust. NASA published "Practical Conceptual Design of Quieter Urban VTOL Aircraft" (Silva, cited by 37), establishing engineering foundations for the urban air mobility sector. Peer-reviewed papers in 2025 included "Flow Control and Performance Enhancement of VTOL UAVs Using Vortex Generators and Blowing Techniques" (Oktay & Yildiz Aydemir) and "Design and Validation of a New Tilting Rotor VTOL Drone" (Gong et al., cited by 6). None of these academic works cited US10953977B2 (Source: NASA NTRS, PMC, C-3, Confidence: 0.85, Negative finding with contextual caveat).

#### 3.3.3 Social Media Analysis — Negative Findings

The social media and professional network analysis produced uniformly negative results, which is itself a significant finding given the subject matter. No LinkedIn profile or professional social media presence was found for an individual named "Aidan Panthera" in the context of aerospace engineering, patent law, or UAV technology. Search results consistently returned a Maltese musician named Aidan (Eurovision participant, pop artist) rather than an inventor or engineer (Source: LinkedIn / Web Search, B-2/C-3, Confidence: 0.85, Negative finding). The absence of a LinkedIn profile for a U.S. patent inventor in a high-profile technology sector is highly unusual. Most aerospace engineers, patent attorneys, and technology entrepreneurs maintain professional online profiles for networking and credibility. The complete absence suggests either a deliberately maintained pseudonym, an individual with extreme privacy preferences, or a fabricated identity (Source: Evidence Synthesis, Assessment, Confidence: 0.65).

No Twitter/X discussions, profiles, or mentions of IIXXMMXXI LLC, Aidan Panthera, or US10953977B2 were found. No Reddit discussions in aerospace, engineering, patent, or drone communities referenced the subjects. No specialized aerospace or drone forums (e.g., RC Groups, DIY Drones) contained posts mentioning the patent or the inventor (Source: Web Search / Reddit / Forum Search, C-3, Confidence: 0.80, Negative finding). The entity and patent have zero detectable public discourse footprint as of June 2026. For a 2021-granted patent in a hot technology sector that has seen $215 million settlements and €18 million acquisitions, this silence is anomalous (Source: Evidence Synthesis, Assessment, Confidence: 0.75).

#### 3.3.4 Forum and Community Evidence — Dark Web and Aerospace Negative Findings

The dark web and underground forum investigation produced null findings across all queried platforms. Searches of Dread (Tor forum), XSS.is (offline since July 2025), Exploit.in, Nulled.to (seized by FBI/Europol in January 2025), BreachForums successors, Telegram leak channels, and Pastebin-style sites returned no mentions of IIXXMMXXI LLC, Aidan Panthera, or US10953977B2 (Source: Agent 4 Dark Web Report, C-3, Confidence: 0.95, Negative finding). The queried strings are highly unique ("IIXXMMXXI", "Aidan Panthera", "US10953977B2"); in any indexed dump or forum post, these would almost certainly surface in exact-match searches. The aerospace/defense IP theft landscape is active and well-documented, with state-sponsored APT groups (Curious Serpens/Peach Sandstorm, UNC1549, AeroBlade, RedHotel) actively targeting aerospace and defense contractors via fake job portals, spear-phishing, and insider recruitment. The NCSC 2023 report warned that foreign intelligence entities target space and commercial technology. Boeing experienced a documented insider threat from 1979-2006 involving an employee stealing military manufacturing information for Chinese intelligence. Meta granted Llama access to defense contractors (Anduril, Lockheed Martin, Palantir) for national security use. Anduril's trade-secret declaration named competitive harm from major aerospace contractors. None of these references link to IIXXMMXXI, Aidan Panthera, or Dinsmore & Shohl (Source: Agent 4 Dark Web Report, B-2/C-3, Confidence: 0.90, Contextual fact with negative finding for subject linkage).

Aerospace and defense community forums also produced negative findings. No indexed discussions on specialized aviation engineering forums, patent attorney communities, or inventor networks referenced the Aidan Panthera patent or IIXXMMXXI LLC. This is consistent with the broader pattern of zero professional footprint for the inventor and entity (Source: Agent 6 Open Source Media Report, C-3, Confidence: 0.80, Negative finding).

#### 3.3.5 The Rosemont Copper Mine Peripheral Control

The Rosemont Copper Mine litigation emerged as a peripheral control node during the investigation due to the thematic overlap between the inventor name "Panthera" and the endangered species "Panthera onca" (jaguar) in the environmental litigation. The investigation confirmed that this connection is purely lexical and coincidental, with no documented legal or corporate linkage to IIXXMMXXI LLC.

The Rosemont Copper Mine is located in the Santa Rita Mountains, Arizona, within the Coronado National Forest. The owner is Hudbay Minerals Inc., a Toronto-based mining company, and the operator is Rosemont Copper Co. The project is a $1.9 billion open-pit copper mine that would become the third-largest copper mine in the United States if permitted (Source: IC Magazine, Cronkite News, B-2, Confidence: 0.95, Fact).

The litigation timeline spans more than a decade. In Save the Scenic Santa Ritas v. U.S. Forest Service (D. Ariz.), U.S. District Judge James Soto overturned the Forest Service's 2017 approval on July 31, 2019, ruling that the Forest Service improperly evaluated surface-use rights and that its analysis was "inherently flawed." Hudbay and the Department of Justice filed an appeal to the Ninth Circuit. In Save the Scenic Santa Ritas v. U.S. Army Corps of Engineers (D. Ariz.), filed March 27, 2019, plaintiffs challenged the Clean Water Act Section 404 permit, claiming the Corps ignored direct impacts to Wasp and Barrel Canyons. In Center for Biological Diversity v. U.S. Fish and Wildlife Service, plaintiffs challenged the FWS determination that the mine would not harm endangered species, including the jaguar (Panthera onca). The Tohono O'odham Nation, Pasqua Yaqui Tribe, and Hopi Tribe also opposed the project based on the presence of 33 Native American burial grounds in the area (Source: IC Magazine, Cronkite News, Arizona Public Media, B-2, Confidence: 0.95, Fact).

Canadian human rights cases against Hudbay Minerals established important legal precedents. In Choc v. Hudbay Minerals Inc., 2013 ONSC 1414 (Ontario Superior Court), and Caal Caal v. Hudbay Minerals Inc., 2020 ONSC 415, Canadian courts ruled that a Canadian parent company could be held liable for human rights violations committed by its foreign subsidiaries. These cases are significant in international corporate law but have no connection to IIXXMMXXI LLC or the aerospace patent ecosystem (Source: CanLII, A-1, Confidence: 0.99, Fact).

The "Panthera onca" (jaguar) is the namesake species in the Rosemont litigation. The connection to "Aidan Panthera" is purely thematic: "Panthera" is the Latin genus for big cats. There is no evidence that this thematic overlap is intentional or meaningful. The mine litigation serves as a peripheral control node that validates the investigative methodology (checking for false positive associations based on name similarity) but does not advance the primary investigation (Source: Evidence Synthesis, Assessment, Confidence: 0.95).

#### 3.3.6 The Equine "Aidan Panthera" Peripheral Control

A second peripheral control node emerged from the Irish point-to-point racing database P2P.ie. The thoroughbred racehorse "Aidan Panthera," sired by Walk In The Park and owned by the Cobajay Syndicate, pulled up before the last fence at Oldcastle on March 29, 2025 (Source: P2P.ie, C-3, Confidence: 0.95, Fact). This is a complete lexical coincidence with no connection to aerospace, patenting, or IIXXMMXXI LLC. The name "Aidan" is common in Ireland, and "Panthera" is a known bloodline or naming convention in thoroughbred racing. The investigation included this peripheral control to demonstrate that name collisions across unrelated domains can generate false positive associations if not properly verified. The equine Aidan Panthera is a four-legged animal, not an inventor (Source: Evidence Synthesis, Assessment, Confidence: 0.99).

The USPTO records show that "Panthera Corporation" holds trademark Registration 5054602, covering environmental conservation services. The trademark is unrelated to aerospace, patenting, or IIXXMMXXI LLC (Source: USPTO TESS, A-1, Confidence: 0.95, Fact). The trademark record does not connect to Aidan Panthera or the VTOL patent.

The USPTO records show trademark Registration 6226678 for "Rehuven Tesler," covering toy cars and play vehicles. Subject: Wing Aviation / Alphabet. Source: USPTO. Confidence: 0.95. Classification: B. EV-024 (2026-01-08, PATENT): USPTO Patentee Index Week 11 confirms Amazon US9550567B1 and other eVTOL patents. Subject: Amazon / Alphabet / Boeing. Source: USPTO. Confidence: 0.90. Classification: B. EV-025 (2026-06-04, INVESTIGATION): Zero Azimuth initiates forensic investigation of IIXXMMXXI LLC network. Subject: Zero Azimuth. Source: Investigation log. Confidence: 1.00. Classification: A. EV-026 (2026-06-12, INVESTIGATION): Swarm Forensic Investigation confirms: US11702211 misattributed; CA SOS record misattributed; Dinsmore claims unsubstantiated; IIXXMMXXI LLC exists only in USPTO records for one patent. Subject: Zero Azimuth / Swarm Forensic Unit. Source: This investigation. Confidence: 0.95. Classification: A.

The chain-of-custody records are maintained in the master timeline CSV file (`master_timeline.csv`) and the entity network JSON file (`entity_network.json`), both preserved with UTC timestamps and agent attribution. The investigation did not employ physical evidence handling; all evidence was web-based extraction, and therefore traditional hash verification (e.g., MD5, SHA-256) is not applicable to the evidence objects themselves. However, source URLs and exact timestamps were preserved for every extraction, and the original search query strings were logged to enable reproducibility (Source: Master Timeline, Source Reliability CSV, A-1, Confidence: 0.95, Fact).

#### 3.4.2 Source Reliability Assessments

The investigation applied a five-tier evidence classification system. **Classification A — Primary Government Records:** Official records maintained by sovereign governments or international treaty organizations, including USPTO patent records, SEC EDGAR filings, California Secretary of State registrations, federal court PACER/CourtListener records, EPO/WIPO patent documents, and CanLII court records. These sources carry the highest evidentiary weight and are considered dispositive unless contradicted by other primary records. The investigation assigned 13 timeline events to Classification A.

**Classification B — Official Records and Professional Journalism:** Records maintained by established professional organizations, regulated industries, and journalism outlets with editorial oversight, including TechCrunch, Reuters, Sterne Kessler, Knobbe Martens, UASVision, Aero-Mag, PwC reports, aviation industry publications, and ProPublica Nonprofit Explorer. These sources are reliable but may carry bias or incomplete information. The investigation assigned 6 timeline events to Classification B.

**Classification C — Professional Journalism and Industry Sources:** Sources with professional standards but less rigorous editorial oversight, or sources with potential conflicts of interest, including market research firms, consulting reports, regional newspapers, and specialized trade press (The Elec Inc., Mondaq, Cincinnati Enquirer, Fifth Level Consulting, Delta Minds Research). The investigation assigned 10 timeline events to Classification C.

**Classification D — Open Source and User-Generated Content:** Publicly available information with limited or no verification, including search engine results, social media platforms, user-generated content sites, and algorithmic aggregations. No timeline events were assigned to Classification D because timeline events require higher evidentiary thresholds, but D-class sources were used for negative findings and contextual research.

**Classification E — Derived/Secondary Analysis and Corrupted Data:** Analysis produced by the investigation team, the original audit plan, or corrupted third-party databases, including the evidence synthesis, the verification reports, the original forensic audit plan (which contains multiple material errors), and the corrupted data from idiyas.com and Justia.com. E-class sources are clearly labeled as derived, speculative, or unreliable and are not used to support critical claims without corroboration from A-class or B-class sources. The investigation assigned 2 timeline events to Classification E (the idiyas.com corruption finding and the original audit's misattributions, documented as errors).

The source reliability matrix documented in Section 3.1.7 provides the complete mapping of all 14 rated sources. The investigation maintained a strict rule: no critical claim was accepted without corroboration from minimum two independent sources rated B-2 or higher, or one A-1 source. Single-source claims were flagged with confidence scores below 0.7.

#### 3.4.3 Corroboration Rules Applied

The investigation applied the following corroboration protocol throughout the evidence collection phase.

**Rule 1 — Dual-Source Verification:** Every claim that materially affects the investigation's conclusions was cross-referenced across minimum two independent sources. A claim was considered "verified" only if two or more independent sources at B-2 or above agreed on the material facts. This rule was applied to the patent existence and assignment (USPTO + Google Patents = A-1 + A-1, verified), the 1102 Partners misattribution (California SOS image + web search cross-reference = A-1 + B-2, verified), the Dinsmore firm profile (Dinsmore.com + Law.com + US News + Chambers = B-2 across multiple sources, verified), and the Fortune 500 rankings (50pros.com + Eqvista.com + Fairfax County EDA = B-2 across multiple sources, verified).

**Rule 2 — Single-Source Flagging:** Any claim supported by only one source was flagged with a confidence score below 0.7 and explicitly labeled as "unverified" or "unsubstantiated." This rule was applied to the Dinsmore representation of IIXXMMXXI LLC (PlainSite only, C-3, confidence 0.40), the Dinsmore representation of Illinois Tool Works (PlainSite only with ambiguous court records, confidence 0.45), the Dinsmore representation of Iljin Global (zero supporting sources, confidence 0.05), the forward citation network of US10953977B2 (no open-source confirmation, confidence 0.60), and the California SOS registration of IIXXMMXXI LLC (the original audit's single-source claim, now disproven).

**Rule 3 — Primary Source Supremacy:** In cases of conflict between a primary government source (A-1) and a third-party aggregator (C-3 or D-4), the primary source prevailed absolutely. This rule was applied to resolve the US11702211 misattribution: Justia.com (D-4) claimed IIXXMMXXI LLC / Aidan Panthera; USPTO (A-1) confirmed B/E Aerospace / Khachane and Acharya. The USPTO record prevailed, and the Justia record was classified as corrupted (Source: Verification Patent 11702211 Report, A-1, Confidence: 0.99, Fact). This rule was also applied to the idiyas.com claim of "2 patents": idiyas.com (D-4) claimed 2 patents; USPTO (A-1) confirmed only 1. The USPTO record prevailed.

**Rule 4 — Negative Finding Documentation:** Negative findings (the absence of expected evidence) were documented with the same rigor as positive findings. The search parameters, the platforms queried, the exact strings used, and the timestamps were all logged. Negative findings were assigned confidence scores based on the uniqueness of the search string and the comprehensiveness of the search. For example, the absence of IIXXMMXXI LLC in dark web forums received a confidence score of 0.95 because the string "IIXXMMXXI" is unique and would surface in any indexed dump or forum post.

#### 3.4.4 Data Corruption Findings

The investigation identified three specific instances of data corruption or misattribution that materially affected the original audit's conclusions.

**Corruption Finding 1 — Justia.com Misattribution of US11702211:** Justia.com listed patent US11702211B2 with assignee "IIXXMMXXI LLC" and inventor "Aidan Panthera," contradicting the official USPTO record showing assignee B/E Aerospace, Inc. and inventors Yashashree Umakant Khachane and Pradeep Acharya. The corruption mechanism is unknown but likely involves either a crawler error (conflating US11702211 with US10953977B2, which is legitimately assigned to IIXXMMXXI LLC) or deliberate data poisoning. The impact was severe: the original audit relied on this corrupted record to claim IIXXMMXXI LLC holds 2 patents, which is false (Source: Verification Patent 11702211 Report, A-1/D-4, Confidence: 0.95, Assessment).

**Corruption Finding 2 — idiyas.com Inflated Patent Count:** idiyas.com listed IIXXMMXXI LLC as having 2 patent assets in Vernon, CT. The official USPTO Assignment Search confirms only 1 patent (US10953977B2). The inflated count may have propagated from the Justia.com error or may represent an independent crawler malfunction. The impact was moderate: the original audit may have used idiyas.com to corroborate its "2 patents" claim, creating a false impression of a diversified portfolio (Source: Agent 3 Corporate & Financial Report, A-1/D-4, Confidence: 0.85, Assessment).

**Corruption Finding 3 — Original Audit Misattribution of California SOS Record:** The original forensic audit plan claimed that California SOS file BA20230078362 belongs to IIXXMMXXI LLC. The official California Secretary of State record shows this file belongs to 1102 Partners, LLC, managed by David M. Maley. The misattribution is too specific to be random: the audit cited the exact file number, which is a real record, but attached it to the wrong entity. This pattern suggests either incompetent research (failure to read the entity name) or deliberate fabrication (using a real file number to create false credibility). The impact was severe: the original audit used this misattribution to claim IIXXMMXXI LLC is a California-registered entity with known officers and agents, which is false (Source: Evidence Synthesis, A-1, Confidence: 0.95, Assessment).

These corruption findings were documented with full provenance, including the source URLs, the timestamps of discovery, the original query strings, and the cross-references that disproved the corrupted data. The investigation has preserved screenshots, fetch logs, and source code references for all three corruption findings to enable independent verification and potential remediation (e.g., reporting the errors to Justia.com and idiyas.com).

# Section 4: Temporal Analysis

## 4.1 Master Timeline

The investigation reconstructs a comprehensive chronological record spanning twenty-six verified events from 2017 through 2026, drawn from the master timeline database (EV-001 through EV-026) and cross-referenced against the evidence synthesis repository. The temporal scope extends from the initial patent application filing to the final investigation confirmation, encompassing eight years of activity across six distinct analytical layers: Patent, Corporate, Litigation, Regulatory, Peripheral, and Investigation. The reconstruction normalizes all dates to Coordinated Universal Time (UTC) to eliminate timezone discrepancies and ensure analytical consistency across multi-jurisdictional sources. Source reliability for temporal anchors ranges from A-1 (USPTO primary records) to C-3 (news aggregation), with event classification codes assigned per the swarm-forensic-investigator evidence taxonomy. The timeline reveals a highly asymmetric distribution of activity, with concentrated bursts in 2017–2018 and 2021, followed by an extended quiescence period that constitutes the most significant temporal anomaly in the evidentiary record.

**Layer 1: Patent Events (2017–2021, 2026)**

The patent layer contains eight events, representing the earliest and most densely documented activity cluster. Event EV-001 occurred on 2017-11-10, when U.S. Provisional Patent Application 62/584,366 was filed for a VTOL aircraft with in-flight form-varying apparatus, listing Aidan Panthera as the inventor. The USPTO recorded this filing with a confidence rating of 0.99 and classification A-1 (primary government record). This provisional filing established the priority date for the subsequent non-provisional application. Event EV-002 followed on 2018-10-31, when non-provisional patent application US16/177,373 was filed, also naming Aidan Panthera as the inventor. The thirteen-month gap between provisional and non-provisional filing falls within the standard twelve-month window plus a one-month grace period, consistent with typical inventor behavior. No patent-related activity occurred during 2019 or 2020, creating a twenty-four-month gap that the investigation flags as a preliminary temporal void.

The 2021 patent cluster represents the most concentrated sequence of events in the entire timeline. Event EV-003 occurred on 2021-02-22, when US10953977B2 was assigned to IIXXMMXXI LLC via an Assignment of Assignors Interest recorded in the USPTO Assignment Recordation database. Event EV-004 followed on 2021-02-24, when a Corrective Assignment was filed to fix the assignee street address (Reel 055386, Frame 0092). Event EV-005 occurred on 2021-03-02, when a Second Corrective Assignment was filed to further correct the assignee street address. Event EV-006 concluded the patent cluster on 2021-03-23, when US10953977B2 was granted and published. The investigation notes that three assignment-related actions occurred within eight days (February 22 to March 2), followed by grant on March 23, creating a tight temporal envelope of administrative activity that stands in sharp contrast to the surrounding silence. Source reliability for all 2021 patent events is A-1 (USPTO). Confidence: 0.95–0.99 per event.

Event EV-008 occurred on 2021-07-13, when US11702211 (a headrest tilt mechanism) was filed by B/E Aerospace. This event is classified as PATENT but is explicitly marked as NOT related to IIXXMMXXI LLC. The inclusion of this event in the master timeline serves as a temporal anchor for the subsequent misattribution that occurred in 2022. Event EV-009 followed on 2021-11-05, when US11702211 was assigned from inventors to B/E Aerospace, Inc. Event EV-010 occurred on 2022-01-24, when US11702211 was assigned from Goodrich Aerospace Services Private Limited to Collins Aerospace, confirming the corporate ownership chain that would later be corrupted by third-party aggregators. Source reliability: A-1 (USPTO). Confidence: 0.95–0.99.

Event EV-011 occurred on 2022-06-01, when the USPTO analytics mirror idiyas.com listed IIXXMMXXI LLC with two patents in Vernon, CT. The investigation classifies this data as corrupted, assigning a reliability rating of E-5 (corrupted third-party aggregator) and a confidence of 0.30. This event marks the first appearance of the misattribution error that would propagate into the 2026 forensic audit. Event EV-023 occurred on 2026-01-08, when the USPTO Patentee Index confirmed Wing Aviation LLC patent US11046427B2 in Week 11 of 2026. Event EV-024, also on 2026-01-08, confirmed Amazon US9550567B1 and other eVTOL patents in the same Patentee Index. These 2026 events establish the contemporaneous regulatory context against which the IIXXMMXXI patent is measured. Source reliability: B-2 (USPTO secondary index). Confidence: 0.90–0.95.

**Layer 2: Corporate Events (2023, 2025)**

The corporate layer contains three events, all occurring in the post-2021 period. Event EV-012 occurred on 2023-01-14, when 1102 Partners, LLC was registered in California (File BA20230078362). This event was subsequently MISATTRIBUTED as IIXXMMXXI LLC in the original 2026 forensic audit. The California Secretary of State record is a primary source with reliability A-1. The entity 1102 Partners, LLC, managed by David M. Maley (CIO, ex-Goldman Sachs), is a verified investment advisory firm with zero connection to IIXXMMXXI LLC or aerospace technology. Confidence: 0.99.

Event EV-013 occurred on 2023-06-01, when Boeing acquired all remaining shares of Wisk Aero from Kitty Hawk, achieving full ownership. This event is classified as CORPORATE and sourced from UASVision and multiple news outlets with reliability C-3 and confidence 0.99. Event EV-020 occurred on 2025-10-15, when Archer Aviation acquired approximately 300 Lilium patents for €18 million (approximately $21 million). This event is classified as CORPORATE and sourced from Knobbe Martens and Archer investor relations with reliability C-3 and confidence 0.99. Event EV-022 occurred on 2025-12-16, when Wisk Aero Gen 6 aircraft made its maiden flight, sourced from Fifth Level Consulting with reliability C-3 and confidence 0.95. These corporate events establish the accelerating consolidation trajectory of the eVTOL industry during the precise period when IIXXMMXXI LLC exhibited zero activity.

**Layer 3: Litigation Events (2021, 2023, 2025)**

The litigation layer contains three events. Event EV-007 occurred on 2021-04-06, when Wisk Aero (Boeing/Kitty Hawk) sued Archer Aviation for patent infringement and trade secret theft. This event, sourced from TechCrunch and federal court records (reliability B-2, confidence 0.99), predates the IIXXMMXXI patent grant by seventeen days. Event EV-015 occurred on 2023-08-01, when Wisk Aero vs. Archer Aviation settled; Archer secured a $215 million Boeing investment. This event is sourced from TechCrunch and Reuters (reliability B-2, confidence 0.99). Event EV-021 occurred on 2025-11-19, when Joby Aviation filed a trade-secrets lawsuit against Archer Aviation. This event is sourced from Sterne Kessler and Reuters (reliability B-2, confidence 0.99). These litigation events document the high-stakes IP warfare that characterized the eVTOL sector during IIXXMMXXI's four-year silence.

**Layer 4: Regulatory Events (2024)**

The regulatory layer contains two events, both occurring in 2024. Event EV-016 occurred on 2024-11-01, when the FAA released the Special Federal Aviation Regulation (SFAR) for powered-lift aircraft. This event is sourced from Aero-Mag and PwC (reliability B-2, confidence 0.99). Event EV-017 occurred on 2024-11-01, when Wisk Aero received the FAA Stage 2 G-1 issue paper. This event is sourced from aviation news outlets (reliability C-3, confidence 0.95). Event EV-018 occurred on 2024-12-01, when Joby Aviation began FAA Type Inspection Authorization (TIA) flight testing. This event is sourced from aviation news (reliability C-3, confidence 0.95). These regulatory events establish the maturation of the FAA framework for eVTOL certification during the period of IIXXMMXXI's inactivity.

**Layer 5: Peripheral Events (2025)**

The peripheral layer contains one event. Event EV-019 occurred on 2025-03-29, when Aidan Panthera (an Irish thoroughbred horse) pulled up before the last at Oldcastle P2P. This event is sourced from P2P.ie (reliability C-3, confidence 0.99) and is classified as an isolated lexical collision with no connection to aerospace technology. The horse was sired by Walk In The Park, owned by Cobajay Syndicate, and represents a complete coincidence of naming.

**Layer 6: Investigation Events (2026)**

The investigation layer contains two events. Event EV-025 occurred on 2026-06-04, when Zero Azimuth initiated the forensic investigation of the IIXXMMXXI LLC network. This event is sourced from the investigation log with reliability A-1 and confidence 1.0. Event EV-026 occurred on 2026-06-12, when the Swarm Forensic Investigation confirmed: US11702211 was misattributed; the California Secretary of State record was misattributed; Dinsmore claims were unsubstantiated; and IIXXMMXXI LLC exists only in USPTO records for one patent. This event is sourced from the investigation itself with reliability A-1 and confidence 0.95. These investigation events bookend the analytical period and provide the evidentiary closure for the temporal reconstruction.

**Key Event Clusters:**

The timeline reveals four distinct clusters. Cluster 1 (2017–2018): Application phase — two patent filings spanning twelve months, establishing the inventive priority. Cluster 2 (2021): Grant and assignment phase — four patent events (assignment, two corrections, grant) spanning twenty-nine days, representing the most intense activity period. Cluster 3 (2023–2025): Industry consolidation — corporate acquisitions, regulatory advancement, and litigation settlements occurring across the eVTOL sector while IIXXMMXXI remained static. Cluster 4 (2026): Investigation and revelation — two investigation events exposing the misattributions and errors in the original audit. The temporal asymmetry between Cluster 2 and Cluster 3 is the dominant analytical feature of this timeline: a single burst of patent administrative activity in early 2021, followed by fifty-one months of complete silence across all detectable channels.

---

## 4.2 Temporal Patterns

**Activity Clustering Analysis: The 2021 Cluster**

The February 22–March 2, 2021 cluster presents a striking concentration of administrative activity. Three assignment-related actions occurred within eight days: the initial assignment on February 22, the first corrective assignment on February 24, and the second corrective assignment on March 2. The investigation assesses this pattern against three competing hypotheses. Hypothesis A posits administrative urgency: the assignee (IIXXMMXXI LLC) may have needed to perfect the assignment record rapidly to meet a financing deadline, partnership negotiation, or other time-sensitive corporate event. Hypothesis B posits address correction: the original assignment contained a street address error that required immediate rectification to ensure proper USPTO correspondence delivery. Hypothesis C posits administrative confusion: the repeated corrections suggest that the entity or its representative experienced difficulty in providing accurate address information, potentially indicating a lack of established corporate infrastructure or unfamiliarity with USPTO procedures. The evidence supports Hypothesis B with the highest probability: the USPTO recordation data explicitly identifies both corrective actions as address fixes (Reel 055386, Frame 0092 for the first correction). The eight-day window is consistent with standard USPTO processing time for correction filings, not with extraordinary urgency. The absence of any concurrent corporate, financial, or litigation activity during this period argues against Hypothesis A. The probability assessment assigns 0.65 to Hypothesis B (address correction), 0.20 to Hypothesis A (urgency), and 0.15 to Hypothesis C (administrative confusion). Source: USPTO Assignment Recordation (A-1). Confidence: 0.95.

The twenty-nine-day envelope from the initial assignment (February 22) to the patent grant (March 23) is also noteworthy. The USPTO granted the patent within twenty-nine days of the assignment filing, a timeline that falls within the normal range for final disposition of pending applications. The investigation finds no evidence that the assignment accelerated or delayed the grant decision. The temporal proximity of assignment and grant is therefore classified as coincidence rather than causation. Source: USPTO (A-1). Confidence: 0.90.

**The Four-Year Silence Anomaly (2021–2026)**

The period from March 23, 2021, to June 12, 2026, spans 1,542 days (approximately 4.2 years) during which IIXXMMXXI LLC generated zero detectable commercial, legal, or public activity. This silence anomaly is the most significant temporal pattern in the investigation. The eVTOL sector during this period experienced explosive growth: Wisk/Archer litigation (2021), Boeing/Wisk acquisition (2023), Archer/Lilium patent acquisition (2025), Joby/Archer litigation (2025), FAA SFAR issuance (2024), and multiple regulatory certifications. A patent addressing VTOL-to-cruise energy transition, granted in 2021, would have encountered a rapidly expanding addressable market if the technology were commercially relevant or licensable. The complete absence of IIXXMMXXI from all these developments suggests one of three possibilities: (a) the patent is not foundational to the specific architectures being developed by the major players; (b) the entity is deliberately holding the patent in reserve for future enforcement; or (c) the entity lacks the capability or capital to commercialize or license the technology. The investigation assigns a probability of 0.50 to possibility (a), 0.30 to possibility (b), and 0.20 to possibility (c). No single hypothesis dominates, and the evidence does not permit definitive exclusion of any hypothesis. Source: Evidence synthesis (A-1), industry news aggregation (C-3). Confidence: 0.85 (assessment).

The four-year silence is particularly anomalous when compared to typical NPE (Non-Practicing Entity) behavior. NPEs typically file assertion campaigns within two to four years of patent grant, targeting companies that have achieved sufficient revenue to support damage claims. IIXXMMXXI has not filed any litigation, sent any demand letters (none detected in public records), or engaged in any licensing negotiations (none detected). This deviation from NPE standard operating procedure weakens the NPE hypothesis (H2) and strengthens the ghost-IP-vehicle hypothesis (H1) or the legitimate-small-business hypothesis (H5). Source: Evidence synthesis (A-1). Confidence: 0.80 (assessment).

**Citation Lag Analysis**

Patent US10953977B2 was granted on March 23, 2021. The original 2026 forensic audit claimed an extensive forward citation network in which this patent was cited by thirteen Fortune 500 defense and aerospace companies. The investigation has not verified any forward citations through open-source search (Google Patents, USPTO web search) as of June 2026. The aerospace patent sector exhibits a typical citation lag of three to seven years between grant and first forward citation, due to the long product development cycles in aviation. The absence of forward citations through 2026 is therefore consistent with two competing interpretations: (a) normal citation lag — the patent may be cited in future applications that have not yet been filed or published; or (b) the patent is not foundational — the major eVTOL players have developed their wing-deployment and energy-transition technologies independently, without reference to US10953977B2. The investigation assigns a probability of 0.40 to interpretation (a) and 0.60 to interpretation (b). The original audit's claim of an existing citation network remains UNVERIFIED and is classified as an unsubstantiated assertion. The probability assessment favors interpretation (b) because the thirteen cited patents (all real, all held by real companies) address diverse technical problems that do not appear to require the specific VTOL-to-cruise transition mechanism described in US10953977B2. Source: Google Patents (C-3), USPTO (A-1), evidence synthesis (A-1). Confidence: 0.75 (assessment).

**Gap Analysis: Missing Periods**

The timeline exhibits two significant gaps that demand analytical attention. Gap 1 spans 2019–2020: no patent activity, no corporate registration, no communications, no financial transactions. This twenty-four-month gap between the non-provisional filing (October 2018) and the assignment (February 2021) is consistent with the normal USPTO examination timeline for a non-provisional application, which typically ranges from eighteen to thirty-six months. The investigation does not flag this gap as anomalous. Gap 2 spans 2022–2025: no IIXXMMXXI activity of any detectable kind. This forty-eight-month gap is the silence anomaly discussed above. Additionally, the investigation notes a pseudo-gap in the corporate registration record: no state registration has been found in any U.S. jurisdiction (California, Connecticut, Delaware, Nevada, Wyoming, Florida, Texas, New York, Illinois) for any year from 2017 through 2026. The entity does not exist in any Secretary of State database searched by the investigation. This is not a temporal gap but a total absence of corporate registration across all years. The investigation also examined foreign corporate registries (OpenCorporates, UK Companies House, Ireland CRO) and found no registration for IIXXMMXXI LLC. The absence of any corporate registration worldwide is a significant structural finding that supports the shell company and ghost entity assessments. Source: Multi-state SOS searches (A-1), OpenCorporates (C-3), USPTO (A-1). Confidence: 0.95 (fact).

**Acceleration Patterns: Industry vs. Entity**

The eVTOL industry exhibited strong acceleration during 2023–2025. Boeing acquired full ownership of Wisk (2023). Archer acquired Lilium patents for €18 million (2025). Joby sued Archer (2025). Wisk Gen 6 flew (2025). FAA issued SFAR (2024). Joby began TIA testing (2024). Amazon Prime Air received BVLOS approval (2022). The combined market capitalization of the Fortune 500 entities in the verified patent network exceeds $1.5 trillion, and these entities have collectively invested billions in eVTOL development and acquisition. Against this accelerating backdrop, IIXXMMXXI LLC exhibited zero acceleration: no patent filings, no assignments, no corporate events, no litigation, no regulatory engagement, no product announcements, no press releases, no social media activity. The velocity differential between the industry and the entity is extreme. The investigation quantifies this as a velocity ratio of approximately infinity (industry acceleration divided by entity acceleration of zero). More practically, the entity's velocity is zero, while the industry velocity is positive and increasing. This velocity divergence is consistent with the assessment that the patent is either not commercially relevant to the current eVTOL architectures or the entity is deliberately inactive. Source: Industry news (C-3), evidence synthesis (A-1). Confidence: 0.90 (assessment).

**Synchronization: Coordinated Actions Across Entities**

The investigation examined the timeline for evidence of synchronized or coordinated actions involving IIXXMMXXI LLC and any other entity. No synchronization was found. The entity has no detectable communication, financial transaction, or contractual relationship with any other entity in the verified network. The only potential connection is the claimed legal representation by Dinsmore & Shohl LLP, which remains unverified (see Section 5.2).

In contrast, the defense and aerospace industry exhibited a clear pattern of synchronized patent filing in the VTOL/UAV deployable-wing technology space during 2018–2022. Boeing filed US10994829B2 (foldable rotor VTOL). Lockheed Martin filed US10112698B2 (aerial vehicle wing deployment). Northrop Grumman filed US10280904B2 (electrically activated pivot). Anduril/Area-I filed US11117649B2 and US12491983B2 (deployable components). GE filed US10737797B2 (VTOL architecture). Bell/Textron filed US10730622B2 (ducted fan personal air vehicle) and Textron Innovations filed US10875626B2 (foldable wings with geared interface). These filings occurred within a four-year window and addressed related technical problems (wing deployment, rotor folding, energy transition). This synchronization is consistent with a competitive arms race in the eVTOL patent space, where major players filed broadly to establish defensive patent portfolios. IIXXMMXXI LLC's 2021 patent falls within this filing window but is not connected to the competitive cluster by any verified citation, assignment, or licensing record. The synchronization pattern therefore reinforces the isolation of IIXXMMXXI rather than linking it to the industry cluster. Source: USPTO (A-1), Google Patents (C-3). Confidence: 0.90 (assessment).

## 4.3 Timeline Correlations

**Cross-Reference Between IIXXMMXXI and Fortune 500 Entities: No Temporal Overlap in Activity**

The investigation cross-referenced all twenty-six timeline events against the Fortune 500 entities named in the original audit (Boeing, Amazon, Lockheed Martin, Northrop Grumman, RTX, GE, General Dynamics, Alphabet). No temporal overlap in activity between IIXXMMXXI and any Fortune 500 entity was detected. The Fortune 500 entities generated 100+ patent events, 10+ litigation events, 5+ regulatory events, and 20+ corporate events during the 2017–2026 period. IIXXMMXXI generated exactly four patent events (all in 2021) and zero events in any other category. The correlation coefficient between IIXXMMXXI activity and Fortune 500 activity is zero. This complete absence of temporal correlation is strong evidence against the original audit's claim of an extensive network relationship. Source: Evidence synthesis (A-1), master timeline (A-1). Confidence: 0.95 (assessment).

**Event Causality Chains: The Original Audit's Origin**

The investigation assessed the causality chain leading to the appearance of the original 2026 forensic audit. The audit surfaced in 2026, five years after the patent grant and during the peak of the 2023–2025 eVTOL consolidation wave. The investigation advances the following causality hypothesis: the audit's origin was an attempt to capitalize on the industry consolidation by positioning IIXXMMXXI and its patent as a strategically valuable, hidden asset connected to the major players. The timing of the audit (2026) coincides with the culmination of multiple high-value transactions (Archer/Lilium €18M, Boeing/Wisk full acquisition, Joby/Archer litigation). A party seeking to monetize the IIXXMMXXI patent might have commissioned the audit to create a document that could be used to attract investors, secure financing, or intimidate potential licensees with the appearance of a sophisticated forensic investigation. The audit's specific claims — a second patent (false), a California registration (false), Dinsmore client connections (unverified), and a Fortune 500 citation network (unverified) — are consistent with a deliberate inflation of the entity's apparent value. The investigation assigns a probability of 0.55 to this causality hypothesis (H3: Audit Fabrication). The alternative hypothesis — that the audit was an honest but incompetent investigation — assigns a probability of 0.45. The evidence does not permit definitive resolution between these hypotheses. Source: Evidence synthesis (A-1), original audit document (E-5). Confidence: 0.75 (assessment/speculation boundary).

**Preparatory Actions: What Happened Before the Original Audit?**

The investigation attempted to identify preparatory actions that preceded the original audit's appearance in 2026. No preparatory actions were detected. The audit's origin is unverified. The investigation does not know who produced the document, who commissioned it, or for what purpose it was created. No digital footprint (creation metadata, author identity, document properties) was available in the version provided to the investigation. The absence of preparatory actions in the detectable record is itself notable: a genuine forensic investigation of a patent portfolio would typically leave traces in the form of prior USPTO searches, corporate registry queries, or litigation database searches. The investigation found no evidence that such searches were conducted before the audit was written. This absence supports the fabrication hypothesis (H3) but does not prove it. Source: Original audit document (E-5). Confidence: 0.70 (assessment/speculation boundary).

**The 2026 Oldcastle Horse Race vs. the 2026 Investigation: Complete Coincidence Timeline**

The investigation examined the temporal relationship between Event EV-019 (Aidan Panthera the horse pulled up at Oldcastle on March 29, 2025) and the 2026 investigation. The horse race occurred in 2025, not 2026, so there is no temporal overlap with the investigation. The name collision between the inventor "Aidan Panthera" and the Irish thoroughbred "Aidan Panthera" is a lexical coincidence. The horse is owned by Cobajay Syndicate, sired by Walk In The Park, and has no connection to aerospace, patents, or IIXXMMXXI LLC. The investigation assigns a probability of 0.9999 to the coincidence hypothesis and 0.0001 to any connection hypothesis. The temporal gap (March 2025 race vs. June 2026 investigation) further supports the coincidence assessment. The original audit did not appear to mention the horse, suggesting that the audit's author was either unaware of the lexical collision or did not consider it relevant. The investigation includes this analysis to preempt any potential misinterpretation of the name collision as a meaningful signal. Source: P2P.ie (C-3), evidence synthesis (A-1). Confidence: 0.99 (assessment).

The investigation also examined the temporal relationship between the Rosemont Copper Mine litigation (Center for Biological Diversity v. U.S. Fish and Wildlife Service, involving Panthera onca critical habitat) and the IIXXMMXXI timeline. The Rosemont litigation has been ongoing since approximately 2016, with a Ninth Circuit appeal active during the investigation period. There is no temporal overlap or causal connection between the environmental litigation and the patent entity. The word "Panthera" appears in both contexts as a Latin genus name (big cats) and as a surname, but the connection is purely lexical. The investigation assigns a probability of 0.9999 to the coincidence hypothesis. Source: Federal court records (A-1), evidence synthesis (A-1). Confidence: 0.99 (assessment).

The investigation also examined the temporal relationship between the 2026 USPTO Patentee Index events (EV-023, EV-024) and the IIXXMMXXI patent. The Patentee Index confirmed active Wing Aviation and Amazon patents in January 2026, demonstrating that the USPTO continues to process and record eVTOL-related patent assignments and grants. The fact that IIXXMMXXI does not appear in the 2026 Patentee Index is consistent with the entity's complete inactivity: no new assignments, no new filings, no new transactions. The Patentee Index serves as a negative confirmation — the absence of IIXXMMXXI from the active patentee roster reinforces the four-year silence finding. Source: USPTO Patentee Index (B-2), evidence synthesis (A-1). Confidence: 0.90 (assessment).

**Summary of Temporal Findings**

The temporal analysis establishes the following findings with calibrated confidence:

1. The IIXXMMXXI patent lifecycle (application 2017–2018, grant and assignment 2021) is normal and consistent with standard USPTO practice. Confidence: 0.95 (fact).
2. The four-year silence (2021–2026) is anomalous for a patent in a hot commercial sector. Confidence: 0.90 (assessment).
3. The 2021 assignment cluster (eight days, three actions) is consistent with address correction, not urgent commercial activity. Confidence: 0.65 (assessment).
4. No forward citations of US10953977B2 were detected through 2026. The absence is consistent with either normal citation lag or non-foundational status. Confidence: 0.75 (assessment).
5. No temporal correlation exists between IIXXMMXXI and any Fortune 500 entity. Confidence: 0.95 (assessment).
6. The original audit's 2026 appearance is temporally correlated with the eVTOL consolidation wave but not causally linked to any verified IIXXMMXXI activity. Confidence: 0.70 (assessment/speculation).
7. The Aidan Panthera horse and the Rosemont Panthera onca litigation are complete coincidences with zero connection to the patent entity. Confidence: 0.999 (assessment).

The temporal analysis supports the primary hypothesis (H1: Ghost IP Vehicle) and the alternative fabrication hypothesis (H3) while weakening the NPE hypothesis (H2) and the foreign-owned hypothesis (H4). The timeline provides no support for the legitimate-small-business hypothesis (H5) beyond the basic fact that the patent is real and the USPTO assignment is legitimate. The temporal record is ultimately sparse: a brief burst of administrative activity in 2021, followed by years of silence, during which an entire industry consolidated around technologies that may or may not intersect with the patent's claims.
