# Section 5: Network and Relationship Analysis

## 5.1 Entity Relationship Graph

The investigation constructed a complete network graph from the entity_network.json evidence repository, comprising forty-two nodes and thirty-seven edges. This graph represents the full universe of entities that have been identified, verified, or disproven during the course of the investigation. The network topology reveals a starkly bifurcated structure: one small, isolated dyad at the center of the inquiry (IIXXMMXXI LLC and Aidan Panthera), one large, dense competitive cluster of Fortune 500 defense and aerospace companies, and a third group of peripheral isolates that connect to neither of the primary clusters. The network analysis proceeds from node classification through edge typology to centrality metrics and community detection, with each analytical step calibrated to the reliability of the underlying evidence.

**Node Classification and Counts**

The forty-two nodes are classified into six primary types based on the entity_taxonomy applied by the swarm-forensic-investigator methodology. Companies constitute the largest category with twenty-eight nodes (66.7%), reflecting the corporate-heavy nature of the patent and aerospace landscape. Persons comprise four nodes (9.5%): Aidan Panthera (inventor), David M. Maley (investment professional), Michael Maley (agent for service), and Aidan Fitzgerald (horse handler). Law_Firms comprise one node (2.4%): Dinsmore & Shohl LLP. Nonprofits comprise two nodes (4.8%): the Edward L. Hutton Foundation and the Richard H. & Ann Shafer Foundation. Organizations comprise four nodes (9.5%): the Center for Biological Diversity, the U.S. Fish and Wildlife Service, the Panthera Corporation, and the Cobajay Syndicate (classified as equine ownership organization). The residual category of Other is not used in the primary taxonomy; the investigation maps all nodes to the six main types.

The twenty-eight Company nodes are further segmented by relevance to the core inquiry. Core nodes (directly connected to IIXXMMXXI in the claimed network): IIXXMMXXI LLC (N-001), Boeing (N-008), Amazon (N-009), Lockheed Martin (N-010), Northrop Grumman (N-011), RTX (N-012), GE (N-013), Alphabet/Wing (N-014), Airbus (N-015), Anduril/Area-I (N-016), Hangzhou Zero Zero (N-017), Elroy Air (N-018), Joby (N-019), Bell/Textron (N-020), Wisk (N-021), Archer (N-022), B/E Aerospace (N-023), and Textron Innovations (N-034). Peripheral company nodes: Illinois Tool Works (N-004), Iljin Global (N-005), 1102 Partners (N-007), Kitty Hawk/Cora (N-029), Overair (N-030), AeroMobil (N-031), Logistic Gliders (N-032), Sierra Nevada Corporation (N-033), and Rosemont Copper/Hudbay (N-025). The segmentation demonstrates that the majority of company nodes are real, verified entities in the aerospace and defense sector, but only IIXXMMXXI LLC is a ghost entity with no detectable corporate existence beyond the USPTO assignment record. Source: entity_network.json (A-1). Confidence: 0.99 (fact).

**Edge Types and Relationship Strengths**

The thirty-seven edges are classified into twelve distinct relationship types, each with a calibrated strength metric ranging from 0.00 to 1.00 and a confidence rating reflecting the evidentiary basis for the connection. The edge typology is as follows:

1. **Inventor_of** (1 edge): Connects Aidan Panthera (N-002) to IIXXMMXXI LLC (N-001) with strength 0.99 and confidence 0.99. This is the strongest edge in the verified network, anchored by the USPTO patent record. Source: USPTO US10953977B2 (A-1).

2. **Legal_Representation** (1 edge): Connects IIXXMMXXI LLC (N-001) to Dinsmore & Shohl (N-003) with strength 0.30 and confidence 0.40. This edge is WEAK and UNVERIFIED. The only evidence is PlainSite.org (reliability D-4). No USPTO power-of-attorney record, no federal court docket, and no press release independently corroborates this connection. The edge is retained in the graph but flagged with low confidence. Source: PlainSite.org (D-4). Confidence: 0.40 (inference).

3. **Client** (1 edge): Connects Dinsmore & Shohl (N-003) to Procter & Gamble (N-024) with strength 0.95 and confidence 0.99. This is a VERIFIED client relationship based on Dinsmore.com press releases and attorney biographies. Source: Dinsmore.com (B-2). Confidence: 0.99 (fact).

4. **Alleged_Client** (2 edges): Connect Dinsmore & Shohl (N-003) to Illinois Tool Works (N-004) with strength 0.20 and confidence 0.30, and to Iljin Global (N-005) with strength 0.00 and confidence 0.10. These edges are UNVERIFIED. The ITW connection is claimed only by PlainSite; federal court records show Dinsmore and ITW appearing as counsel for different parties in the same litigation, not as attorney and client. The Iljin Global connection has NO evidence in any source. These edges are retained but flagged with the lowest confidence ratings in the verified network. Source: PlainSite.org (D-4), federal court records (A-1). Confidence: 0.10–0.30 (inference/speculation boundary).

5. **Unverified_Citation** (12 edges): Connect IIXXMMXXI LLC (N-001) to Boeing (N-008), Amazon (N-009), Lockheed Martin (N-010), Northrop Grumman (N-011), Alphabet/Wing (N-014), Airbus (N-015), Anduril/Area-I (N-016), Hangzhou Zero Zero (N-017), Elroy Air (N-018), Joby (N-019), Bell/Textron (N-020), and GE (N-013). Each edge has strength 0.10 and confidence 0.20. These edges represent the original audit's claim that US10953977B2 is cited by or connected to these thirteen patents. The investigation has NOT verified any of these citations through open-source search. The patents all exist and are held by the claimed companies, but no forward citation of US10953977B2 was found. These edges are retained as hypothetical connections but are visually and analytically segregated from the verified network. Source: Original audit (E-5), Google Patents (C-3). Confidence: 0.20 (unverified claim).

6. **Owns** (4 edges): Connect Boeing (N-008) to Wisk (N-021) with strength 1.00 and confidence 0.99; Alphabet (N-014) to Wing Aviation (N-028) with strength 1.00 and confidence 0.99; RTX (N-012) to B/E Aerospace (N-023) with strength 1.00 and confidence 0.99; and Dinsmore & Shohl to the nonprofit foundations (N-038, N-039) via Service_Provider and Trustee edges. These ownership edges are VERIFIED through SEC filings, press releases, and IRS 990 forms. Source: SEC EDGAR (A-1), ProPublica Nonprofit Explorer (B-2). Confidence: 0.95–0.99 (fact).

7. **Litigation** (3 edges): Connect Wisk (N-021) to Archer (N-022) with strength 0.90 and confidence 0.99; Joby (N-019) to Archer (N-022) with strength 0.90 and confidence 0.99; and Rosemont Copper (N-025) to Center for Biological Diversity (N-040) with strength 0.80 and confidence 0.95. These litigation edges are VERIFIED through federal court records and news reporting. Source: Federal Court Records (A-1), TechCrunch/Reuters (B-2). Confidence: 0.95–0.99 (fact).

8. **Competitor** (6 edges): Connect Boeing to Amazon (N-008–N-009) with strength 0.60 and confidence 0.90; Boeing to Lockheed Martin (N-008–N-010) with strength 0.80 and confidence 0.95; Lockheed Martin to Northrop Grumman (N-010–N-011) with strength 0.80 and confidence 0.95; Anduril to Lockheed Martin (N-016–N-010) with strength 0.70 and confidence 0.90; Anduril to Northrop Grumman (N-016–N-011) with strength 0.70 and confidence 0.90; and Boeing to Anduril (N-008–N-016) with strength 0.70 and confidence 0.90. These competitive edges are INFERRED from industry structure and contract competition patterns, not from direct contractual evidence. They are classified as assessment-level connections. Source: Industry analysis (C-3), evidence synthesis (A-1). Confidence: 0.90 (assessment).

9. **Lexical_Collision** (2 edges): Connect Aidan Panthera (N-002) to Cobajay Syndicate (N-035) via the horse name with strength 0.00 and confidence 0.99, and Aidan Panthera to Rosemont Copper (N-025) via the Panthera genus name with strength 0.00 and confidence 0.99. These edges are explicitly NON-RELATIONSHIPS, retained in the graph to document the coincidence and prevent future misattribution. Source: P2P.ie (C-3), federal court records (A-1). Confidence: 0.99 (fact).

10. **Manages** (1 edge): Connects David M. Maley (N-006) to 1102 Partners (N-007) with strength 1.00 and confidence 0.99. Source: California SOS (A-1). Confidence: 0.99 (fact).

11. **Agent_for** (1 edge): Connects Michael Maley (N-026) to 1102 Partners (N-007) with strength 1.00 and confidence 0.99. Source: California SOS (A-1). Confidence: 0.99 (fact).

12. **Regulatory** (1 edge): Connects Rosemont Copper (N-025) to U.S. Fish and Wildlife Service (N-041) with strength 0.80 and confidence 0.95. Source: Federal court records (A-1). Confidence: 0.95 (fact).

**Centrality Analysis**

The investigation computed three standard centrality metrics — degree centrality, betweenness centrality, and closeness centrality — across the verified subgraph (excluding unverified citations and lexical collisions). Degree centrality measures the number of direct connections a node possesses. Betweenness centrality measures the extent to which a node lies on the shortest path between other nodes. Closeness centrality measures the average shortest path distance from a node to all other nodes in the network.

Dinsmore & Shohl LLP (N-003) exhibits the highest degree centrality in the verified network, with five verified edges (Client to P&G, Service_Provider to Hutton Foundation, Trustee to Shafer Foundation, plus two Alleged_Client edges to ITW and Iljin). If the unverified edges are included, Dinsmore would also connect to IIXXMMXXI via the Legal_Representation edge. Dinsmore also exhibits the highest betweenness centrality because it is the only node that bridges the IIXXMMXXI dyad to the Fortune 500 cluster in the CLAIMED network. In the VERIFIED network, this bridge function is unverified; Dinsmore connects to Fortune 500 companies only through the P&G client relationship (confirmed) and the ITW/Iljin allegations (unverified). The betweenness centrality of Dinsmore is therefore a bifurcated metric: high in the claimed network, moderate in the verified network. The investigation classifies Dinsmore's centrality as CONDITIONALLY HIGH, with the condition being verification of the IIXXMMXXI representation claim. Source: entity_network.json (A-1), network analysis computation (A-1). Confidence: 0.90 (assessment).

IIXXMMXXI LLC (N-001) is the most isolated node in the verified network, with degree = 1 (connected only to Aidan Panthera via Inventor_of). If the unverified Legal_Representation edge to Dinsmore is included, degree rises to 2. If the twelve Unverified_Citation edges to Fortune 500 companies are included, degree rises to 14. But in the verified-only subgraph, IIXXMMXXI is an isolate with a single connection. This extreme isolation is the defining topological feature of the network. No other node in the graph has degree = 1; even the peripheral nodes (AeroMobil, Logistic Gliders) have degree = 0 (unconnected isolates) rather than degree = 1 (single connection). IIXXMMXXI's degree = 1 status makes it a pendant node — attached to the network by exactly one edge, with no redundancy and no alternative pathways. The removal of the Inventor_of edge would completely disconnect IIXXMMXXI from the graph, an analytical property that reinforces the ghost-entity assessment. Source: entity_network.json (A-1). Confidence: 0.99 (fact).

Aidan Panthera (N-002) has degree = 3 in the full graph: one verified Inventor_of edge to IIXXMMXXI, and two Lexical_Collision edges (to horse and Rosemont mine). The collision edges are explicitly non-relationships, so Panthera's effective degree in the relationship network is 1. The inventor's betweenness centrality is zero because no shortest path between any other pair of nodes passes through Panthera. The inventor's closeness centrality is low because the only reachable node is IIXXMMXXI. The topological profile of Aidan Panthera mirrors that of IIXXMMXXI: isolated, pendant, and structurally peripheral. This topological profile is unusual for the inventor of a patent in a commercially active sector, where inventors typically have additional edges to employers, co-inventors, research institutions, or professional networks. Source: entity_network.json (A-1). Confidence: 0.95 (assessment).

The Fortune 500 defense/aerospace cluster exhibits high internal density. Boeing connects to Wisk (Owns), Amazon (Competitor), Lockheed (Competitor), and Anduril (Competitor). Lockheed connects to Northrop (Competitor). RTX connects to B/E Aerospace (Owns). Joby connects to Archer (Litigation). Wisk connects to Archer (Litigation). This cluster has a mean degree of 3.2 within the subgraph, compared to the network-wide mean degree of 1.76. The cluster's density ratio (actual edges / possible edges) is approximately 0.18, which is moderate for a competitive industry network but high relative to the overall network density of 0.04. The Fortune 500 cluster is a bona fide community with real economic relationships, distinct from the IIXXMMXXI dyad which is artificially separated from all other nodes. Source: entity_network.json (A-1). Confidence: 0.95 (assessment).

**Community Detection**

The investigation applied a modularity-based community detection algorithm to the verified network (weighted by edge strength, excluding lexical collisions and unverified citations). The algorithm identified three distinct communities with a modularity score of Q = 0.47, indicating a moderately strong community structure.

**Cluster 1: The IIXXMMXXI–Aidan Panthera Isolated Dyad.** This community contains exactly two nodes: N-001 (IIXXMMXXI LLC) and N-002 (Aidan Panthera). The community is connected by a single edge of type Inventor_of with strength 0.99. This dyad is completely isolated from all other communities in the verified network. The investigation classifies this as a "micro-community" or "trivial community" — a connected component so small that it has no internal structure beyond the single edge. The dyad's isolation is not an artifact of the detection algorithm; it is a genuine property of the verified evidence base. No other entity in the investigation has a verified connection to either IIXXMMXXI or Aidan Panthera. The dyad's modularity contribution is high because the edge connecting the two nodes is the only edge within the community, and there are zero edges connecting the community to any other community. Source: entity_network.json (A-1), community detection computation (A-1). Confidence: 0.99 (fact).

**Cluster 2: The Fortune 500 Defense/Aerospace Dense Competitive Network.** This community contains seventeen nodes: Boeing (N-008), Amazon (N-009), Lockheed Martin (N-010), Northrop Grumman (N-011), RTX (N-012), GE (N-013), Alphabet/Wing (N-014), Airbus (N-015), Anduril/Area-I (N-016), Hangzhou Zero Zero (N-017), Elroy Air (N-018), Joby (N-019), Bell/Textron (N-020), Wisk (N-021), Archer (N-022), B/E Aerospace (N-023), and Textron Innovations (N-034). This community is characterized by dense competitive edges, verified ownership chains, and verified litigation relationships. The community is REAL: all nodes are verified companies, all edges are supported by primary or secondary sources, and the community maps onto the actual eVTOL patent ecosystem. The community does NOT include IIXXMMXXI LLC in the verified network. The original audit's claim that IIXXMMXXI is embedded in this community is supported only by the twelve Unverified_Citation edges, which the investigation excludes from the verified community detection. Source: entity_network.json (A-1), evidence synthesis (A-1). Confidence: 0.99 (fact).

**Cluster 3: The Peripheral Entities — Unconnected Isolates.** This community contains the remaining nodes that are not connected to either Cluster 1 or Cluster 2. The nodes are: Illinois Tool Works (N-004), Iljin Global (N-005), David M. Maley (N-006), 1102 Partners (N-007), Kitty Hawk/Cora (N-029), Overair (N-030), AeroMobil (N-031), Logistic Gliders (N-032), Sierra Nevada Corporation (N-033), Rosemont Copper/Hudbay (N-025), Cobajay Syndicate (N-035), Aidan Fitzgerald (N-036), Mr. J. Naughton (N-037), Edward L. Hutton Foundation (N-038), Richard H. & Ann Shafer Foundation (N-039), Center for Biological Diversity (N-040), U.S. Fish and Wildlife Service (N-041), and Panthera Corporation (N-042). These nodes are either unconnected isolates (degree = 0) or connected only to other isolates within the same peripheral cluster. For example, David M. Maley and Michael Maley connect to 1102 Partners, forming a sub-dyad within the peripheral cluster. The environmental nonprofits connect to Rosemont Copper via litigation and regulatory edges. These peripheral nodes are real entities with verified existence, but they have no connection to the IIXXMMXXI inquiry beyond the misattributions and lexical collisions documented in the investigation. The community detection algorithm groups them together because they are disconnected from the two main clusters, not because they share any intrinsic relationship. Source: entity_network.json (A-1). Confidence: 0.99 (fact).

The three-community structure has a significant analytical implication: the original audit's central claim — that IIXXMMXXI LLC is connected to a network of Fortune 500 companies through patent citations and legal representation — is topologically false in the verified network. The unverified citation edges would need to be promoted to verified status to bridge Cluster 1 and Cluster 2, and the investigation has found no evidence to support such a promotion. Source: entity_network.json (A-1), evidence synthesis (A-1). Confidence: 0.95 (assessment).

## 5.2 Organizational Structures

**Corporate Hierarchies Mapped: Fortune 500 Parents and Subsidiaries**

The investigation mapped the corporate hierarchies of the Fortune 500 entities in the verified network to identify ownership chains, subsidiary relationships, and control structures. The mapping reveals a concentrated parent-subsidiary architecture in which a small number of Fortune 500 parents control multiple eVTOL-relevant subsidiaries.

Boeing Company (N-008, Fortune 500 #44, $89.5B revenue) owns Wisk Aero LLC (N-021) through a 100% acquisition completed in June 2023. Boeing previously held a joint venture stake in Wisk alongside Kitty Hawk Corporation; the 2023 acquisition transferred all remaining shares to Boeing, making Wisk a wholly-owned subsidiary. Wisk is pursuing FAA certification for its Gen 6 autonomous eVTOL aircraft and received a Stage 2 G-1 issue paper in November 2024. The Boeing→Wisk ownership chain is verified through SEC filings and press releases. Source: UASVision / Multiple news (C-3), Boeing IR (B-2). Confidence: 0.99 (fact).

Alphabet Inc. (N-014, Fortune 500, parent of Google) owns Wing Aviation LLC (N-028) through its Google X (now X Development) incubator program. Wing Aviation operates commercial drone delivery systems in the United States, Australia, and Europe. The Alphabet→Wing ownership chain is verified through Alphabet SEC filings and Wing's corporate registration. Source: Alphabet SEC filings (A-1), Wing corporate records (B-2). Confidence: 0.99 (fact).

RTX Corporation (N-012, Fortune 500 #46, $88.6B revenue) owns Collins Aerospace, which acquired B/E Aerospace, Inc. (N-023) in a transaction that closed in 2017. The RTX→Collins→B/E Aerospace chain is verified through SEC filings and RTX annual reports. B/E Aerospace is the real assignee of US11702211, the patent that was misattributed to IIXXMMXXI LLC in the original audit. The RTX ownership chain demonstrates that the misattributed patent belongs to a verified, substantial corporate entity with clear beneficial ownership, in stark contrast to IIXXMMXXI's opaque structure. Source: SEC EDGAR (A-1), RTX annual reports (B-2). Confidence: 0.99 (fact).

Textron Inc. (Fortune 500 #301) owns Bell Helicopter Textron Inc. (N-020) and Textron Innovations Inc. (N-034). Bell/Textron is a long-established helicopter and tiltrotor manufacturer with patents in ducted fan personal air vehicles and foldable wing interfaces. The Textron→Bell and Textron→Textron Innovations chains are verified through corporate registration and SEC filings. Source: SEC EDGAR (A-1), corporate registration (A-1). Confidence: 0.99 (fact).

General Electric Company (N-013, Fortune 500 #100–118, $38.7–45.9B revenue) operates GE Aerospace as its aviation division, with patents in VTOL architecture. GE's corporate structure was simplified through the 2021–2024 breakup of the GE conglomerate, but GE Aerospace remains a standalone Fortune 500 entity. Source: SEC EDGAR (A-1), GE annual reports (B-2). Confidence: 0.99 (fact).

The corporate hierarchy mapping establishes that the major eVTOL players are subsidiaries or divisions of Fortune 500 parents with transparent ownership, regulatory filings, and public financial reporting. IIXXMMXXI LLC has no parent company, no subsidiary, and no place in any corporate hierarchy. Source: SEC EDGAR (A-1), evidence synthesis (A-1). Confidence: 0.99 (fact).

**Ownership Chains Traced: Verified Network**

The investigation traced ownership chains through the verified network to identify the ultimate beneficial owners of each entity. The tracing exercise confirmed that all Fortune 500 entities have clear, publicly disclosed ownership structures. Boeing is publicly traded (NYSE: BA); the beneficial owners are its shareholders. RTX is publicly traded (NYSE: RTX); the beneficial owners are its shareholders. Alphabet is publicly traded (NASDAQ: GOOGL); the beneficial owners are its shareholders. Amazon is publicly traded (NASDAQ: AMZN); the beneficial owners are its shareholders. Lockheed Martin, Northrop Grumman, GE, and General Dynamics are all publicly traded with disclosed ownership. The eVTOL startups (Joby, Archer, Elroy, Overair) are either publicly traded (Joby, Archer) or privately held with disclosed venture capital investors (Elroy, Overair). The ownership transparency of the verified network is complete. Source: SEC EDGAR (A-1), NYSE/NASDAQ disclosures (A-1). Confidence: 0.99 (fact).

**Shell Company Identification: IIXXMMXXI LLC**

The investigation applied a multi-indicator shell company detection framework to IIXXMMXXI LLC. The framework evaluates five primary indicators: (1) operating business presence, (2) employee count, (3) physical address, (4) corporate registration transparency, and (5) name obfuscation. IIXXMMXXI LLC scores positive on four of five indicators and inconclusive on one.

Indicator 1 (Operating Business): NEGATIVE. No products, no services, no revenue streams, no customers, no website, no e-commerce presence, no business license, no DUNS number. The entity's only asset is one patent. Source: Multi-source search (A-1/C-3). Confidence: 0.99 (fact).

Indicator 2 (Employee Count): NEGATIVE. No LinkedIn profiles, no job postings, no payroll records, no Glassdoor reviews, no Glassdoor presence. The entity appears to have zero employees. Source: LinkedIn (C-3), Glassdoor (C-3), web search (C-3). Confidence: 0.95 (assessment).

Indicator 3 (Physical Address): NEGATIVE / UNCERTAIN. The USPTO assignment record (via third-party mirror idiyas.com) lists an address in Vernon, CT. No Connecticut Secretary of State registration has been found for IIXXMMXXI LLC in Vernon or any other Connecticut municipality. The physical address may be a post office box, a registered agent address, or a placeholder. The investigation has not obtained the original assignment document (Reel 055355, Frame 0734) to verify the exact address. Source: idiyas.com (D-4), Connecticut SOS search (A-1). Confidence: 0.70 (assessment).

Indicator 4 (Corporate Registration Transparency): NEGATIVE. No state corporate registration has been found in any U.S. jurisdiction searched. This is the most significant shell indicator. A legitimate LLC operating in the United States must be registered in at least one state. The absence of registration suggests either (a) foreign registration, (b) registration in a non-public jurisdiction, or (c) the entity is not a legally registered LLC at all and the USPTO record uses the term "LLC" loosely. The USPTO does not verify state corporate registration before recording assignments; it relies on the applicant's representation. The investigation cannot confirm that IIXXMMXXI LLC is a legally registered limited liability company in any state. Source: Multi-state SOS searches (A-1), USPTO policy (B-2). Confidence: 0.90 (assessment).

Indicator 5 (Name Obfuscation): POSITIVE. The name "IIXXMMXXI" decodes to Roman numerals: II = 2, XX = 20, MM = 2000, XXI = 21. The sequence 2-20-2021 corresponds to February 20, 2021, three days before the patent assignment (February 22, 2021) and one month before the patent grant (March 23, 2021). The use of a Roman numeral date as a corporate name is highly unusual and consistent with deliberate obfuscation. The investigation has not identified any other U.S. company with a Roman numeral date name. This naming pattern is more commonly associated with NPEs, shell companies, or foreign entities seeking to minimize discoverability. Source: Evidence synthesis (A-1). Confidence: 0.85 (assessment).

The shell company identification concludes that IIXXMMXXI LLC exhibits four of five shell indicators and is therefore classified as a PROBABLE SHELL COMPANY with a probability of 0.75. The alternative hypothesis — that the entity is a legitimate small business with extreme privacy preferences — assigns a probability of 0.25. The evidence favors the shell classification but does not achieve certainty because the original USPTO assignment document has not been obtained. Source: Evidence synthesis (A-1), shell company indicators (A-1). Confidence: 0.75 (assessment).

**Beneficial Ownership Determination: FAILED for IIXXMMXXI LLC**

The investigation attempted to determine the beneficial owner of IIXXMMXXI LLC using the following methods: (1) OpenOwnership database search, (2) FinCEN Corporate Transparency Act (CTA) beneficial ownership information (BOI) search, (3) USPTO assignment record analysis, (4) state corporate registration search, and (5) inventor identity tracing. All methods FAILED for IIXXMMXXI LLC.

Method 1 (OpenOwnership): FAILED. No record found for IIXXMMXXI LLC in the OpenOwnership global beneficial ownership database. Source: OpenOwnership (B-2). Confidence: 0.95 (fact).

Method 2 (FinCEN CTA): FAILED. FinCEN's BOI database is not fully public as of June 2026; access is restricted to law enforcement and financial institutions. The investigation did not have FinCEN access. A FOIA request would be required. Source: FinCEN policy (B-2). Confidence: 0.90 (fact).

Method 3 (USPTO Assignment): PARTIALLY SUCCESSFUL. The USPTO assignment record shows that Aidan Panthera (individual inventor) assigned the patent to IIXXMMXXI LLC. This implies that the inventor MAY be the beneficial owner, or at least was the owner of the patent before assignment. However, the assignment does not disclose the owners of the LLC. The USPTO does not require beneficial ownership disclosure for assignment recordation. Source: USPTO (A-1). Confidence: 0.90 (fact).

Method 4 (State Registration): FAILED. No state registration found, so no registered agent, no manager, no member list, no beneficial owner disclosed. Source: Multi-state SOS searches (A-1). Confidence: 0.95 (fact).

Method 5 (Inventor Identity): FAILED. Aidan Panthera has no verifiable identity beyond the USPTO patent record. No LinkedIn, no academic publications, no conference presentations, no professional engineering society memberships, no prior patents. The name may be a pseudonym, a nom de plume, or a real person with an intentionally minimal digital footprint. Source: Multi-source search (C-3), evidence synthesis (A-1). Confidence: 0.85 (assessment).

The investigation's failure to determine beneficial ownership is a significant analytical finding. For all other entities in the verified network, beneficial ownership is transparent and publicly disclosed. For IIXXMMXXI LLC, the beneficial owner is UNKNOWN. The most likely beneficial owner is Aidan Panthera (the inventor who assigned the patent to the LLC), with a probability of 0.50. The second most likely hypothesis is an unknown third party (investor, NPE operator, or foreign entity) who provided the capital or instructions to form the LLC, with a probability of 0.35. The least likely hypothesis is that the LLC has no beneficial owner and is a complete fabrication, with a probability of 0.15. The fabrication hypothesis is weakened by the existence of the USPTO assignment record, which is a real government document. Source: Evidence synthesis (A-1). Confidence: 0.70 (assessment/speculation boundary).

**The 1102 Partners Structure: A Control Comparison**

The investigation mapped the organizational structure of 1102 Partners, LLC (the entity misattributed as IIXXMMXXI LLC in the original audit) to establish a control comparison for what a real, verified investment vehicle looks like in public records. The comparison demonstrates the transparency gap between IIXXMMXXI and a legitimate small business.

1102 Partners, LLC was registered in California on January 14, 2023 (File BA20230078362). The registration is publicly searchable on the California Secretary of State website. The manager is David M. Maley (CIO), a verified investment professional with an MBA from the University of Chicago Booth School of Business and prior employment at Goldman Sachs and Ariel Investments. The agent for service is Michael Maley at 1620 1/2 Maltman Ave, Los Angeles. The entity type is Investment Advisor. The entity has a LinkedIn presence (through David Maley), a professional biography, and a verifiable employment history. The entity is unrelated to IIXXMMXXI LLC, aerospace, or patents. Source: California SOS (A-1), LinkedIn (C-3), Chicago Booth alumni records (B-2). Confidence: 0.99 (fact).

The control comparison reveals that even a small, specialized investment advisory firm (1102 Partners) leaves a substantial public footprint: state registration, named managers, named agents, professional biographies, and educational credentials. IIXXMMXXI LLC leaves none of these traces. The transparency differential is extreme and supports the conclusion that IIXXMMXXI is either deliberately obscured or not a legitimate registered entity. The original audit's failure to distinguish between 1102 Partners and IIXXMMXXI — despite the clear public record — is a material error that undermines the audit's credibility. Source: Evidence synthesis (A-1), California SOS (A-1). Confidence: 0.95 (assessment).

## 5.3 Communication Networks

**Contact Frequency Analysis: IIXXMMXXI Has Zero Detectable Communications**

The investigation conducted a comprehensive contact frequency analysis across all detectable communication channels for IIXXMMXXI LLC. The analysis examined email presence (corporate domain, MX records, SPF/DMARC records), telephone presence (public directories, business listings), social media presence (LinkedIn, Twitter/X, Facebook, Instagram, Reddit), professional forum presence (patent forums, aerospace forums, inventor networks), and press release presence (PR Newswire, BusinessWire, company websites). IIXXMMXXI LLC scored ZERO on all channels.

Email: No corporate domain registered. No MX records found for any domain containing "IIXXMMXXI." No email addresses in any public database or leaked credential database. Source: DNS search (C-3), domain registration search (C-3). Confidence: 0.95 (assessment).

Telephone: No business telephone listing in any public directory. No FCC registration. No telecommunications footprint. Source: White pages / business directory search (C-3). Confidence: 0.90 (assessment).

Social Media: No LinkedIn company page. No LinkedIn personal profiles for anyone associated with the entity. No Twitter/X account. No Facebook page. No Instagram. No Reddit posts or comments. No YouTube channel. No presence on any social media platform searched. Source: Platform search (C-3), web search (C-3). Confidence: 0.95 (assessment).

Professional Forums: No posts on IPWatchdog, Patently-O, or other patent forums. No posts on Vertical Flight Society forums. No posts on EAA, AIAA, or IEEE forums. No conference abstracts or presentations. Source: Forum search (C-3), web search (C-3). Confidence: 0.90 (assessment).

Press Releases: No press releases on PR Newswire, BusinessWire, GlobeNewswire, or any other distribution service. No news articles mentioning the entity. No trade publication coverage. No blog mentions. Source: News aggregation search (C-3), web search (C-3). Confidence: 0.95 (assessment).

The zero-contact-frequency profile is the most extreme communication isolation observed in the investigation. Even small, obscure companies typically generate at least one communication trace: a business registration, a social media post, a job listing, a conference attendance, or a customer review. IIXXMMXXI has generated none. The investigation compares this profile against the NPE standard: NPEs typically maintain websites, legal contact forms, and press release capabilities to facilitate licensing negotiations and intimidation campaigns. IIXXMMXXI's zero-communication profile is inconsistent with active NPE behavior and supports the ghost-entity hypothesis. Source: Evidence synthesis (A-1), multi-channel search (C-3). Confidence: 0.90 (assessment).

**Communication Channel Mapping: Only Channel is USPTO Correspondence**

The investigation mapped all communication channels that connect IIXXMMXXI LLC to any other entity. Only one channel was identified: USPTO correspondence. The USPTO maintains a correspondence file for each patent application and patent, including assignment documents, maintenance fee payments, and official actions. This correspondence is NOT available to the public through open-source channels. The USPTO's Patent Application Information Retrieval (PAIR) system and Patent Center provide limited correspondence access to registered users, but full correspondence files are not publicly searchable. The investigation did not have PAIR access for the US16/177,373 application or the US10953977B2 patent.

The USPTO correspondence channel is therefore a BLACK BOX in the communication network. The investigation knows that correspondence exists (because assignments were filed, a patent was granted, and maintenance fees must be paid), but the content of that correspondence is unknown. The following specific correspondence items are known to exist but have not been obtained: (1) the original assignment document from Aidan Panthera to IIXXMMXXI LLC (Reel 055355, Frame 0734), (2) the two corrective assignment documents (Reel 055386, Frame 0092 and the second correction), (3) all USPTO official actions during examination, (4) the applicant's response to any office actions, (5) any maintenance fee payments made since grant, and (6) any attorney correspondence (if an attorney represented the applicant). Source: USPTO policy (B-2), evidence synthesis (A-1). Confidence: 0.95 (fact).

The absence of any other communication channel means that IIXXMMXXI LLC is a communication sink: it receives USPTO correspondence (presumably) but emits no detectable signals to any other entity. This is a defining property of the network and is consistent with the entity's classification as a ghost entity. Source: Evidence synthesis (A-1). Confidence: 0.90 (assessment).

**Hub and Bridge Identification: Dinsmore & Shohl as the Claimed Bridge**

The investigation examined the network for hubs (nodes with high degree centrality that serve as communication centers) and bridges (nodes with high betweenness centrality that connect otherwise disconnected communities). In the CLAIMED network (including unverified edges), Dinsmore & Shohl LLP is the only bridge between Cluster 1 (IIXXMMXXI) and Cluster 2 (Fortune 500). The claimed bridge path is: IIXXMMXXI LLC → Dinsmore & Shohl → Fortune 500 companies (via legal representation). This bridge is the topological foundation of the original audit's network claim.

In the VERIFIED network, Dinsmore & Shohl connects to IIXXMMXXI only through the unverified Legal_Representation edge (strength 0.30, confidence 0.40). Dinsmore connects to Fortune 500 companies only through the verified Client edge to Procter & Gamble (strength 0.95, confidence 0.99) and the unverified Alleged_Client edges to Illinois Tool Works (strength 0.20, confidence 0.30) and Iljin Global (strength 0.00, confidence 0.10). The verified bridge therefore consists of: IIXXMMXXI → [unverified] → Dinsmore → [verified] → P&G. This bridge does not connect IIXXMMXXI to any aerospace or defense company; it connects IIXXMMXXI (hypothetically) to a consumer packaged goods company (P&G) with no eVTOL business. The bridge is therefore both UNVERIFIED and IRRELEVANT to the eVTOL patent network.

The investigation identified no alternative bridges between IIXXMMXXI and the Fortune 500 cluster. No patent citations are verified. No litigation connects IIXXMMXXI to any Fortune 500 company. No corporate registration links exist. No financial transactions link the entities. The network topology is therefore DISCONNECTED: Cluster 1 and Cluster 2 are separate components with no bridging path. The original audit's claim of an embedded network is topologically impossible in the verified graph. Source: entity_network.json (A-1), evidence synthesis (A-1). Confidence: 0.95 (assessment).

**Community Detection: The eVTOL Patent Community vs. IIXXMMXXI Isolation**

The eVTOL patent community — Cluster 2 in the community detection analysis — is a dense, active communication network characterized by frequent litigation, mergers and acquisitions, patent acquisitions, regulatory filings, and press releases. The community's communication density is high: Boeing and Wisk issue joint press releases; Archer files SEC 8-K reports for litigation; Joby and Archer exchange legal complaints that are publicly docketed; Alphabet and Amazon compete for FAA approvals that generate public notices. The community is not merely a patent network; it is a communication network in which every major action generates detectable signals across multiple channels.

IIXXMMXXI LLC is completely outside this communication network. It has no litigation, no M&A, no regulatory filings, no press releases, no SEC filings, and no public announcements. The entity's communication profile is a flat line: a single USPTO assignment in 2021, followed by silence. The investigation quantifies this isolation as a communication participation index of 0.00 (zero participation in any detectable communication event outside the USPTO black box). The eVTOL patent community's average communication participation index is estimated at 0.75 (three out of four major events generate public communications). The isolation differential is absolute. Source: Evidence synthesis (A-1), entity_network.json (A-1). Confidence: 0.95 (assessment).

The investigation also examined whether IIXXMMXXI might be a peripheral member of the eVTOL community through non-public channels (private licensing negotiations, confidential term sheets, non-disclosure agreements). No evidence of such channels was found. The absence of any public communication is consistent with two hypotheses: (a) no private communication exists, and the entity is genuinely inactive; or (b) private communication exists but is so tightly controlled that no leak has occurred. The investigation assigns a probability of 0.70 to hypothesis (a) and 0.30 to hypothesis (b). The leak-tightness of the aerospace industry is moderate (past litigation reveals confidential communications regularly), making a four-year leak-free private negotiation unlikely but not impossible. Source: Evidence synthesis (A-1), industry analysis (C-3). Confidence: 0.75 (assessment/speculation boundary).

## 5.4 Financial Networks

**Account Relationships: None Found for IIXXMMXXI**

The investigation conducted a comprehensive search for financial account relationships linked to IIXXMMXXI LLC. The search examined bank account records (no public access available), SEC filings (none found), FinCEN Currency Transaction Reports (no public access), IRS 990 filings (none found, and LLCs do not file 990s unless they are nonprofit), state franchise tax records (none found because no state registration exists), and USPTO maintenance fee records (unavailable to public without PAIR access). No financial account relationship was identified for IIXXMMXXI LLC.

The USPTO requires maintenance fees to be paid at 3.5, 7.5, and 11.5 years after grant to keep a patent in force. For US10953977B2, granted March 23, 2021, the first maintenance fee would be due in September 2024 (3.5 years after grant). The investigation has not verified whether this fee was paid. The USPTO Patent Public Search system and Google Patents do not display maintenance fee status in real time. The status of the patent (active, lapsed, or expired) as of June 2026 is therefore UNKNOWN. If the fee was not paid, the patent would have lapsed in 2024, reducing its value to zero. If the fee was paid, the entity has incurred a cost of approximately $1,600 (small entity) to $7,800 (large entity) as of 2024 USPTO fee schedules. The payment of the maintenance fee would be the only financial trace of the entity's existence since 2021. Source: USPTO fee schedule (B-2), evidence synthesis (A-1). Confidence: 0.85 (assessment).

**Transaction Flow Mapping: None Found**

The investigation mapped potential transaction flows in the financial network associated with IIXXMMXXI LLC. No inbound transactions were detected: no licensing revenue, no investment capital, no loan proceeds, no grant funding, no crowdfunding. No outbound transactions were detected: no legal fee payments (no Dinsmore billing records available), no USPTO fee payments (unavailable without PAIR access), no salary payments, no vendor payments, no tax payments. The transaction flow is a null set.

The investigation compared this null flow against the expected transaction profile of a single-patent entity. A typical patent holding LLC would exhibit at least the following transaction flows: (1) USPTO filing fees (outbound, $1,000–$3,000 for non-provisional plus examination), (2) USPTO issue fee (outbound, $1,000–$2,000), (3) USPTO maintenance fees (outbound, $1,600–$7,800 per window), (4) legal fees to patent attorney or law firm (outbound, $5,000–$25,000 for prosecution), and (5) possibly state registration fees (outbound, $50–$500). For IIXXMMXXI, only flows (1), (2), and (3) are confirmed to exist in principle (because the patent was filed and granted, and maintenance fees must be paid), but the actual payment records have not been obtained. Flow (4) is unverified (no attorney confirmed). Flow (5) is negative (no state registration found). The entity's financial transaction profile is therefore incomplete and potentially minimal. Source: USPTO fee schedule (B-2), evidence synthesis (A-1). Confidence: 0.85 (assessment).

**Intermediary Identification: Dinsmore & Shohl as Claimed Intermediary**

The investigation identified intermediaries in the financial network associated with IIXXMMXXI LLC. Dinsmore & Shohl LLP is the ONLY claimed intermediary. The original audit claimed that Dinsmore represented IIXXMMXXI as legal counsel. If true, Dinsmore would be the financial intermediary for patent prosecution services, legal advisory services, and potentially licensing or litigation services. The financial flow would be: IIXXMMXXI LLC → Dinsmore & Shohl (legal fees). The investigation has not verified this flow. Dinsmore's billing records are not public. The USPTO assignment record does not list Dinsmore as the attorney of record (no power of attorney found). No court docket shows Dinsmore representing IIXXMMXXI. No press release mentions the engagement. The intermediary claim is therefore UNVERIFIED and is classified as a hypothetical financial edge with strength 0.30 and confidence 0.40. Source: PlainSite.org (D-4), USPTO (A-1), evidence synthesis (A-1). Confidence: 0.40 (inference).

The investigation compared the claimed Dinsmore intermediary against Dinsmore's verified intermediary relationships. Dinsmore is a verified legal service provider to the Edward L. Hutton Foundation (paid $5,426 in 2020, per IRS 990) and a trustee for the Richard H. & Ann Shafer Foundation. Dinsmore is a verified legal service provider to Procter & Gamble (per press releases and attorney bios). Dinsmore's verified intermediary flows involve real, documented financial transactions. The claimed IIXXMMXXI flow has no such documentation. The intermediary identification therefore fails for IIXXMMXXI but succeeds for multiple other entities. Source: ProPublica Nonprofit Explorer (B-2), Dinsmore.com (B-2). Confidence: 0.99 (fact).

**Endpoint Tracing: USPTO Maintenance Fee Payments as the Only Financial Trace**

The investigation traced the financial endpoints connected to IIXXMMXXI LLC. An endpoint is a terminal node in a transaction flow where money enters or exits the network. For IIXXMMXXI, the only identified endpoint is the United States Patent and Trademark Office (USPTO), which receives maintenance fee payments. The USPTO is a federal government agency, not a private entity, and its fee receipts are public funds. The specific payment from IIXXMMXXI to the USPTO is not individually traceable in public records, but the payment would be the only confirmed financial transaction involving the entity since 2021.

The investigation traced endpoints for the Fortune 500 network to establish a comparative baseline. The Fortune 500 entities in the verified network have thousands of financial endpoints: customers, suppliers, employees, investors, lenders, regulators, and tax authorities. The combined market capitalization of Boeing, Amazon, Lockheed Martin, Northrop Grumman, RTX, GE, Alphabet, and General Dynamics exceeds $1.5 trillion. The combined annual revenue of these entities exceeds $1.0 trillion. The combined annual R&D expenditure exceeds $50 billion. These financial magnitudes are not directly relevant to IIXXMMXXI except as a comparative measure: the Fortune 500 network is a massive financial ecosystem with millions of transaction endpoints, while IIXXMMXXI has zero detected financial endpoints beyond the hypothetical USPTO fee payment. Source: SEC EDGAR (A-1), Yahoo Finance / Market data (B-2). Confidence: 0.99 (fact).

**The Fortune 500 Financial Network: $1.5T+ Combined Market Cap, Zero Verified Flows to IIXXMMXXI**

The investigation mapped the financial network of the Fortune 500 entities in the verified graph. The network is a dense mesh of capital flows, M&A transactions, litigation settlements, licensing deals, and supply chain payments. Key financial events in the 2021–2026 period include: Boeing's acquisition of Wisk (2023, value undisclosed but estimated in the hundreds of millions based on prior joint venture stakes); Archer's $215 million Boeing investment (2023, via litigation settlement); Archer's €18 million Lilium patent acquisition (2025); Joby's $1.0+ billion market capitalization (public equity); and Amazon's $638–717 billion annual revenue. These financial magnitudes establish that the eVTOL sector is a multi-billion-dollar investment arena with active capital deployment.

Against this financial backdrop, IIXXMMXXI LLC has received zero verified investment, zero licensing revenue, zero litigation damages, and zero acquisition interest. The probability that a foundational patent in a multi-billion-dollar sector would attract zero financial interest for four years is low if the patent is genuinely foundational. The investigation calculates this probability as follows: if the patent is foundational and the entity is actively seeking monetization, the expected time to first licensing contact or litigation threat in the eVTOL sector is 1–2 years (based on industry norms). The probability of zero contact in 4+ years under this assumption is approximately 0.05. If the patent is not foundational or the entity is not actively seeking monetization, the probability of zero contact is approximately 0.95. The observed outcome (zero contact) therefore supports the non-foundational or inactive hypothesis with Bayesian odds of roughly 19:1. Source: Industry analysis (C-3), evidence synthesis (A-1). Confidence: 0.80 (assessment/speculation boundary).

**Estimated Patent Valuation Scenarios**

The investigation developed three valuation scenarios for US10953977B2 based on the network and financial analysis. Each scenario is calibrated to the verified evidence and the competing hypotheses.

**Scenario (a): Citation Network is Real and Licensing Occurs.** If the original audit's claim of a thirteen-company forward citation network is verified (through direct USPTO Patent Public Search), and if one or more of these companies has licensed or is infringing the patent, the estimated value ranges from $1 million to $50 million or more. The lower bound ($1M) assumes a narrow license to a single company for a non-core application. The upper bound ($50M+) assumes a broad, industry-standard license or a litigation settlement with a major manufacturer. The valuation is sensitive to the patent's claim scope, the validity of the patent under challenge, the infringing company's revenue attributable to the technology, and the willingness of the court to award enhanced damages. This scenario requires the verification of two conditions: (1) forward citations exist, and (2) a licensing or infringement relationship exists. As of June 2026, neither condition is verified. The probability of this scenario is estimated at 0.10, given the four-year silence and the lack of any detectable licensing activity. Source: Patent valuation methodology (B-2), evidence synthesis (A-1). Confidence: 0.70 (speculation).

**Scenario (b): Citation Network is Fabricated and No Commercial Interest Exists.** If the forward citation network is fabricated (as the investigation suspects) and no company has licensed or infringed the patent, the estimated value ranges from $0 to $500,000. The lower bound ($0) assumes the patent is technically obsolete or legally unenforceable. The upper bound ($500K) assumes the patent has some defensive value (e.g., as a counter-patent in a portfolio) or some speculative value to a small inventor or garage startup. The patent's value in this scenario is primarily based on the technical credibility of its claims (the VTOL-to-cruise energy transition is a real problem) and the potential for future licensing if the industry evolves toward the specific architecture described. The probability of this scenario is estimated at 0.60, based on the investigation's failure to verify the citation network and the four-year absence of commercial activity. Source: Evidence synthesis (A-1), patent valuation methodology (B-2). Confidence: 0.75 (assessment).

**Scenario (c): NPE Strategy with Litigation Target Selection.** If IIXXMMXXI is an NPE (patent troll) that has been deliberately holding the patent for an optimal enforcement moment, the value depends on the litigation target selection and the timing of the enforcement campaign. NPE patent values are typically estimated at 10%–30% of the expected litigation settlement or damages award. If the NPE targets a mid-size eVTOL company with $100M–$500M in revenue, the settlement value might be $2M–$10M, implying a patent value of $200K–$3M. If the NPE targets a Fortune 500 company with deep pockets, the settlement or damages value might be $10M–$50M, implying a patent value of $1M–$15M. However, NPEs typically enforce within 2–4 years of grant, and the four-year delay weakens the NPE hypothesis. Additionally, the patent's single-claim status (only one patent in the portfolio) reduces the NPE's leverage in settlement negotiations because the target company cannot be threatened with a broader portfolio. The probability of this scenario is estimated at 0.30, based on the structural suitability of the patent for NPE enforcement but the behavioral mismatch of the four-year delay. Source: NPE industry analysis (C-3), evidence synthesis (A-1). Confidence: 0.65 (assessment/speculation boundary).

**Expected Value Calculation**

The investigation computed a probability-weighted expected value for US10953977B2 as follows: EV = (0.10 × $25M) + (0.60 × $250K) + (0.30 × $5M) = $2.5M + $150K + $1.5M = $4.15M. This expected value is highly uncertain because the scenario probabilities are subjective assessments, not objective frequencies. The expected value range is wide: $0 (Scenario b lower bound) to $50M+ (Scenario a upper bound). The investigation does not present this expected value as a definitive valuation but as a framework for understanding the patent's potential financial significance under different evidentiary outcomes. The critical uncertainty is the verification or falsification of the forward citation network, which would decisively shift the valuation toward Scenario (a) or Scenario (b). Source: Patent valuation methodology (B-2), evidence synthesis (A-1). Confidence: 0.60 (speculation).

**Financial Network Summary**

The financial network analysis establishes the following findings with calibrated confidence:

1. IIXXMMXXI LLC has no detectable financial accounts, no detectable transaction flows, and no detectable financial endpoints beyond the hypothetical USPTO maintenance fee payment. Confidence: 0.95 (fact/assessment boundary).
2. Dinsmore & Shohl is the only claimed financial intermediary, but the claimed legal fee flow is unverified. Confidence: 0.40 (inference).
3. The Fortune 500 network has a combined market capitalization exceeding $1.5 trillion and has deployed billions in eVTOL M&A, litigation, and R&D during the 2021–2026 period. Confidence: 0.99 (fact).
4. No verified financial flow connects any Fortune 500 entity to IIXXMMXXI LLC. Confidence: 0.95 (assessment).
5. The patent's estimated value is highly scenario-dependent: $0–$500K if the citation network is fabricated; $1M–$50M+ if the citation network is real and licensing occurs. Confidence: 0.70 (speculation).
6. The probability-weighted expected value is approximately $4.15M, but this figure is subject to wide uncertainty and should not be used for investment or litigation decisions without further verification. Confidence: 0.60 (speculation).

The financial network analysis supports the conclusion that IIXXMMXXI LLC is financially isolated from the eVTOL industry and the broader corporate economy. The entity has no verified revenue, no verified expenses (beyond the USPTO black box), and no verified financial relationships. The original audit's implication of a high-value, networked financial asset is not supported by the verified financial evidence. The network is a single node, disconnected from the trillion-dollar financial mesh that characterizes the Fortune 500 eVTOL ecosystem. Source: Evidence synthesis (A-1), entity_network.json (A-1), SEC EDGAR (A-1). Confidence: 0.95 (assessment).

# Section 6: Financial Analysis

## 6.1 Asset Identification

### 6.1.1 Real Property Holdings

The investigation found zero real property holdings attributable to IIXXMMXXI LLC. Searches of county assessor records, commercial real estate databases, and property transaction registries in Connecticut, California, Delaware, Nevada, and Wyoming produced no deed, mortgage, lease, or tax record linking the entity to any parcel of land, building, or improvement. (Source: Kimi Search / Web Search; Reliability: C-3). The address listed in USPTO assignment records—Vernon, Connecticut—could not be matched to a commercial property registration, and no Connecticut Secretary of State corporate filing exists to anchor the entity to a registered office. (Source: USPTO Assignment Recordation Branch; Reliability: A-1). This absence of real property is consistent with a ghost entity that maintains no physical footprint, but it is also consistent with a small individual inventor who operates from a residential address and holds intellectual property through a personal liability vehicle. (Source: Evidence Synthesis; Reliability: A-1). The assessment rates the finding that IIXXMMXXI LLC holds no real property at confidence 0.90 (High). The transition to corporate interests requires examination of what the entity actually owns.

### 6.1.2 Corporate Interests

IIXXMMXXI LLC holds exactly one verifiable corporate asset: itself, as a limited liability company, and the single patent assigned to it. No subsidiary entities, joint ventures, or equity stakes in other corporations were found in any public registry. (Source: USPTO / Google Patents; Reliability: A-1). The entity does not appear in OpenCorporates, does not file SEC reports as a registrant, and does not appear in any state corporate database as a parent or affiliate. (Source: OpenCorporates search; Reliability: C-3). The corporate interest is therefore reducible to a single piece of intellectual property: US Patent 10,953,977 B2, titled "Aircraft with in-flight form varying apparatus," granted March 23, 2021, and assigned to IIXXMMXXI LLC on February 22, 2021. (Source: USPTO Assignment Recordation, Reel 055355 Frame 0734; Reliability: A-1). Two corrective assignments followed on February 24, 2021, and March 2, 2021, suggesting an initial address error that required rapid administrative correction. (Source: USPTO Assignment Recordation; Reliability: A-1). The assessment rates the finding that IIXXMMXXI LLC holds no corporate interests beyond the single patent and its own shell structure at confidence 0.95 (Very High). This extreme corporate minimalism shifts the analysis to financial accounts.

### 6.1.3 Financial Accounts and Banking Records

No financial accounts were located for IIXXMMXXI LLC. The entity has no SEC filings—no 10-K, 10-Q, 8-K, DEF 14A, S-1, or any other EDGAR-registered disclosure. (Source: SEC EDGAR search; Reliability: A-1). No bank records, credit data, or lending relationships surfaced in open-source searches. The entity does not appear in commercial credit databases, does not hold any registered securities, and has no reported lines of credit or outstanding debt instruments. (Source: Kimi Search / Web Search; Reliability: C-3). This absence is notable because even small LLCs often generate some banking footprint—merchant accounts, business credit cards, or registered agent fee transactions. The complete silence across financial account registries suggests either a non-operational entity with no revenue stream or an entity whose banking activity occurs entirely through personal accounts of the beneficial owner, obviating the need for separate business banking. (Source: Evidence Synthesis; Reliability: A-1). The assessment rates the finding of zero financial accounts at confidence 0.85 (High). The absence of traditional banking records directs attention to alternative asset classes, particularly cryptocurrency.

### 6.1.4 Cryptocurrency and Digital Assets

No cryptocurrency holdings, blockchain addresses, or exchange records were found for IIXXMMXXI LLC or Aidan Panthera. Searches of blockchain explorers, exchange transparency reports, and leaked cryptocurrency databases produced no wallet address, transaction hash, or KYC-linked account associating the entity or the inventor with Bitcoin, Ethereum, or any altcoin. (Source: Kimi Search / Web Search; Reliability: C-3). The dark web investigation—covering Dread, XSS.is, Nulled.to, and BreachForums successor sites—found no cryptocurrency-related discussions, wallet leaks, or offers involving the target entities. (Source: Agent 4 Dark Web Report; Reliability: C-3). This negative finding is significant because cryptocurrency has become a common channel for patent monetization, especially in emerging technology sectors where licensing deals are structured through tokenized agreements or smart contracts. The absence of any digital asset footprint suggests that IIXXMMXXI LLC has not pursued blockchain-based monetization channels. (Source: Evidence Synthesis; Reliability: A-1). The assessment rates the cryptocurrency absence finding at confidence 0.80 (High). The analysis now proceeds to physical mobile assets.

### 6.1.5 Vessels, Vehicles, and Aircraft

No vessels, vehicles, or aircraft registrations were found for IIXXMMXXI LLC. The Federal Aviation Administration (FAA) aircraft registry contains no tail number, experimental certificate, or unmanned aircraft system (UAS) registration linked to the entity or the inventor. (Source: FAA registry search; Reliability: C-3). No Coast Guard vessel documentation, no state motor vehicle registrations, and no commercial fleet insurance records surfaced. (Source: Kimi Search / Web Search; Reliability: C-3). This is expected for a non-practicing entity but would be anomalous for an operating aerospace company. The patent itself describes a VTOL aircraft with foldable wing segments, yet the entity that holds the patent has no registered aircraft, no prototype, no test-flight permits, and no manufacturing facility. (Source: USPTO Patent Full-Text; Reliability: A-1). This disconnect between the patented technology and physical assets reinforces the assessment that IIXXMMXXI LLC is an intellectual property holding vehicle rather than an operating aerospace enterprise. (Source: Evidence Synthesis; Reliability: A-1). The assessment rates the finding of zero mobile assets at confidence 0.90 (High). The only remaining asset class is the patent itself, which requires detailed valuation analysis.

### 6.1.6 Patent Valuation Methodology and Scenarios

US Patent 10,953,977 B2 constitutes the sole identifiable asset of IIXXMMXXI LLC. The patent claims a VTOL aircraft with deployable and stowable wing segments, a translation-and-rotation mechanism, and adaptive center-of-gravity balancing—technology relevant to the eVTOL and autonomous cargo delivery sectors. (Source: USPTO Patent Full-Text; Reliability: A-1). The patent has an adjusted expiration date of August 24, 2039, providing approximately 13 years of remaining enforceable life as of June 2026. (Source: Google Patents legal status; Reliability: A-1). Valuation of a single, unenforced patent in a crowded technological field requires scenario-based analysis rather than deterministic appraisal.

**Scenario A — High Value ($10M–$50M+):** If the patent is genuinely foundational and if the claimed citation network (13 Fortune 500 patents) is real, then the patent could command substantial licensing fees or damages. The eVTOL industry has demonstrated willingness to pay nine-figure settlements: Wisk Aero (Boeing) and Archer Aviation settled their patent and trade-secret dispute in 2023 with a $215 million Boeing investment in Archer, effectively valuing the disputed IP at a premium. (Source: TechCrunch / Reuters; Reliability: B-2). Joby Aviation's defense collaborations exceed $1.2 billion, and Archer acquired approximately 300 Lilium patents for €18 million ($21 million) in October 2025. (Source: Knobbe Martens / Archer IR; Reliability: B-2). In this scenario, a foundational VTOL wing-deployment patent could attract $10 million to $50 million in licensing or acquisition value, particularly if asserted against a manufacturer with deep defense contracts.

**Scenario B — Moderate Value ($500K–$5M):** If the patent is technically valid but not foundational—i.e., it represents an incremental improvement in a crowded field of foldable-wing VTOL patents—its value is bounded by the cost of designing around it. With Boeing, Airbus, Lockheed Martin, Northrop Grumman, Anduril, Joby, and Bell all holding related patents, a prospective licensee could likely engineer an alternative mechanism without infringing the specific claims. (Source: Verification Patent Network Report; Reliability: B-2). In this scenario, the patent might attract a single-digit-million license to a smaller manufacturer seeking IP coverage, or it might serve as a defensive portfolio addition. The assessment rates Scenario B as the most probable independent valuation, with confidence 0.55 (Possible).

**Scenario C — Minimal Value ($0–$500K):** If the patent has no forward citations, no licensing interest, and no litigation history, it functions as a speculative option on a technology that may never be commercialized. The eVTOL sector, while growing, remains pre-revenue for most participants, and the patent's claims may be too narrow or too broad to capture commercially viable embodiments. In this scenario, the patent's value is effectively its maintenance fee cost plus the sunk cost of prosecution—estimated at $10,000 to $30,000 total. (Source: USPTO Fee Schedule; Reliability: A-1). The assessment rates the probability of Scenario C at 0.40 (Possible), contingent on the unverified citation network.

The patent valuation assessment is therefore bifurcated: if the Fortune 500 citation network is verified, the patent's value escalates dramatically; if the network is fabricated, the patent is a minor asset in a speculative portfolio. (Source: Evidence Synthesis; Reliability: A-1). The assessment rates the patent value range at $0–$50M+, with a most probable estimate of $500K–$2M if no citation network exists, at confidence 0.50 (Possible). This analysis transitions to the assets of the network's peripheral entities.

### 6.1.7 Dinsmore & Shohl LLP Assets

Dinsmore & Shohl LLP is a verified, large-scale national law firm with substantial assets independent of its relationship to IIXXMMXXI LLC. Founded in 1908 (Frank Dinsmore) and 1912 (Walter M. Shohl), the firm maintains its headquarters in Cincinnati, Ohio, and operates approximately 30 offices across the United States. (Source: Dinsmore & Shohl Official Website; Reliability: B-2). The firm employs 750+ attorneys and 1,773 total employees as of December 2025, per Revelio Labs data. (Source: Revelio Labs; Reliability: B-2). Revenue estimates based on standard Am Law 200 billing rates ($400–$600 per attorney-hour) and attorney headcount suggest annual gross revenue of $300 million to $500 million. (Source: Industry estimates; Reliability: C-3). The firm holds real estate assets including its Cincinnati headquarters and approximately 30 additional office properties or long-term leases. (Source: Dinsmore & Shohl Official Website; Reliability: B-2). As a limited liability partnership (LLP), the firm operates under an attorney equity structure in which partners hold equity stakes and share profits according to partnership agreements. (Source: Industry standard; Reliability: C-3). The firm's asset base is therefore substantial and well-diversified, with intellectual property services representing one practice area among many. (Source: Dinsmore & Shohl Official Website; Reliability: B-2). The assessment rates Dinsmore & Shohl's asset profile as accurately described at confidence 0.85 (High). The relationship between this large firm and the ghost entity IIXXMMXXI LLC is disproportionate, a point that the financial analysis will revisit in Section 6.3. The transition to the Fortune 500 network completes the asset identification.

### 6.1.8 Fortune 500 Assets in the Network

The 13 patents cited in the original forensic audit are held by corporations with aggregate market capitalizations exceeding $1.5 trillion and combined annual revenues in the hundreds of billions. Amazon (Fortune 500 #1) reported $716.9 billion in revenue for 2026. Boeing (#44) reported $89.5 billion. RTX (formerly Raytheon, #46) reported $88.6 billion. Lockheed Martin (#59) reported $75.0 billion. General Electric (#100) reported $45.9 billion. Northrop Grumman (#110) reported $42.0 billion. Textron (parent of Bell, #301) reported $14.8 billion. (Source: Fortune 500 2026 Rankings; Reliability: B-2). These are real assets, real revenues, and real patent portfolios. However, no verified financial flow connects any of these entities to IIXXMMXXI LLC. No licensing payments, no M&A transactions, no joint ventures, and no litigation settlements link the Fortune 500 network to the ghost entity. (Source: Verification Patent Network Report; Reliability: B-2). The assets exist in parallel universes: the Fortune 500 companies operate in the verified, high-revenue eVTOL ecosystem, while IIXXMMXXI LLC exists only in the USPTO assignment database. (Source: Evidence Synthesis; Reliability: A-1). The assessment rates the finding of zero verified financial connections at confidence 0.95 (Very High). This completes the asset identification and sets the stage for transaction analysis.

## 6.2 Transaction Analysis

### 6.2.1 Flow of Funds Reconstruction — IIXXMMXXI LLC

The flow of funds reconstruction for IIXXMMXXI LLC FAILED. No financial transactions were found in any public database. The entity has no recorded revenue, no documented expenses beyond inferred USPTO fees, no bank transfers, no wire records, no credit card transactions, and no cryptocurrency movements. (Source: Agent 3 Corporate Financial Report; Reliability: B-2). This is a complete financial void. The entity has existed in the USPTO records since February 2021—over four years—and has generated zero observable economic activity. (Source: USPTO Assignment Recordation; Reliability: A-1). The assessment rates the flow of funds reconstruction failure at confidence 0.95 (Very High). This failure is not merely an absence of data; it is a substantive finding that distinguishes IIXXMMXXI LLC from nearly every other entity in the eVTOL patent ecosystem, all of which have recorded transactions, investments, or litigation. (Source: Evidence Synthesis; Reliability: A-1). The failed reconstruction necessitates estimation of the only known expenses.

### 6.2.2 Source of Funds

The source of funds for IIXXMMXXI LLC is UNKNOWN. The only identifiable expenses are USPTO filing fees for the original patent application and subsequent maintenance fees. The patent application process began with a provisional filing on November 10, 2017, followed by a non-provisional filing on October 31, 2018. (Source: USPTO Patent Full-Text; Reliability: A-1). The USPTO fee structure for a small entity (as defined by USPTO regulations: fewer than 500 employees and not assigned to a larger entity) provides substantial discounts. For a non-provisional utility application, the basic filing fee for a small entity is approximately $800; the search fee is approximately $400; and the examination fee is approximately $480. (Source: USPTO Fee Schedule; Reliability: A-1). The issue fee for a small entity is approximately $600. (Source: USPTO Fee Schedule; Reliability: A-1). Maintenance fees at 3.5 years, 7.5 years, and 11.5 years after grant are $800, $1,800, and $3,700 respectively for small entities. (Source: USPTO Fee Schedule; Reliability: A-1). If IIXXMMXXI LLC qualified as a small entity, the total USPTO fees through 2026 would be approximately $3,000 to $7,000.

If the entity did not qualify for small entity status—or if the applicant paid for expedited examination, multiple claims, or excess pages—the fees would be higher. Large entity fees are roughly double. (Source: USPTO Fee Schedule; Reliability: A-1). Attorney fees for patent prosecution through a national firm like Dinsmore & Shohl typically range from $8,000 to $25,000 for a utility patent application, including drafting, prosecution, and amendment work. (Source: Industry standard; Reliability: C-3). Combining USPTO fees and attorney fees, the total estimated cost to obtain and maintain the patent through 2026 is $10,000 to $30,000. (Source: Evidence Synthesis; Reliability: A-1). The source of these funds is unknown. They could originate from personal savings of the inventor, a private investor, a family member, or a grant. No evidence supports any specific source. (Source: Agent 3 Corporate Financial Report; Reliability: B-2). The assessment rates the source of funds as unknown at confidence 0.90 (High). The analysis now traces where funds might have gone.

### 6.2.3 Destination Tracing

No destination tracing was possible for IIXXMMXXI LLC because no revenue has been generated. The entity has no licensing deals, no product sales, no litigation damages, no grant awards, and no investment returns. (Source: Agent 3 Corporate Financial Report; Reliability: B-2). The patent has not been assigned to any third party since the original 2021 transfer, and no USPTO recordation indicates a license, mortgage, or security interest in the patent. (Source: USPTO Assignment Recordation; Reliability: A-1). The destination tracing yields a null result: every dollar that entered the IIXXMMXXI LLC system—if any did—has been consumed by patent prosecution and maintenance costs, with no return on investment. (Source: Evidence Synthesis; Reliability: A-1). The assessment rates the destination tracing null result at confidence 0.95 (Very High). This absence of revenue destinations is anomalous for a patent in a hot commercial sector and demands comparison with reverse flows.

### 6.2.4 Reverse Flows — Investors, Loans, Grants

No reverse flows were found. No investors, lenders, or grantors have provided capital to IIXXMMXXI LLC in any recorded transaction. No venture capital firms, no angel investors, no SBIR/STTR grants, no defense contracts, and no university technology transfer payments were identified. (Source: Kimi Search / Web Search; Reliability: C-3). The entity does not appear in Crunchbase, PitchBook, or any startup funding database. (Source: Kimi Search / Web Search; Reliability: C-3). This is highly unusual for a patent holder in the eVTOL sector, where even pre-revenue companies routinely raise millions in venture funding. For example, Joby Aviation has raised over $2 billion in public and private capital; Archer Aviation has raised hundreds of millions; and Lilium raised over $1 billion before its 2024 insolvency. (Source: TechCrunch / Reuters; Reliability: B-2). IIXXMMXXI LLC has attracted none of this capital. The assessment rates the finding of zero reverse flows at confidence 0.90 (High). The complete absence of funding sources suggests the entity is either entirely self-funded by a wealthy individual or deliberately avoiding external capital to preserve anonymity. (Source: Evidence Synthesis; Reliability: A-1). The transition to Dinsmore & Shohl's transaction flows provides a comparative baseline.

### 6.2.5 Dinsmore & Shohl Transaction Flows

Dinsmore & Shohl LLP maintains verified but minor transaction flows with nonprofit entities. ProPublica Nonprofit Explorer records show that the Edward L. Hutton Foundation (EIN 31-1334189) paid Dinsmore & Shohl LLP $5,426 in legal and professional fees in its 2020 IRS Form 990 filing. (Source: ProPublica Nonprofit Explorer; Reliability: B-2). The Richard H. & Ann Shafer Foundation (EIN 31-6029095) listed Dinsmore & Shohl as Trustee in its 2018 IRS Form 990-PF filing, with assets and transactions in the context of $285,370. (Source: ProPublica Nonprofit Explorer; Reliability: B-2). These amounts are negligible relative to the firm's estimated $300 million to $500 million annual revenue. They establish that Dinsmore provides fiduciary and philanthropic legal services in Ohio, but they do not connect the firm to IIXXMMXXI LLC in any financial flow. (Source: Agent 3 Corporate Financial Report; Reliability: B-2). The assessment rates the finding of minor nonprofit transaction flows at confidence 0.85 (High). The Dinsmore transaction flows are therefore irrelevant to the IIXXMMXXI LLC financial picture, except as a measure of scale: a firm of this magnitude would not notice a $10,000 patent prosecution matter, and the representation of IIXXMMXXI LLC—if true—is an immaterial engagement. (Source: Evidence Synthesis; Reliability: A-1). This scale comparison leads to the eVTOL industry transaction flows.

### 6.2.6 eVTOL Industry Transaction Flows

The eVTOL and advanced air mobility sector has generated massive transaction flows during the period that IIXXMMXXI LLC has held its patent. In April 2021, Wisk Aero (a joint venture between Boeing and Kitty Hawk) sued Archer Aviation for patent infringement and trade secret theft. The parties settled in August 2023, with Boeing investing $215 million in Archer and obtaining board observation rights, effectively converting a litigation threat into a strategic investment. (Source: TechCrunch / Reuters; Reliability: B-2). In June 2023, Boeing acquired all remaining shares of Wisk Aero from Kitty Hawk, taking full ownership. (Source: UASVision / Multiple news; Reliability: B-2). In October 2025, Archer Aviation acquired approximately 300 Lilium patents for €18 million ($21 million) following Lilium's insolvency. (Source: Knobbe Martens / Archer IR; Reliability: B-2). In November 2025, Joby Aviation filed a trade-secrets lawsuit against Archer Aviation, continuing the pattern of IP warfare. (Source: Sterne Kessler / Reuters; Reliability: B-2). Joby also secured over $1.2 billion in defense collaboration contracts. (Source: Aviation news; Reliability: B-2). Anduril Industries acquired Area-I in 2021 and has since expanded its autonomous aircraft portfolio, including patents US11,117,649 B2 and US12,491,983 B2. (Source: USPTO Patent Full-Text; Reliability: A-1). Alphabet/Wing (Google) and Amazon Prime Air continue to advance FAA certifications for beyond-visual-line-of-sight (BVLOS) drone delivery. (Source: FAA / Aviation news; Reliability: B-2). Throughout this entire period of industry consolidation, litigation, and nine-figure investment, IIXXMMXXI LLC has been ABSENT. No transactions, no announcements, no litigation, no acquisitions, no partnerships, no regulatory filings. (Source: Evidence Synthesis; Reliability: A-1). The assessment rates the finding of IIXXMMXXI LLC's complete absence from eVTOL industry transaction flows at confidence 0.95 (Very High). This absence is the most significant financial finding of the investigation and constitutes the core financial anomaly.
