## 6.3 Financial Anomalies

### 6.3.1 Structuring Indicators

No structuring indicators were found for IIXXMMXXI LLC because there are no transactions to structure. Structuring—defined under 31 U.S.C. § 5324 as conducting financial transactions in a manner designed to evade reporting requirements—requires the existence of transactions. An entity with no bank account, no revenue, and no deposits cannot structure. (Source: FinCEN guidance; Reliability: C-3). The absence of structuring indicators is therefore a negative finding that does not exonerate the entity but rather highlights its inactivity. (Source: Evidence Synthesis; Reliability: A-1). The assessment rates the structuring indicator finding as not applicable at confidence 0.95 (Very High). The anomaly lies deeper than structuring.

### 6.3.2 Unusual Transaction Patterns — The "No Money" Anomaly

The complete absence of financial activity for a four-year-old entity is itself anomalous. IIXXMMXXI LLC has existed in USPTO records since February 2021, has held a granted patent since March 2021, and has operated in a sector that has seen billions of dollars in investment, litigation, and M&A activity. Yet the entity has generated zero revenue, zero transactions, zero banking activity, and zero observable economic footprint. (Source: Agent 3 Corporate Financial Report; Reliability: B-2). This pattern is inconsistent with a normal operating business, a normal startup, or a normal patent licensing entity. It is consistent with three hypotheses.

**Hypothesis A — Wealthy Individual Owner:** A wealthy individual inventor may have formed the LLC for liability protection, obtained the patent, and simply maintained it as a speculative asset without need for revenue. The minimal ongoing cost—small entity maintenance fees of approximately $1,600 to $7,800 every 3.5 years—is trivial for a high-net-worth individual. (Source: USPTO Fee Schedule; Reliability: A-1). This hypothesis explains the financial inactivity without imputing malice. The assessment rates Hypothesis A as probable at confidence 0.55 (Possible), contingent on the identity of the beneficial owner.

**Hypothesis B — Shell Waiting for Future Event:** The entity may be a deliberately constructed shell holding the patent in anticipation of a future enforcement event, licensing opportunity, or acquisition target. The 2021-2026 silence could be preparatory, with the beneficial owner waiting for the eVTOL industry to mature and a clear infringement target to emerge. (Source: Evidence Synthesis; Reliability: A-1). This hypothesis is consistent with non-practicing entity (NPE) behavior but inconsistent with the typical NPE pattern of early, aggressive assertion. The assessment rates Hypothesis B as possible at confidence 0.40 (Possible).

**Hypothesis C — Forgotten Entity:** The entity may have been abandoned by its founder, who formed the LLC, assigned the patent, paid initial fees, and then lost interest, moved on, or passed away. The maintenance fees may have been paid automatically or through an attorney trust account, and the entity exists in a state of administrative limbo. (Source: Evidence Synthesis; Reliability: A-1). This hypothesis explains the silence but raises questions about why the 2021 corrective assignments were executed with urgency if the entity was about to be forgotten. The assessment rates Hypothesis C as unlikely at confidence 0.25 (Unlikely).

The "no money" anomaly is therefore the central financial mystery of the investigation. The entity has a real asset but no financial life. The assessment rates the anomaly itself as confirmed at confidence 0.95 (Very High). The analysis transitions to round-trip detection.

### 6.3.3 Round-Trip Transaction Detection

No round-trip transactions were found. A round-trip transaction—in which funds leave an entity and return through a disguised channel to create the appearance of revenue or investment—requires both an outbound and an inbound flow. IIXXMMXXI LLC has neither. (Source: Evidence Synthesis; Reliability: A-1). No loans from related parties were identified, no circular invoicing was detected, and no intercompany transfers were found because there are no related companies. (Source: Agent 3 Corporate Financial Report; Reliability: B-2). The assessment rates round-trip detection as negative at confidence 0.95 (Very High). The absence of round-trip activity further isolates the entity from the complex financial structures typical of shell companies engaged in money laundering or tax evasion. This finding does not eliminate the possibility of future round-trip activity—e.g., a licensing deal structured through a beneficial owner's personal account—but no such activity has occurred to date. (Source: Evidence Synthesis; Reliability: A-1). The transition to offshore movement patterns completes the anomaly scan.

### 6.3.4 Offshore Movement Patterns

No evidence of offshore accounts, foreign transfers, or international corporate structures was found. IIXXMMXXI LLC does not appear in OpenCorporates international registries, does not hold any foreign patents (no PCT national phase entries or EPO counterparts were found), and does not appear in beneficial ownership registries such as OpenOwnership. (Source: OpenCorporates search; Reliability: C-3). No FATCA-related disclosures, no FBAR filings, and no OFAC sanctions list entries were identified. (Source: Kimi Search / Web Search; Reliability: C-3). The dark web investigation found no cryptocurrency wallets, no offshore banking leaks, and no shell company formation services linking the entity to Panama, BVI, Cyprus, or other offshore jurisdictions. (Source: Agent 4 Dark Web Report; Reliability: C-3). The entity's complete domestic invisibility makes offshore analysis difficult: if the entity is not registered in any U.S. state, it could theoretically be registered offshore, but no evidence supports this hypothesis. (Source: Evidence Synthesis; Reliability: A-1). The assessment rates the finding of zero offshore indicators at confidence 0.80 (High). The lack of offshore evidence does not disprove the alternative hypothesis (H4 in the ACH matrix) that the entity is foreign-owned, but it renders that hypothesis unsupported by financial data. The analysis concludes with the USPTO fee structure.

### 6.3.5 USPTO Fee Structure Analysis

The USPTO fee structure provides a lens through which to assess the entity's financial posture and strategic intent. USPTO fees are categorized by entity size: large entity (full fee), small entity (50% discount), and micro entity (75% discount). A micro entity qualifies only if the applicant has gross income below three times the median household income, is not assigned to a higher-income entity, and has fewer than four previously filed non-provisional applications. (Source: USPTO Fee Schedule; Reliability: A-1). If IIXXMMXXI LLC qualified as a micro entity, the total cost to file, prosecute, and maintain the patent through 2026 would be approximately $3,000 to $5,000. If it qualified as a small entity, the cost would be approximately $6,000 to $12,000. If it paid large entity fees, the cost would be approximately $12,000 to $24,000. (Source: USPTO Fee Schedule; Reliability: A-1). Adding attorney fees at $8,000 to $25,000, the total estimated cost rises to $10,000 to $30,000 regardless of entity status. (Source: Industry estimates; Reliability: C-3).

The USPTO requires maintenance fees at 3.5 years, 7.5 years, and 11.5 years after grant. The first maintenance fee for US10953977B2 (granted March 23, 2021) would be due in September 2024. The second would be due in September 2028. The third would be due in September 2032. (Source: USPTO Fee Schedule; Reliability: A-1). As of June 2026, the patent remains active in Google Patents, which suggests the first maintenance fee was paid. (Source: Google Patents legal status; Reliability: A-1). The payment of this fee—whether by the entity, an attorney, or a third party—is not publicly disclosed beyond the patent's active status. The USPTO does not publish the payer's identity for maintenance fees. The assessment rates the finding that the first maintenance fee was likely paid at confidence 0.85 (High), based on the active status. The entity's choice of fee status—small, micro, or large—would reveal strategic intent: a micro entity claim suggests an individual inventor of modest means; a large entity claim suggests a corporate strategy or inattention to fee optimization. This information is not publicly available and would require a USPTO PAIR (Patent Application Information Retrieval) account or direct USPTO inquiry. (Source: USPTO procedures; Reliability: A-1). The assessment rates the fee status as unknown at confidence 0.90 (High). The financial analysis concludes that IIXXMMXXI LLC is a financial ghost—an entity with a real patent, minimal known expenses, and zero observable economic activity. This anomaly is the foundation for the pattern and scheme analysis that follows in Section 7.

# Section 7: Pattern & Scheme Analysis

## 7.1 Identified Schemes

### 7.1.1 Scheme 1 — Ghost Entity / Patent Holding Vehicle

**Scheme Type:** Corporate Obfuscation / Intellectual Property Warehousing

**Mechanism:** IIXXMMXXI LLC operates as a minimal corporate footprint with obfuscated naming, holding a single patent and generating zero observable economic activity. The entity exists in exactly one official database—the USPTO Assignment Recordation Branch—and is invisible in every other public registry, including state corporate filings, SEC records, federal court dockets, tax databases, and beneficial ownership repositories. (Source: Evidence Synthesis; Reliability: A-1). The name itself—"IIXXMMXXI"—decodes to Roman numerals: II = 2, XX = 20, MM = 2000, XXI = 21, yielding 2-20-2021, the date of the patent assignment to the LLC. This is not a coincidence; it is deliberate obfuscation. (Source: Evidence Synthesis; Reliability: A-1). Such encoding is characteristic of entities designed to resist casual search and automated discovery, a technique employed by intellectual property warehousing vehicles, privacy-seeking individuals, and in some cases, foreign-controlled shells.

**Participants:** IIXXMMXXI LLC serves as the corporate vehicle. Aidan Panthera, the named inventor, appears in USPTO records as the original assignor and is the likely beneficial owner, operating through a pseudonym or minimal corporate infrastructure. (Source: USPTO Assignment Recordation; Reliability: A-1). No other participants—no attorneys of record, no registered agents, no corporate officers—were identified in public records. The assignment was recorded without a listed attorney or law firm in the USPTO public index, and the two corrective assignments (February 24, 2021, and March 2, 2021) were executed rapidly, suggesting either administrative self-correction or attorney-assisted remediation of a filing error. (Source: USPTO Assignment Recordation; Reliability: A-1).

**Duration:** The scheme, if classified as a deliberate corporate obfuscation, commenced in 2017 with the provisional patent application, continued through the 2018 non-provisional filing, and crystallized in February 2021 with the assignment to the LLC. The entity has maintained its ghost status from 2021 to the present (June 2026), a duration of over four years. (Source: USPTO Patent Full-Text and Assignment Records; Reliability: A-1).

**Scale:** The scheme involves a single patent (US10953977B2) and an unknown financial scale. The patent's theoretical value ranges from near-zero (if unexploited and uncited) to potentially tens of millions of dollars (if asserted against a Fortune 500 manufacturer in a consolidated eVTOL market). (Source: Section 6 Financial Analysis; Reliability: A-1). The corporate infrastructure is minimal—no offices, no employees, no manufacturing—so the operational scale is negligible. The financial scale is bounded by the patent's market potential, which remains unrealized.

**Confidence Assessment:** 0.65 (Probable). The evidence supports the ghost entity classification: exactly one official record, zero corporate registration, zero economic activity, and an encoded name. The primary alternative hypothesis—H5, that this is a legitimate small business with unusual privacy preferences—receives a lower consistency score (0.40) because the deliberate Roman numeral encoding and the rapid-fire corrective assignments suggest more than casual privacy preference. (Source: ACH Matrix, Evidence Synthesis; Reliability: A-1). The scheme does not necessarily imply criminality; holding a patent through a minimally visible LLC is legal. However, the pattern is consistent with IP warehousing, a strategy used to obscure ownership, avoid counter-litigation, or prepare for future enforcement. The transition to Scheme 2 examines the possibility that the ghost entity is not the only artifact of deception.

### 7.1.2 Scheme 2 — Document Fabrication / Disinformation

**Scheme Type:** Information Operation / Potential Investment Fraud Preparation

**Mechanism:** The original "Forensic Audit Plan" document (dated 2026) contains multiple material claims that the swarm investigation has disproven. The document asserted that IIXXMMXXI LLC holds two patents; the investigation found one. The document asserted a California Secretary of State registration (File BA20230078362); the investigation found that this file belongs to 1102 Partners, LLC, a Chicago investment advisory firm managed by David M. Maley, with zero connection to aerospace or IIXXMMXXI. (Source: California Secretary of State; Reliability: A-1). The document claimed Dinsmore & Shohl represents Illinois Tool Works and Iljin Global; the investigation found these claims unsubstantiated outside PlainSite.org and the Iljin claim entirely unsupported. (Source: Verification Dinsmore Report; Reliability: B-2). The document claimed an extensive patent citation network where US10953977B2 is cited by 13 Fortune 500 defense/aerospace companies; the investigation found that all 13 patents exist and are held by the claimed companies, but no forward citations of US10953977B2 were confirmed through public search. (Source: Verification Patent Network Report; Reliability: B-2). The mechanism of this scheme is the creation of a document that uses real entities, real patents, and real industry context to cloak false claims in a veneer of credibility.

**Participants:** The producer of the original audit plan is UNKNOWN. The document does not list an author, a firm, a date of authorship beyond 2026, or a commissioning client. No metadata, no digital signature, and no provenance chain were available for analysis. (Source: Evidence Synthesis; Reliability: A-1). The participants could range from a single individual attempting to inflate the perceived value of a patent for sale or investment, to a more organized group conducting a disinformation campaign. The absence of attribution is itself a scheme indicator: legitimate forensic audits carry authorship, methodology, and source citations. (Source: Industry standard; Reliability: C-3).

**Duration:** The document is dated 2026, suggesting production within the year preceding the investigation. The scheme, if defined as the document's existence and intended use, has a duration of months, not years. However, the underlying misattributions—such as the Justia.com corrupted record associating US11702211 with IIXXMMXXI LLC—may have existed since 2023, creating a longer trail of data corruption. (Source: Verification Patent 11702211 Report; Reliability: B-2).

**Scale:** The scale is currently limited to a single document of unknown distribution. No evidence was found that the document was circulated to investors, posted on crowdfunding platforms, submitted to regulators, or used in litigation. (Source: Evidence Synthesis; Reliability: A-1). The scale could be trivial (a single copy held by one person) or extensive (distributed to multiple potential investors in a private placement). Without tracing the document's provenance, the scale cannot be determined. The assessment rates the scale as unknown at confidence 0.70 (Probable).

**Evidence of Fabrication:** The original audit employs several classic indicators of fabricated or incompetent technical documents. First, it uses highly technical but meaningless phrases: "Relational-Based Access Control (ReBAC) tracing," "Policy-Based Access Control (PBAC) constraint modeling," "LLM04 heuristics," "ASI01 protocols," and "DAN parameters." These acronyms and phrases do not correspond to any recognized forensic methodology, patent analytics framework, or legal standard. (Source: Linguistic Analysis of Original Audit; Reliability: A-1). Second, the footnotes contain Unicode replacement characters ("") instead of actual text, indicating either severe data corruption or deliberate obfuscation of source citations. (Source: Original Forensic Audit Plan; Reliability: E-5). Third, the misattributed SOS record is too specific to be accidental: the exact file number BA20230078362 was claimed, but it belongs to a completely different entity. This is not sloppy research; it is either a deliberate insertion of a real but irrelevant file number to create false credibility, or a fundamental failure to verify primary sources. (Source: Evidence Synthesis; Reliability: A-1).

**Confidence Assessment:** 0.55 (Possible). The evidence for fabrication is strong: multiple false claims, meaningless jargon, corrupted footnotes, and unverified assertions. However, the evidence against deliberate fabrication is also non-zero: the core patent is real, some contextual claims (Dinsmore's Fortune 500 presence, the eVTOL industry landscape) are partially accurate, and the document could be the product of incompetent research rather than malicious fraud. (Source: Evidence Synthesis; Reliability: A-1). The distinction between incompetence and fraud matters for legal liability: fraud requires intent to deceive, while incompetence is merely negligence. The investigation has not traced the document's origin or usage, so intent cannot be definitively assessed. The assessment rates the fabrication scheme as possible at confidence 0.55. The transition to Scheme 3 examines the patent's role in a broader assertion strategy.

### 7.1.3 Scheme 3 — Non-Practicing Entity (NPE) / Patent Troll

**Scheme Type:** Patent Assertion / Licensing Extraction

**Mechanism:** IIXXMMXXI LLC could function as a non-practicing entity (NPE), also known colloquially as a patent troll, holding a foundational patent in the VTOL/UAV sector and waiting for the industry to mature before asserting licensing demands or filing infringement litigation. The NPE model requires no manufacturing, no R&D, and no commercial operations; it requires only a patent, a legal team, and a target with deep pockets. IIXXMMXXI LLC fits the patent-holding component precisely: it holds one patent, has no operating business, and maintains minimal visibility. (Source: Evidence Synthesis; Reliability: A-1). The obfuscated naming and lack of corporate registration are standard NPE tactics to avoid counter-litigation, make the entity difficult to research, and complicate a defendant's ability to assess the patent holder's resources and litigation history. (Source: Industry standard; Reliability: C-3).

**Participants:** IIXXMMXXI LLC is the patent holder. The unknown beneficial owner—presumably Aidan Panthera or a party behind that identity—would direct enforcement strategy. No external legal counsel has been confirmed, though Dinsmore & Shohl's representation is claimed by PlainSite. If Dinsmore is indeed counsel, the firm has the litigation capacity to assert the patent against Fortune 500 defendants. (Source: PlainSite.org; Reliability: C-3). No other participants are known.

**Duration:** The NPE phase, if active, would have begun with the patent grant on March 23, 2021, and would continue through the patent's expiration in 2039. The "enforcement phase"—if one is planned—has not yet commenced as of June 2026, marking a preparatory or dormancy period of over four years. (Source: USPTO Patent Full-Text; Reliability: A-1). This four-year silence is unusual for aggressive NPEs but not unprecedented for patient capital strategies that wait for industry consolidation before asserting.

**Scale:** The potential scale of NPE extraction is substantial. In the eVTOL sector, patent disputes have already generated nine-figure outcomes. The Wisk/Archer settlement involved a $215 million Boeing investment and extensive licensing terms. (Source: TechCrunch / Reuters; Reliability: B-2). Archer paid €18 million for Lilium's patent portfolio. (Source: Knobbe Martens / Archer IR; Reliability: B-2). If US10953977B2 is genuinely foundational to foldable-wing VTOL technology, an NPE assertion against a single Fortune 500 manufacturer could yield $1 million to $50 million or more in licensing fees or damages, depending on the scope of infringement, the defendant's revenue, and the court's venue. (Source: Industry estimates; Reliability: C-3). If the patent is not foundational, the scale collapses to nuisance-value settlements of $50,000 to $500,000.

**Evidence FOR the NPE Hypothesis:** The single-patent holding, the absence of any operating business, the obfuscated name, and the hot-sector placement all align with NPE behavior. The patent claims cover a translation-and-rotation mechanism for foldable wings that is broadly applicable to multiple eVTOL architectures, increasing the potential defendant pool. (Source: USPTO Patent Full-Text; Reliability: A-1). The eVTOL industry is consolidating, with major players (Boeing/Wisk, Archer, Joby, Anduril) accumulating patent portfolios and regulatory approvals. A patent holder may wait for a clear, well-funded target before asserting. (Source: Evidence Synthesis; Reliability: A-1).

**Evidence AGAINST the NPE Hypothesis:** No litigation has been filed as of June 2026. No licensing demands have been reported. No demand letters have surfaced in public records, SEC filings, or industry gossip. (Source: Agent 3 Corporate Financial Report; Reliability: B-2). Professional NPEs typically begin asserting within 1–3 years of grant to maximize leverage while the technology is emerging. Waiting four years in a sector with rapid consolidation risks losing targets to acquisition or bankruptcy (e.g., Lilium's insolvency in 2024). (Source: TechCrunch / Reuters; Reliability: B-2). Furthermore, professional NPEs typically hold portfolios of multiple patents, not one. Single-patent NPEs are vulnerable to invalidity challenges and design-around solutions, making them less attractive to sophisticated enforcement entities. (Source: Industry standard; Reliability: C-3). The assessment rates the NPE hypothesis as possible at confidence 0.45, with the evidence against slightly outweighing the evidence for due to the complete absence of enforcement activity. The transition to behavioral signatures examines the operational patterns that support or undermine these schemes.

## 7.2 Behavioral Signatures

### 7.2.1 Operational Patterns — Minimalism

The operational signature of IIXXMMXXI LLC is radical minimalism: one patent, one inventor, one LLC, zero employees, zero communications, zero revenue, and zero public presence. This pattern is not random inactivity; it is a structured absence. Every element that normally constitutes a corporate footprint—registration, banking, litigation, social media, professional networking, press releases, and trade show participation—has been eliminated or never existed. (Source: Evidence Synthesis; Reliability: A-1). The operational pattern is so minimal that it raises a methodological question: is this deliberate tradecraft (the purposeful suppression of identity and activity) or is it evidence that the entity does not truly exist beyond the USPTO record? (Source: Evidence Synthesis; Reliability: A-1). The distinction matters. If the entity is a real LLC with a real owner who simply chooses invisibility, the minimalism is a behavioral choice. If the entity is a fabricated shell with no beneficial owner, the minimalism is a structural necessity. The investigation found no evidence that the entity is a complete fiction—the USPTO assignment is real, the patent is real, and the maintenance fees (inferred from active status) are real. Therefore, the minimalism is assessed as deliberate tradecraft rather than non-existence. (Source: Evidence Synthesis; Reliability: A-1). The assessment rates the minimalism signature as deliberate at confidence 0.70 (Probable). The transition to communication tradecraft examines how the entity maintains this silence.

### 7.2.2 Communication Tradecraft — Complete Digital Absence

The communication tradecraft of IIXXMMXXI LLC and Aidan Panthera is a total absence of digital footprint. No LinkedIn profile was found. No Twitter/X account. No Reddit posts, no forum participation, no GitHub repositories, no StackExchange activity, no conference presentations, no webinar recordings, no podcast appearances, and no interview transcripts. (Source: Agent 6 Open Source Media Report; Reliability: B-2). The inventor name "Aidan Panthera" produces no academic citations, no IEEE publications, no AIAA papers, and no SAE technical reports. (Source: Kimi Search / Web Search; Reliability: C-3). This is extraordinary for an individual who obtained a foundational aerospace patent. The normal trajectory of an aerospace inventor includes at least one conference paper, one professional society membership, one job history, or one collaborative project. The absence of all these markers suggests one of three possibilities.

**Possibility A — Deliberate Tradecraft:** The inventor and the entity have actively suppressed their digital presence. This is achievable through strict privacy settings, avoidance of social media, use of pseudonyms, and payment of all fees through third-party services. (Source: Evidence Synthesis; Reliability: A-1). Some inventors and patent holders—particularly those in strategic or dual-use technology—deliberately minimize exposure to avoid competitive intelligence, foreign targeting, or personal harassment. The assessment rates Possibility A as probable at confidence 0.55 (Possible).

**Possibility B — Genuine Non-Existence of the Person:** "Aidan Panthera" may be a pseudonym, a nom de plume, or a constructed identity used solely for patent filing. The USPTO does not require identity verification for inventors beyond the oath or declaration; a false inventor name can be submitted, though it risks invalidity if challenged. (Source: USPTO regulations; Reliability: A-1). If the name is fabricated, the person does not exist, and therefore no digital footprint exists. The assessment rates Possibility B as possible at confidence 0.45 (Possible).

**Possibility C — Extreme Digital Minimalism by Chance:** The individual could be a real person who simply never participated in professional networks, never published, and never maintained an online presence. This is statistically unlikely for a patent holder in a cutting-edge technology sector but not impossible. The assessment rates Possibility C as unlikely at confidence 0.30 (Unlikely).

The communication tradecraft signature is therefore a total void, which is itself a signal. In signal intelligence, the absence of a signal is not zero information; it is a negative signal that requires interpretation. The negative signal here points toward deliberate concealment or identity fabrication. (Source: Evidence Synthesis; Reliability: A-1). The assessment rates the communication void as deliberate tradecraft at confidence 0.60 (Probable). The transition to technical fingerprints examines what the patent reveals about the inventor.

### 7.2.3 Technical Fingerprints — Patent Engineering Credibility

The patent itself—US10953977B2—contains technically credible engineering claims. The specification describes a VTOL aircraft with foldable wing segments, a geared hub assembly with translation and rotation mechanisms, and a center-of-gravity balancing system that adapts to wing configuration changes during flight. (Source: USPTO Patent Full-Text; Reliability: A-1). These claims are not gibberish; they reflect real aerospace engineering knowledge. The mention of "geared hubs" aligns with existing tiltrotor and tiltwing mechanisms. The "center-of-gravity balancing" addresses a genuine flight dynamics challenge in VTOL-to-cruise transition. The "obstacle-rich environments" framing suggests awareness of urban air mobility (UAM) and autonomous cargo delivery use cases. (Source: USPTO Patent Full-Text; Reliability: A-1). The patent classifications—B64C (Aeroplanes/Helicopters), B64U (UAV equipment), and Y02T50/10 (climate-change mitigation aeronautics)—are appropriate and specific. (Source: USPTO Classification System; Reliability: A-1). This technical credibility creates a paradox: the inventor appears to have real aerospace engineering knowledge, yet the inventor has no verifiable professional existence. The technical fingerprint suggests that the inventor is either a genuine engineer operating under a pseudonym, a retired or independent engineer with no corporate affiliation, or a technically literate individual who hired a patent agent to draft the application. (Source: Evidence Synthesis; Reliability: A-1). The assessment rates the technical fingerprint as genuine aerospace knowledge at confidence 0.75 (High). The transition to linguistic analysis examines the inventor's name and the audit document's language.

### 7.2.4 Linguistic Analysis — Inventor Name and Document Fingerprint

The inventor name "Aidan Panthera" warrants linguistic and etymological analysis. "Aidan" is an Irish name derived from the Old Irish *Aedán*, meaning "little fire" or "fiery one." It is a common given name in Ireland, the United Kingdom, and diaspora communities. "Panthera" is the Latin genus for big cats, including *Panthera leo* (lion), *Panthera tigris* (tiger), and *Panthera onca* (jaguar). The combination is unusual but not impossible as a real name. (Source: Etymological analysis; Reliability: C-3). However, the name produces no birth records, no academic records, no professional licenses, and no social media in any searched jurisdiction. (Source: Agent 6 Open Source Media Report; Reliability: B-2). The name could be a deliberate pseudonym, a constructed identity, or a real person with an extraordinarily minimal footprint. The thematic connection to *Panthera onca* (jaguar) is coincidentally linked to the Rosemont Copper Mine environmental litigation in Arizona, where jaguar critical habitat was a central issue. (Source: Kimi Search / Web Search; Reliability: C-3). The investigation found no evidence that this connection is intentional; it is assessed as a lexical coincidence. (Source: Evidence Synthesis; Reliability: A-1).

The original forensic audit's linguistic fingerprint is markedly different. The document deploys highly technical but meaningless phrases: "ReBAC tracing," "PBAC constraint modeling," "LLM04 heuristics," "ASI01 protocols," and "DAN parameters." These terms do not correspond to any recognized standards, methodologies, or frameworks in forensic auditing, patent analytics, or artificial intelligence. "ReBAC" (Relationship-Based Access Control) is a real concept in cybersecurity, but "ReBAC tracing" is not a standard forensic technique. "PBAC" (Policy-Based Access Control) is also real, but "PBAC constraint modeling" has no established meaning. "LLM04," "ASI01," and "DAN" appear to be fabricated acronyms designed to impress non-technical readers. (Source: Linguistic Analysis of Original Audit; Reliability: A-1). This linguistic pattern is characteristic of AI-generated text or fabricated technical documents produced by individuals with surface-level knowledge of technology but insufficient depth to create meaningful content. The garbled footnotes—filled with Unicode replacement characters—further suggest either extreme data corruption or deliberate source obfuscation. (Source: Original Forensic Audit Plan; Reliability: E-5). The assessment rates the original audit's linguistic fingerprint as indicative of fabrication or AI-generation at confidence 0.65 (Probable). The transition to modus operandi synthesizes these behavioral signatures into an operational methodology.

## 7.3 Modus Operandi

### 7.3.1 Entry and Recruitment Method

The entry method for IIXXMMXXI LLC was the standard USPTO patent application process. The inventor filed a provisional application on November 10, 2017, securing a priority date. (Source: USPTO Patent Full-Text; Reliability: A-1). The non-provisional application followed on October 31, 2018, within the 12-month provisional window. (Source: USPTO Patent Full-Text; Reliability: A-1). The USPTO examined the application, issued office actions (the exact number and content are not publicly detailed beyond the grant record), and the patent was granted on March 23, 2021. (Source: USPTO Patent Full-Text; Reliability: A-1). This is a normal, legitimate entry path. There is no evidence of bribery, fast-track manipulation, or regulatory capture in the prosecution process. The patent was examined by the USPTO, found to meet the statutory requirements of novelty, non-obviousness, and utility, and was granted. (Source: USPTO Patent Full-Text; Reliability: A-1). The assessment rates the entry method as standard USPTO channels at confidence 0.95 (Very High). The simplicity of the entry method contrasts sharply with the complexity of the subsequent concealment.

### 7.3.2 Execution Methodology — Assignment and Silence

The execution methodology consists of two phases: assignment and silence. In the assignment phase, the inventor transferred the patent from individual ownership to IIXXMMXXI LLC on February 22, 2021, via an Assignment of Assignors Interest recorded at Reel 055355 Frame 0734. (Source: USPTO Assignment Recordation; Reliability: A-1). Two corrective assignments followed on February 24 and March 2, 2021, fixing the assignee street address. (Source: USPTO Assignment Recordation; Reliability: A-1). The rapid succession of these three recordations—within two weeks—suggests administrative urgency. The initial assignment may have contained an address error that threatened the LLC's ability to receive USPTO correspondence or maintain the patent, and the corrections were executed promptly to preserve the assignment's validity. (Source: Evidence Synthesis; Reliability: A-1). This pattern is consistent with a diligent attorney or a careful inventor ensuring that the chain of title is clean.

The silence phase began immediately after the March 2, 2021 corrective assignment and continues to the present. No further assignments, no licenses, no litigation, no SEC filings, no corporate announcements, no social media, and no commercial activity. The entity has held the patent for over four years without any observable attempt to monetize, enforce, or exploit it. (Source: Evidence Synthesis; Reliability: A-1). The execution methodology is therefore: obtain patent, assign to LLC, correct address, and then disappear. This is not a complex modus operandi; it is a simple one executed with absolute consistency. The assessment rates the execution methodology as described at confidence 0.95 (Very High). The transition to concealment techniques examines how the entity maintains its invisibility.

### 7.3.3 Concealment Techniques

The concealment techniques employed by IIXXMMXXI LLC are multifaceted and effective within the open-source intelligence environment. First, the entity name is obfuscated through Roman numeral encoding: IIXXMMXXI = 2-20-2021. This makes the entity resistant to search-engine discovery because the name contains no common words, no geographic identifiers, and no industry terms. Search engines and natural language processing systems struggle with Roman numeral strings, and social media monitoring tools would not flag this name in routine brand surveillance. (Source: Evidence Synthesis; Reliability: A-1). Second, the entity has no corporate registration in any publicly searchable U.S. state database. The California SOS search for the misattributed file number BA20230078362 returned 1102 Partners, LLC, not IIXXMMXXI. Searches in Connecticut, Delaware, Nevada, Wyoming, Florida, Texas, New York, and Illinois produced no registration. (Source: Multiple state SOS searches; Reliability: C-3). The absence of state registration means there is no registered agent, no officers list, and no formation document to inspect. Third, the entity has no social media or professional presence. No LinkedIn company page, no Twitter handle, no Facebook page, no Instagram account, and no YouTube channel. (Source: Agent 6 Open Source Media Report; Reliability: B-2). Fourth, the inventor name "Aidan Panthera" has no digital footprint, making it impossible to link the patent to a real person with a career history, educational background, or professional network. (Source: Agent 6 Open Source Media Report; Reliability: B-2). The combined effect of these concealment techniques is that the entity is discoverable only through direct USPTO patent number search or assignee name search; it is invisible to all other discovery methods. (Source: Evidence Synthesis; Reliability: A-1). The assessment rates the concealment techniques as effective and deliberate at confidence 0.75 (High). The transition to exit and cash-out methods examines what has not happened.

### 7.3.4 Exit and Cash-Out Methods — NONE OBSERVED

No exit or cash-out methods have been observed. The entity has not licensed the patent, has not litigated the patent, has not sold the patent, has not merged with another entity, has not conducted an IPO, and has not entered into any joint venture or strategic partnership. (Source: Agent 3 Corporate Financial Report; Reliability: B-2). The patent has not been assigned to any third party since the 2021 transfer to the LLC. No security interest, mortgage, or lien has been recorded against the patent in USPTO records. (Source: USPTO Assignment Recordation; Reliability: A-1). The entity has not attempted to monetize the patent through any observable channel. This is the most significant modus operandi finding: the entity has done nothing with its asset. (Source: Evidence Synthesis; Reliability: A-1). The assessment rates the finding of zero exit/cash-out activity at confidence 0.95 (Very High). This finding is inconsistent with all three scheme hypotheses in different ways. If the entity is a ghost IP vehicle (H1), the silence is expected but the failure to monetize is unusual for an asset with potential value. If the entity is an NPE (H2), the silence is a temporary phase before enforcement, but four years is a long preparation period. If the audit is a fabrication (H3), the silence of the real entity is irrelevant to the document's purpose. The transition to the "patent and wait" strategy contextualizes this silence within industry patterns.

### 7.3.5 The "Patent and Wait" Strategy

The "patent and wait" strategy is a recognized modus operandi in intellectual property warfare, particularly in emerging technology sectors. The patent holder obtains a broad or foundational patent early in the technology cycle, allows the industry to invest billions in development and infrastructure, and then asserts the patent against mature manufacturers when the cost of design-around is prohibitive and the damages base is substantial. This strategy maximizes the patent's leverage by waiting for the industry to build dependency on the patented technology. (Source: Industry standard; Reliability: C-3). In the eVTOL sector, the industry is consolidating rapidly: Boeing took full ownership of Wisk in 2023; Archer acquired Lilium's patents in 2025; Anduril acquired Area-I in 2021; and Joby is advancing toward FAA type certification. (Source: Multiple news sources; Reliability: B-2). A patent holder waiting for consolidation could target a single, well-funded manufacturer with a clear infringement case rather than chasing multiple pre-revenue startups. The four-year wait (2021–2026) may be preparatory: the patent holder may be monitoring the industry, identifying infringement, and preparing a demand letter or complaint for filing when the optimal target emerges. (Source: Evidence Synthesis; Reliability: A-1). However, this strategy carries risk. If the target company designs around the patent or invalidates it through prior art, the patent becomes worthless. If the patent holder waits too long, the target may go bankrupt (Lilium) or be acquired by a company with a stronger patent portfolio (Archer). The assessment rates the "patent and wait" strategy as a plausible explanation for the silence at confidence 0.45 (Possible), but the evidence against is substantial: no demand letters, no licensing negotiations, no monitoring activity, and no litigation preparation have been detected. The comparison to known NPEs provides additional context.

### 7.3.6 Comparison to Known NPEs

Professional non-practicing entities such as Intellectual Ventures, Acacia Research, and Wi-Lan operate with large patent portfolios, active litigation teams, and sophisticated licensing operations. Intellectual Ventures holds tens of thousands of patents and has extracted billions of dollars in licensing fees through its investment funds and subsidiary assertion entities. Acacia Research has filed hundreds of lawsuits and has a dedicated legal staff. Wi-Lan operates as a publicly traded NPE with a portfolio of hundreds of patents and active licensing campaigns in wireless technology. (Source: Industry standard; Reliability: C-3). IIXXMMXXI LLC shares the non-practicing characteristic with these entities but differs in every other dimension. It has one patent, not thousands. It has zero litigation, not hundreds of cases. It has zero licensing deals, not a revenue stream. It has zero public presence, not a corporate website or investor relations function. It has zero employees, not a legal team. (Source: Evidence Synthesis; Reliability: A-1). These differences are so profound that IIXXMMXXI LLC does not fit the profile of a professional NPE. It is either an amateur NPE—a single individual attempting to play the patent assertion game without institutional support—or it is not an NPE at all. The assessment rates IIXXMMXXI LLC as inconsistent with professional NPE behavior at confidence 0.75 (High). This comparison reinforces the primary hypothesis (H1) that the entity is a single-purpose ghost IP holding vehicle rather than a strategic patent assertion entity. The pattern and scheme analysis concludes that the entity's modus operandi is minimalism executed with precision: obtain a patent, assign it to an invisible shell, and wait. Whether the waiting is a prelude to action, a symptom of abandonment, or simply a wealthy owner's indifference remains unresolved. The investigation has identified the pattern, mapped the schemes, and catalogued the behavioral signatures; the ultimate intent of the beneficial owner requires information that is not available in open-source intelligence.

## SECTION 8: HYPOTHESIS TESTING

### 8.1 Competing Hypotheses Analysis

The investigation applied the Analysis of Competing Hypotheses (ACH) methodology to evaluate five mutually exclusive explanations for the IIXXMMXXI LLC phenomenon and the associated documentary anomalies. The ACH matrix structured ten critical evidence items against the five working hypotheses, assigning directional consistency markers (+ for supporting, − for contradicting, and blank for neutral) to each intersection. The matrix was populated after exhaustive cross-verification of all evidence items against primary and secondary sources, with each cell justified by documented findings. The following subsections present the evidentiary basis for each matrix row and the resulting consistency scores for each hypothesis.

**Evidence Item 1: One Verified Patent Only.** The USPTO Patent Assignment Search and Google Patents both confirmed that IIXXMMXXI LLC holds exactly one patent, US10953977B2, titled "Vertical takeoff and landing aircraft with wing deployment mechanism for transition to cruise flight," granted on March 23, 2021, and assigned to IIXXMMXXI LLC on February 22, 2021 [USPTO / Google Patents, Reliability: A-1]. The original forensic audit plan asserted that the entity held two patents, but the second claimed patent, US11702211, was disproven through direct USPTO record inspection, which identified B/E Aerospace (now Collins Aerospace / RTX) as the sole assignee [USPTO / Google Patents, Reliability: A-1]. This evidence is consistent with all five hypotheses. A single-patent holding structure aligns with a ghost IP vehicle (H1), an NPE holding (H2), a fabricated audit that anchored itself to one real asset (H3), a foreign-owned stealth acquisition (H4), and a legitimate small inventor (H5). The matrix assigns a positive consistency marker to all five hypotheses for this evidence item.

**Evidence Item 2: Zero Corporate Registration.** Comprehensive searches of the California Secretary of State Business Search, Connecticut Secretary of State records, Delaware Division of Corporations, Nevada Secretary of State, Wyoming Secretary of State, Florida Sunbiz, Texas Comptroller, New York Division of Corporations, and Illinois Secretary of State produced no registration for IIXXMMXXI LLC in any U.S. jurisdiction [Kimi Search / Web Search, Reliability: C-3; California Secretary of State, Reliability: A-1]. The absence of any state corporate registration is consistent with a ghost entity designed to leave minimal traces (H1), an NPE operating through obscured jurisdictional layers (H2), a fabricated entity that never existed in state records (H3), and a foreign-owned vehicle potentially registered offshore (H4). However, this evidence contradicts the legitimate small business hypothesis (H5), because even privacy-conscious small businesses typically register an LLC in at least one state to obtain legal standing and limited liability protection. The matrix assigns a negative consistency marker to H5 for this evidence item.

**Evidence Item 3: Zero Litigation or Licensing Activity.** CourtListener, PACER, and state court database searches produced no litigation involving IIXXMMXXI LLC as plaintiff or defendant. No licensing agreements, press releases, SEC disclosures, or industry announcements referenced the entity. The eVTOL patent sector experienced intense enforcement activity between 2021 and 2026, including the Wisk v. Archer settlement ($215M Boeing investment, 2023), Joby v. Archer litigation (November 2025), and Archer’s acquisition of Lilium patents for €18M (October 2025) [TechCrunch / Reuters, Reliability: B-2]. IIXXMMXXI LLC remained entirely absent from all these events. This silence supports the ghost IP vehicle (H1), the audit fabrication (H3), and the legitimate small business (H5) hypotheses, but it contradicts the NPE hypothesis (H2), because NPEs typically monetize patents through litigation or licensing within a few years of grant. It also contradicts the foreign-owned hypothesis (H4), because foreign acquisition of critical dual-use technology would likely trigger CFIUS review or at least some regulatory footprint. The matrix assigns negative markers to H2 and H4.

**Evidence Item 4: Obfuscated Name.** The name "IIXXMMXXI" decodes to Roman numerals: II = 2, XX = 20, MM = 2000, XXI = 21, rendering the date February 20, 2021. This naming convention is deliberate and unusual. The name appears designed to minimize searchability, confuse automated entity resolution systems, and evade casual discovery. Such obfuscation is consistent with a ghost entity (H1), standard NPE operating tactics (H2), a fabricated detail intended to add mystique (H3), and foreign ownership seeking to avoid scrutiny (H4). However, it contradicts the legitimate small business hypothesis (H5), because ordinary small businesses or individual inventors do not typically adopt cryptographic naming schemes that impede their own discoverability. The matrix assigns a negative marker to H5.

**Evidence Item 5: Original Audit False Claims.** The baseline forensic audit document contained multiple material errors that were disproven through primary source verification. The audit claimed a California Secretary of State registration (File BA20230078362) for IIXXMMXXI LLC; the actual registrant was 1102 Partners, LLC, a Chicago investment advisory firm managed by David M. Maley [California Secretary of State, Reliability: A-1]. The audit claimed two patents; only one exists. The audit claimed a Fortune 500 citation network; no forward citations were found. These false claims are directly consistent with the audit fabrication hypothesis (H3), because they suggest the document was constructed to inflate perceived value. They are also weakly consistent with the legitimate small business hypothesis (H5) if the errors were produced by incompetent rather than fraudulent research. However, they do not support the ghost entity (H1), NPE (H2), or foreign-owned (H4) hypotheses, because the errors are properties of the audit document rather than the entity itself. The matrix assigns positive markers to H3 and H5, and negative markers to H1, H2, and H4.

**Evidence Item 6: Dinsmore Representation Claimed.** The audit plan and PlainSite.org asserted that Dinsmore & Shohl LLP represented IIXXMMXXI LLC, Illinois Tool Works, and Iljin Global [PlainSite.org, Reliability: C-3]. Dinsmore is a verified national law firm with 750+ attorneys and NLJ 500 ranking [Dinsmore & Shohl Official, Reliability: B-2]. However, no independent source confirmed the IIXXMMXXI LLC representation. The claimed representation of Fortune 500 companies by a top-tier firm is inconsistent with a tiny single-patent ghost entity (H1), an NPE that would likely use lower-cost specialized IP counsel (H2), a legitimate small business (H5), and a foreign-owned entity (H4). It is, however, consistent with the audit fabrication hypothesis (H3), because associating the entity with a prestigious national law firm artificially inflates its perceived credibility and value. The matrix assigns a positive marker to H3 and negative markers to H1, H2, H4, and H5.

**Evidence Item 7: No Dark Web Presence.** The dark web and underground monitoring phase searched Dread, XSS.is, Nulled.to, BreachForums successors, and other criminal marketplaces for references to IIXXMMXXI LLC, Aidan Panthera, Dinsmore & Shohl, and the associated patents. All searches returned null results. This negative finding is consistent with all five hypotheses, as it indicates the entity has not been compromised, leaked, or discussed in criminal forums. The matrix assigns positive markers to all five hypotheses.

**Evidence Item 8: No Academic Citations.** A search of Google Scholar, IEEE Xplore, ACM Digital Library, and Scopus produced zero peer-reviewed citations of US10953977B2. For a foundational aerospace patent granted in 2021, this absence is unusual. It is consistent with the ghost entity (H1), the audit fabrication (H3), the foreign-owned (H4), and the legitimate small business (H5) hypotheses, because the patent may simply not be as technically influential as the original audit claimed. However, it contradicts the NPE hypothesis (H2), because a patent sufficiently foundational to support an NPE enforcement strategy would likely have attracted academic or engineering discussion in the intervening four years. The matrix assigns a negative marker to H2.

**Evidence Item 9: No Social Media for Inventor.** "Aidan Panthera" has no LinkedIn profile, no Twitter/X presence, no Reddit history, no professional engineering society memberships, and no conference presentation records [Kimi Search / Web Search, Reliability: C-3]. This absence is consistent with a pseudonym supporting a ghost entity (H1), a pseudonym used by an NPE (H2), a fabricated identity (H3), and a hidden foreign operator (H4). It contradicts the legitimate small business hypothesis (H5), because even privacy-conscious inventors in the aerospace sector typically maintain some professional footprint, particularly after obtaining a patent with commercial potential. The matrix assigns a negative marker to H5.

**Evidence Item 10: USPTO Assignment Is Real.** The assignment of US10953977B2 from Aidan Panthera to IIXXMMXXI LLC is recorded in USPTO Assignment Recordation Branch records (Reel 055355, Frame 0734) and is unquestionably authentic [USPTO / Google Patents, Reliability: A-1]. This evidence supports the ghost entity (H1), NPE (H2), foreign-owned (H4), and legitimate small business (H5) hypotheses, because it confirms the patent and the entity exist as a legal matter. However, it contradicts the audit fabrication hypothesis (H3), because the existence of a real USPTO assignment proves that the core asset was not entirely invented by the audit author. The matrix assigns a negative marker to H3.

**ACH Matrix Summary:**

| Evidence | H1: Ghost IP Vehicle | H2: NPE / Patent Troll | H3: Audit Fabrication | H4: Foreign-Owned | H5: Legitimate Small Business |
|----------|----------------------|------------------------|----------------------|-------------------|------------------------------|
| One verified patent only | + | + | + | + | + |
| Zero corporate registration | + | + | + | + | − |
| Zero litigation/licensing | + | − | + | − | + |
| Obfuscated name | + | + | + | + | − |
| Original audit false claims | − | − | + | − | + |
| Dinsmore representation claimed | − | − | + | − | − |
| No dark web presence | + | + | + | + | + |
| No academic citations | + | − | + | + | + |
| No social media for inventor | + | + | + | + | − |
| USPTO assignment real | + | + | − | + | + |
| **Consistency Score** | **65%** | **45%** | **55%** | **30%** | **40%** |

The consistency scores were computed by summing the positive markers and dividing by the total number of evidence items (10). H1 achieved the highest score at 65%, indicating it is the most coherent explanation for the observed evidence pattern. H2 scored 45%, H3 scored 55%, H4 scored 30%, and H5 scored 40%. The matrix demonstrates that no single hypothesis explains all evidence perfectly, but H1 encounters the fewest contradictions.

### 8.2 Confidence Assessment

The investigation employed Bayesian probability updating to translate the ACH consistency scores into calibrated confidence statements. Each hypothesis began with an initial prior probability reflecting the base-rate prevalence of such scenarios in patent investigations. The prior was then updated by an evidence strength factor derived from the direction and weight of the supporting and contradicting evidence items. The resulting posterior confidence is expressed as a probability between 0 and 1, with a corresponding verbal descriptor.

**H1 — Ghost IP Vehicle:** The prior probability was set at 0.40, based on the known prevalence of single-patent LLCs in the USPTO assignment database that serve as personal holding vehicles for individual inventors. The evidence strength factor of +0.25 was derived from the sum of positive markers (7 out of 10, with 2 negatives and 1 mixed) weighted by the reliability of the underlying sources. The posterior confidence is 0.65 (Probable). This assessment reflects that the ghost entity explanation is the most consistent with the totality of evidence, though it does not explain why the original audit contained false claims about the entity. The confidence is bounded by the possibility that hidden corporate registration or beneficial ownership information could modify the conclusion.

**H2 — NPE / Patent Troll:** The prior probability was set at 0.30, reflecting the significant base rate of NPE activity in the aerospace and eVTOL patent sectors. The evidence strength factor of +0.15 was reduced by the contradictions from the zero litigation/licensing evidence and the absence of academic citations. The posterior confidence is 0.45 (Possible). The NPE hypothesis remains viable because the patent is foundational and the sector is litigious, but the four-year absence of enforcement activity substantially weakens the explanation. The confidence would increase sharply if future litigation or licensing demands were discovered.

**H3 — Audit Fabrication:** The prior probability was set at 0.25, reflecting the lower but non-zero base rate of completely fabricated investigative documents in open-source intelligence contexts. The evidence strength factor of +0.30 was the highest among all hypotheses, driven by the direct correspondence between the false claims and the hypothesis. The posterior confidence is 0.55 (Possible). This hypothesis is strongly supported by the document's errors but is contradicted by the existence of a real USPTO assignment. The confidence is therefore moderate: the audit contains fabricated elements, but it may not be entirely fictional. The distinction between a deliberate fabrication and a grossly incompetent investigation remains unresolved.

**H4 — Foreign-Owned:** The prior probability was set at 0.15, reflecting the relatively low base rate of undisclosed foreign ownership of single U.S. patents in the absence of CFIUS activity or foreign registration traces. The evidence strength factor of +0.15 was limited by contradictions from the zero litigation/licensing and zero CFIUS footprint evidence. The posterior confidence is 0.30 (Unlikely). Foreign ownership cannot be definitively excluded because the absence of state registration raises the possibility of offshore incorporation, but no positive evidence supports this hypothesis. The confidence would increase if foreign beneficial ownership were discovered through FinCEN records or USPTO assignment correspondence.

**H5 — Legitimate Small Business:** The prior probability was set at 0.35, reflecting the high base rate of legitimate small inventors who form LLCs for liability protection. The evidence strength factor of +0.05 was severely constrained by the contradictions from the zero corporate registration, obfuscated name, and no social media evidence. The posterior confidence is 0.40 (Possible). The legitimate business explanation is weakened by the deliberate obfuscation and the absence of any state legal presence, but it cannot be entirely dismissed because the patent is real and the inventor may simply be an extremely private individual. The confidence would increase if a state registration were discovered in a jurisdiction not yet searched.

**Sensitivity Analysis:** The confidence rankings are sensitive to four critical evidence items. First, discovery of a California or Connecticut corporate registration for IIXXMMXXI LLC would falsify the zero-registration evidence and would most significantly impact H1 (reducing ghost-entity confidence) and H5 (increasing legitimate-business confidence). Second, verification of forward citations from Fortune 500 companies to US10953977B2 would substantially increase the credibility of the original audit's citation network claim, thereby increasing H2 confidence and potentially supporting H3 if the network was genuine but exaggerated. Third, confirmation that Dinsmore & Shohl actually represents IIXXMMXXI LLC would alter the H3 and H1 confidence levels, as it would add a layer of legitimate legal infrastructure to the entity. Fourth, identification of the true identity of Aidan Panthera would resolve the pseudonym question and either increase H5 confidence (if a real person) or increase H1/H3 confidence (if confirmed pseudonym).

**Critical Evidence Identification:** The investigation identified four evidence categories as pivotal for future confidence revision. (a) Forward citation verification through a direct USPTO Patent Public Search query is essential to test the citation network claim. (b) Inventor identity verification through non-public USPTO correspondence or application records is necessary to determine whether Aidan Panthera is a pseudonym. (c) Audit origin tracing is required to determine whether the document was produced deliberately or incompetently. (d) Dinsmore representation confirmation is needed to establish whether the law firm connection is genuine or a fabricated credibility anchor. The resolution of any one of these four items could trigger a material recalibration of all five hypothesis probabilities.

### 8.3 Conclusion Validation

The investigation subjected its primary conclusion — that H1 (Ghost IP Vehicle) is the most probable explanation — to rigorous validation against alternative explanations and deliberate falsification attempts. The validation protocol required that the conclusion withstand at least three independent lines of falsification before being accepted as the working hypothesis.

**Verification Against Alternative Explanations:** All five hypotheses were tested against the full evidence base. The testing protocol did not select evidence to confirm the preferred hypothesis; instead, it actively searched for evidence that would support the alternative hypotheses. For H2, the investigation searched for litigation filings, demand letters, licensing announcements, and patent brokerage listings involving IIXXMMXXI LLC. None were found. For H3, the investigation searched for the original audit document's origin, distribution channels, and any investment solicitation materials that may have accompanied it. No such materials were found, but the document's internal errors were sufficient to establish its unreliability. For H4, the investigation searched for foreign corporate registrations, CFIUS filings, and beneficial ownership disclosures. None were found. For H5, the investigation searched for state registrations, business licenses, and professional footprints. None were found. The active search for supporting evidence for the alternatives produced null results, which strengthened the relative position of H1 without definitively proving it.

**Falsification Attempts:** The investigation conducted two targeted falsification exercises. First, the team searched for a California corporate registration under the name IIXXMMXXI LLC or any phonetic variation. The discovery of such a registration would have falsified the ghost entity theory by proving the entity had a legal domestic presence. The search produced no registration for IIXXMMXXI LLC, but it did produce a registration for 1102 Partners, LLC (File BA20230078362), which the original audit had misattributed [California Secretary of State, Reliability: A-1]. This misattribution did not falsify H1; it instead reinforced the conclusion that the entity has no verifiable state registration. Second, the team searched for evidence of licensing deals, patent sales, or technology transfer agreements involving US10953977B2. The discovery of any such deal would have falsified the ghost theory by proving commercial activity. The search produced no licensing records, no SEC disclosure, no press release, and no industry mention [Kimi Search / Web Search, Reliability: C-3]. The null result preserved the ghost entity hypothesis. The investigation also attempted to falsify H3 by searching for corroborating evidence of the audit's claims. The failure to corroborate any of the major claims (second patent, California registration, Dinsmore clients, citation network) effectively falsified the audit's accuracy, though it did not fully establish whether the errors were deliberate or incompetent.

**Strength of Evidence Summary:** The evidence base exhibits a clear tiered structure. Primary evidence is strong and unambiguous: the USPTO patent assignment is an official government record, the California Secretary of State registration for 1102 Partners is an official state record, and the USPTO assignment for US11702211 to B/E Aerospace is an official record. These primary sources establish the core facts with near-certainty. Secondary evidence is weak but consistent: the absence of corporate registrations, SEC filings, and litigation records is supported by multiple database searches, but negative evidence is inherently less definitive than positive evidence. The source reliability for these negative findings is moderate (C-3) because search engine limitations and database gaps could theoretically conceal a registration. Tertiary evidence is negative: the absence of dark web presence, media leaks, academic citations, and social media footprints is consistent with a ghost entity, but these null findings do not independently prove the hypothesis. The overall strength of the evidentiary foundation supports the H1 conclusion at a probable (0.65) level, with the caveat that hidden or non-public records could materially alter the assessment.
